, which is well-defined because the traces on both sides coincide. By the Dominated Convergence Theorem, this implies

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\int_{M_{\ell}}|(v_{n}(\cdot+\sigma\nu_{\ell})-v_{n}(\cdot-\sigma\nu_{\ell}))\gamma_{\ell}|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}=\int_{M_{\ell}}|(u(\cdot+\sigma\nu_{\ell})-u(\cdot-\sigma\nu_{\ell}))\gamma_{\ell}|\land 1\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\rm d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}.

Since
(u(\cdot+\sigma\nu_{\ell})-u(\cdot-\sigma\nu_{\ell}))\gamma_{\ell}\to[u]\gamma_{\ell}
in
L^{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}}(M_{\ell})
as
\sigma\to 0^{+}
(with
\sigma\in S
), using again the Dominated Convergence Theorem we deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\to 0^{+}\\
\sigma\in S\end{subarray}}\int_{M_{\ell}}|(u(\cdot+\sigma\nu_{\ell})-u(\cdot-\sigma\nu_{\ell}))\gamma_{\ell}|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}=\int_{M_{\ell}}|[u]\gamma_{\ell}|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}.

Therefore, recalling (
4.28
), for every
\ell\in\{1,\dots,\ell_{\varepsilon}\}
there exists a function
\eta_{\ell}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon S\to[0,+\infty)
, with
\eta_{\ell}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\sigma)\to 0
as
\sigma\to 0^{+}
, such that for every
\sigma\in S
we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\int_{M^{\prime}_{\ell}}|[u_{n}^{\sigma,\varepsilon}]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{K_{\ell}}\!\!\!\!|[u]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}+\int_{M_{\ell}\setminus K_{\ell}}\!\!\!|[u]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}+\eta_{\ell}(\sigma)\color[rgb]{0,0,0},

(4.29)

\displaystyle=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{K_{\ell}}\!\!\!\!|[u]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}+\eta_{\ell}(\sigma),\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

where in the equality we have used
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u}\cap(M_{\ell}\setminus K_{\ell}))=0
.

Combining the last inequality with (
4.19
), (
4.2
), and (
4.27
) we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{V}(u_{n}^{\sigma,\varepsilon},O^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{V}(u,O\setminus J_{u}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})+\!\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell_{\varepsilon}}\int_{K_{\ell}}\!\!|[u]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\!+\varepsilon+\!\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell_{\varepsilon}}\big(C_{\ell}\lambda_{\ell}(\sigma)+\mu_{\ell}(\sigma)+C_{\ell}\omega_{\ell}(\sigma)+\eta_{\ell}(\sigma)\big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(4.30)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{V}(u,O)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell_{\varepsilon}}\big(C_{\ell}\lambda_{\ell}(\sigma)+\mu_{\ell}(\sigma)+C_{\ell}\omega_{\ell}(\sigma)+\eta_{\ell}(\sigma)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)+\varepsilon.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Step 5.
(Conclusion of the proof in
O^{\prime}
)
We now fix two sequences of positive numbers
\{\varepsilon_{j}\}_{j}
and
\{\sigma_{j}\}_{j}
converging to
0
such that for every
j\in\mathbb{N}
we have
0<\sigma_{j}<\sigma^{\varepsilon_{j}}

(see (
4.8
)) and

{\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell_{\varepsilon_{j}}}\big(C_{\ell}\lambda_{\ell}(\sigma_{j})+\mu_{\ell}(\sigma_{j})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+2C_{\ell}\omega_{\ell}(\sigma_{j})+\eta_{\ell}(\sigma_{j})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon_{j}}

(see (
4.17
)–(
4.18
), (
4.23
), (
4.25
), and (
4.29
)).
From this inequality and from (
4.18
) and (
4.30
), we obtain for every
j\in\mathbb{N}
that

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\int_{O^{\prime}}|u^{\sigma_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}_{n}-u|\,{\rm d}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon_{j}\quad\text{ and }\quad\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{V}(u^{\sigma_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}_{n},O^{\prime})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}(u,O)+2\varepsilon_{j}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Therefore, there exists
n_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(4.31)

\int_{O^{\prime}}|u^{\sigma_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}_{n}-u|\,{\rm d}x\leq\varepsilon_{j}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{V}(u^{\sigma_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}_{n},O^{\prime})\leq\mathcal{V}(u,O)+2\varepsilon_{j}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

for every
n\geq n_{j}
. It is not restrictive to assume that
n_{j}<n_{j+1}
for every
j\in\mathbb{N}
. We set
u_{n}\coloneq u^{\sigma_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}_{n_{j}}
for
n_{j}\leq n<n_{j+1}
and conclude observing that by construction
u_{n}\in{\rm SBV}(O^{\prime})\cap L^{\infty}(O^{\prime})
and that from (
4.31
) it follows that
\{u_{n}\}_{n}
converges strongly in
L^{1}(O^{\prime})
as
n\to+\infty
and that (
4.1
) holds true.

Step 6.
(
O
with Lipschitz boundary.) Assume now that
O
has Lipschitz boundary and let us prove that there exists
\{u_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(O)\cap L^{\infty}(O)
converging to
u
in
L^{1}(O)
such that (
4.2
) holds. To this end, we let
\{\varepsilon_{j}\}_{j}
be a sequence of positive numbers converging to
0
as
j\to+\infty
and let
\{O_{j}\}_{j}
be an increasing sequence of open sets with Lipschitz boundary, compactly contained in
O
, such that
O_{j}\nearrow O
as
j\to+\infty
and

(4.32)

|Du|\big(O\setminus\overline{O_{j}}\big)\leq\varepsilon_{j}\quad\text{for all $j\in\mathbb{N}$}.

As
J_{u}
is
\sigma
-finite with respect to
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
, we may choose the sequence
\{O_{j}\}_{j}
in such a way that
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial O_{j}\cap J_{u})=0
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
.
We also recall that (see the comment below (
4.8
))

(4.33)

{\rm dist}(M^{\varepsilon_{j}}(\sigma^{\varepsilon_{j}}),\partial O_{j})>0

for every
j\in\mathbb{N}
, where
M^{\varepsilon_{j}}(\sigma)
is given by (
4.7
).

For every
0<\sigma<\sigma^{\varepsilon_{j}}
, we consider the functions
u^{\sigma,j}_{n}\coloneq u^{\sigma,\varepsilon_{j}}_{n}
constructed in Step 2 and given by (
4.11
).
Note that (
4.33
) implies
that in a neighbourhood of
\partial O_{j}
, independent of
n
, the function
u_{n}^{\sigma,j}
is given by
v_{n}\coloneq u*\rho_{n}
, where
\rho_{n}
is a smooth convolution kernel. Therefore, letting
(u^{\sigma,j}_{n})_{\partial O_{j}^{-}}
(resp.
u_{\partial O^{-}_{j}}
) be the trace from the interior of
u^{\sigma,j}_{n}
(resp.
u
) on
\partial O_{j}
, recalling that
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u}\cap\partial O_{j})=0
, by the same properties of the convolutions of
{\rm BV}
-functions invoked below (
4.28
) we get

(4.34)

\{(u^{\sigma,{j}}_{n})_{\partial O_{j}^{-}}\}_{n}\text{ converges to $u_{\partial O_{j}^{-}}$ strongly in $L^{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}}(\partial O_{j})$ as $n\to+\infty$.}

Next, we apply Anzellotti-Giaquinta’s Theorem
[
6
, Theorem 1]
on
O\setminus\overline{O_{j}}
and obtain a sequence
\{w^{j}_{n}\}_{n}\subset C^{\infty}(O\setminus\overline{O_{j}})\cap L^{\infty}(O\setminus\overline{O_{j}})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(recall that we assumed
u\in L^{\infty}(O)
) such that

(4.35)

\{w^{j}_{n}\}_{n}
converges to
u
strongly in
L^{1}(O\setminus\overline{O_{j}})
as
n\to+\infty

and
|Dw^{j}_{n}|(O\setminus\overline{O_{j}})
converges to
|Du|(O\setminus\overline{O_{j}})
as
n\to+\infty
. Thus, by (
4.32
)

(4.36)

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{V}(w_{n}^{j},O\setminus\overline{O_{j}})\leq\limsup_{n\to+\infty}|Dw^{j}_{n}|(O\setminus\overline{O_{j}})\leq\varepsilon_{j}\quad\text{for all $j\in\mathbb{N}$}.

Moreover, letting
(w^{j}_{n})_{\partial O_{j}^{+}}
(resp.
u_{\partial O^{+}_{j}}
) be the trace from the interior of
w^{j}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(resp.
u
) on
\partial O_{j}^{+}
, by the continuity of the trace operator under strict convergence, we obtain

(4.37)

\{(w^{j}_{n})_{\partial O_{j}^{+}}\}_{n}\text{ converges to $u_{\partial O_{j}^{+}}$ strongly in $L^{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}}(\partial O_{j})$ as $n\to+\infty$.}

We let
\{z^{\sigma,j}_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
be the functions defined by

z_{n}^{\sigma,j}\coloneq\begin{cases}u^{\sigma,j}_{n}&\text{on $O_{j}$},\\
w^{j}_{n}&\text{on $O\setminus\overline{O_{j}}$},\end{cases}

and observe that, by (
4.34
) and (
4.37
), we have

(4.38)

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\int_{J_{z_{n}^{\sigma,j}}\cap\partial O_{j}}|[z^{\sigma,j}_{n}]|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}=\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\int_{\partial O_{j}}|(w^{j}_{n})_{\partial O_{j}^{+}}-(u^{\sigma,{j}}_{n})_{\partial O_{j}^{-}}|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}=0

for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
, where in the last equality we have used that
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u}\cap\partial O_{j})=0
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
. Finally, combining (
4.18
), (
4.30
), (
4.35
), (
4.36
), and (
4.38
) the same diagonal argument employed in Step 5 allows to define a sequence
\{u_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(O)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap L^{\infty}(O)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
converging to
u
strongly in
L^{1}(O)
such that

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{V}(u_{n},O)\leq\mathcal{V}(u,O).

In view of the lower semicontinuity of
\mathcal{V}
(see Remark
2.1
), this inequality implies (
4.2
), concluding the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.3
.
We first give the proof in the scalar case
k=1
and for
u\in{\rm BV}(O)\cap L^{\infty}(O)
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\mathcal{V}(u,O\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus J^{1}_{u})>0
, as otherwise the function
u
is already piecewise constant, see
[
5
, Theorem 4.23]
. Moreover, we can assume that
u\geq 0
.

Step 1.
(Scalar case
k=1
)
We let
M\coloneq\lceil\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(O)}\rceil<+\infty
, where for
a\in\mathbb{R}
the symbol
\lceil a\rceil
denotes the smallest integer greater than or equal to
a
. For
t\in(-\infty,+\infty)
we define
E_{t}\coloneq\{x\in O:u(x)>t\}
. By the coarea formula we have

(4.39)

\int_{0}^{M}|D\chi_{{E_{t}}}|(O\setminus J^{1}_{u})\,{\rm d}t=\int_{0}^{M}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(\partial^{*}E_{t}\cap(O\setminus J^{1}_{u})\big)\,{\rm d}t=\mathcal{V}(u,O\setminus J^{1}_{u}).

Thanks to the well-known approximation properties of Lebesgue integrals by means of Riemann sums (see, for instance,
[
31
, Lemma 3.1]
) we can find a sequence
\{\delta_{n}\}_{n}
converging to
0
as
n\to+\infty
and
s\in[0,1]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that, setting
t_{n}^{j}\coloneq s+\frac{j}{n}
for
j\in\mathcal{J}^{n}\coloneq\{j\in\mathbb{Z}:t_{n}^{j}\in(0,M)\}
, we have that
E_{t_{n}^{j}}
is of finite perimeter in
O
and

(4.40)

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\frac{1}{n}|D\chi_{E_{t_{n}^{j}}}|(O\setminus J^{1}_{u})-\int_{0}^{M}|D\chi_{{E_{t}}}|(O\setminus J^{1}_{u})\,{\rm d}t\Big|\leq\delta_{n}

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We then define

(4.41)

u_{n}\coloneq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\chi_{E_{t_{n}^{j\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}}\in{\rm BV}(O)\cap L^{\infty}(O)

and observe that

|u_{n}(x)-u(x)|\leq\frac{1}{n}\quad\text{ for $\mathcal{L}^{d}$-a.e.\ }x\in O\text{ and for all $n\in\mathbb{N}$},

which implies strong
L^{1}(O)
-convergence of

\{u_{n}\}_{n}
to
u
as
n\to+\infty
.

We want to estimate
\mathcal{V}(u_{n},O)
by means of
\mathcal{V}(u,O)
.
We write

(4.42)

\mathcal{V}(u_{n},O)=\mathcal{V}(u_{n},J^{1}_{u})+\mathcal{V}(u_{n},O\setminus J^{1}_{u}).

The first term can be bounded from above as

(4.43)

\mathcal{V}(u_{n},J^{1}_{u})\leq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{1}_{u})=\mathcal{V}(u,J^{1}_{u}).

As for the second term, we observe that by (
4.41
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}(u_{n},O\setminus J^{1}_{u})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(J_{u_{n}}\setminus J^{1}_{u})\cap O}|[u_{n}]|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}=|Du_{n}|(O\setminus J^{1}_{u})\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\frac{1}{n}|D\chi_{E_{t_{n}^{j}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}|(O\setminus J^{1}_{u}),

which by (
4.40
) and (
4.39
) implies

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{V}(u_{n},O\setminus J^{1}_{u})\leq\mathcal{V}(u,O\setminus J^{1}_{u}).

Combining the previous inequality with (
4.42
) and (
4.43
), we obtain

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{V}(u_{n},O)\leq\mathcal{V}(u,O\setminus J^{1}_{u})+\mathcal{V}(u,J_{u}^{1})=\mathcal{V}(u,O).

Moreover, as
\{u_{n}\}_{n}
converges to
u
strongly in
L^{1}(O)
, the lower semicontinuity of
\mathcal{V}(\cdot,O)
with respect to the strong
L^{1}
-convergence (see Remark
2.1
) implies
\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{V}(u_{n},O)=\mathcal{V}(u,O)
.
This concludes the proof in the case
u\in{\rm BV}(O)\cap L^{\infty}(O)
.

If
u\in{\rm BV}(O)
, for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
we consider the truncation
u^{(m)}\coloneq(u\lor-m)\land m
and use the previous step to obtain a sequence
\{u_{m,n}\}_{n}
of piecewise constant functions converging to
u^{(m)}
strongly in
L^{1}(O)
as
n\to+\infty
and such that

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{V}(u_{m,n},O)=\mathcal{V}(u^{(m)},O).

Since by
[
33
, Remark 2.7]

\lim_{m\to+\infty}\mathcal{V}(u^{(m)},O)=\mathcal{V}(u,O)\quad\text{ and }\quad u^{(m)}\to u\text{ strongly in $L^{1}(O)$ as $m\to+\infty$},

a diagonal argument allows us to conclude.

Step 2.
(Vector-valued case
k>1
) In the vector-valued case
k\geq 2
, we apply the result we have just proved in the scalar case to each component.
For each
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
this yields a finite collection of
sets of finite perimeter
\{E^{j}_{n,i}\}_{j}
, and numbers
\{t^{j}_{n,i}\}_{j}
such that
u_{n,i}\coloneq\sum_{j}t^{j}_{n,i}\chi E^{j}_{n,i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in{\rm BV}(O)\cap L^{\infty}(O)
converges to
u_{i}
strongly in
L^{1}(O)
as
n\to+\infty
and

(4.44)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{V}(u_{n,i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},O)=\mathcal{V}(u_{i},O).

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we let
u_{n}\in{\rm BV}(O)\cap L^{\infty}(O)
be the function whose
i
-th component is given by
u_{n,i}
, which is clearly piecewise constant. By (
4.44
) we then have

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{V}(u_{n},O)=\mathcal{V}(u,O).

As
\{u_{n,i\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\}_{n}
converges to
u_{i}
strongly in
L^{1}(O)
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
, the proof is concluded.∎

5.
Integral representation of bulk and surface parts: Proof of Theorem
3.7

This section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
3.7
, that is, the integral representation of the bulk and surface parts of functionals in
\mathfrak{F}^{p}_{\star}
. The arguments we employ here are those of the Global Method of Bouchitté, Fonseca, & Mascarenhas
[
18
]
(see also
[
17
]
), later adapted to the setting of structured deformations in several different works, see for instance
[
40
,
42
,
16
,
15
]
. Due to the different growth conditions and underlying function spaces considered here, we cannot always rely on the theory developed in the above mentioned works. Therefore, at several points, significant modifications are necessary and, in particular, Poincaré inequalities and blow-up limits tailor-made for our functional setting are required. These arguments are presented in the appendices, see Lemmas
B.5
,
D.1
, and
D.2
, and Corollary
C.7
.

5.1.
The global method

For notational convenience, in this section we depart from the notation introduced in Section
3
, and will use
(u,U)
,
(v,V)
, and
(w,W)
to denote structured deformations in place of the usual
(g,G)
. We keep
\mathcal{F}\colon{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})\times\mathcal{B}(\Omega)\to[0,+\infty)
fixed throughout the section, and we specify at each step the properties we assume on
\mathcal{F}
.
We begin by proving that
\mathcal{F}
satisfies a suitable version of the Fundamental Estimate, which will be used to alter boundary conditions for the displacement fields
g
in an energetically convenient way.

Lemma 5.1

(Fundamental Estimate)
.

Let
\eta>0
and let
O,O^{\prime},O^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
be with Lipschitz boundary and with
O^{\prime}\subset\subset O
. Assume that
\mathcal{F}
satisfies conditions
(H1), (H2),
and
(H4)
. Then, for every
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
v\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(O^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{R}^{k})
, with
u\in L^{1}(O\setminus O^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
v\in L^{1}((O\setminus O^{\prime})\cap O^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{R}^{k})
, there exists a function
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};[0,1])
such that, setting
w\coloneq\varphi u+(1-\varphi)v
, we have
w\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(O^{\prime}\cup O^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{R}^{k})
,

(5.1)

w=u\quad\text{ $\mathcal{L}^{d}$-a.e.\ in }O^{\prime}\quad\text{ and }\quad w=v\quad\text{ $\mathcal{L}^{d}$-a.e.\ in }O^{\prime\prime}\setminus O,

and

(5.2)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\mathcal{F}(w,W,O^{\prime}\cup O^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\leq(1+\eta)\big(\mathcal{F}(u,W,O)+\mathcal{F}(v,W,O^{\prime\prime})\big)+M\|u-v\|_{L^{1}((O\setminus O^{\prime})\cap O^{\prime\prime})}+\eta\mathcal{L}^{d}(O^{\prime}\cup O^{\prime\prime})

for every
W\in L^{p}(O\cup O^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
, where
M=M(O,O^{\prime},O^{\prime\prime},d,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha,\beta,\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}k)
is a positive constant independent of
u
and
v
. Moreover, if
\rho>0
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are such that
O_{\rho,x},O^{\prime}_{\rho,x},\,O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x}\subset\Omega

(
see (
2.1
)
)
, then

M(O_{\rho,x},O^{\prime}_{\rho,x},\,O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x},d,\alpha,\beta,\eta\color[rgb]{0,0,0},k)=\frac{1}{\rho}M(O,O^{\prime},O^{\prime\prime},d,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha,\beta,\eta,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}k).

Proof.

Let us fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and consider open subsets
O_{1},\dots,O_{N+1}
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with

O^{\prime}\subset\subset O_{1}\subset\subset\dots\subset\subset O_{N+1}\subset\subset O.

For
i\in\{1,\dots,N\}
we consider functions
\varphi^{i}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(O_{i+1};[0,1])
with
\varphi^{i}=1
in a neighbourhood of
\overline{O_{i}}
and with
|\nabla\varphi^{i}|\leq 2N/\delta
, where
\delta\coloneq{\rm dist}(O^{\prime},\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus O)>0
. Thanks to Lemma
B.5
, for every
i\in\{1,\dots,N\}
the function
w^{i}\coloneq\varphi^{i}u+(1-\varphi^{i})v
belongs to
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(O^{\prime}\cup O^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{R}^{k})
, where we have extended
u
and
v
arbitrarily outside of
O
and
O^{\prime\prime}
, respectively.

We set
S_{i}\coloneq O^{\prime\prime}\cap(O_{i+1}\setminus\overline{O_{i}})
and fix
i\in\{1,\dots,N\}
. By the measure property
(

H

1)
and locality
(

H

2)
we see that

(5.3)

\mathcal{F}(w^{i},W,O^{\prime}\cup O^{\prime\prime})\leq\mathcal{F}(u,W,O)+\mathcal{F}(v,W,O^{\prime\prime})+\mathcal{F}(w^{i},W,S_{i})

for every
W\in L^{p}(O\cup O^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
.
To estimate the term
\mathcal{F}(w^{i},W,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}S_{i}),
we exploit the upper bounds in
(

H

4)
to get

(5.4)

\mathcal{F}(w^{i},W,S_{i})\leq\beta\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Big(\int_{S_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nabla w^{i}_{j}|\,{\rm d}x+|D^{c}w^{i}_{j}|(S_{i})+\int_{J_{w^{i}_{j}}\cap S_{i}}|[w^{i}_{j}]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\Big)+\beta\int_{S_{i}}(|W|^{p}+1)\,{\rm d}x.

By Lemma
B.5
we have

\displaystyle\nabla w^{i}=\nabla\varphi^{i}\otimes(u-v)+\varphi^{i}\nabla u+(1-\varphi^{i})\nabla v\quad\text{ $\mathcal{L}^{d}$-a.e. in $S_{i}$},

\displaystyle D^{c}w^{i}=\varphi^{i}D^{c}u+(1-\varphi^{i})D^{c}v\quad\text{ as Borel measures on $S_{i}$},

\displaystyle[w^{i}]=\varphi^{i}[u]+(1-\varphi^{i})[v]\quad\text{ $\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-a.e.\ on $S_{i}\cap J_{w^{i}}$},

so that

\displaystyle\int_{S_{i}}|\nabla w^{i}_{j}|\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{S_{i}}(|\nabla u_{j}|+|\nabla v_{j}|)\,{\rm d}x+\|\nabla\varphi^{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\int_{S_{i}}|u-v|\,{\rm d}x,

\displaystyle|D^{c}w^{i}_{j}|(S_{i})

\displaystyle\leq|D^{c}u_{j}|(S_{i})+|D^{c}v_{j}|(S_{i}),

\displaystyle\int_{S_{i}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{w^{i}_{j}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}|[w^{i}_{j}]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{S_{i}\cap J_{u_{j}}}|[u_{j}]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}+\int_{S_{i}\cap J_{v_{j}}}|[v_{j}]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

for all
j\in\{1,\dots,k\}
.
Combining the previous inequalities with (
5.4
), recalling that
|\nabla\varphi^{i}|\leq 2N/\delta
, and using the lower bounds in
(

H

4)
, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(w^{i},W,S_{i})

\displaystyle\leq\beta\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Big(\int_{S_{i}}(|\nabla u_{j}|+|\nabla v_{j}|)\,{\rm d}x+\frac{2N}{\delta}\int_{S_{i}}|u-v|\,{\rm d}x+|D^{c}u_{j}|(S_{i})+|D^{c}v_{j}|(S_{i})

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{S_{i}\cap J_{u_{j}}}|[u_{j}]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}+\int_{S_{i}\cap J_{v_{j}}}|[v_{j}]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\Big)+\beta\int_{S_{i}}|W|^{p}\,{\rm d}x+\beta\mathcal{L}^{d}(S_{i})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\Big(\mathcal{F}(u,W,S_{i})+\mathcal{F}(v,W,S_{i})\Big)+\frac{2\beta Nk}{\delta}\int_{S_{i}}|u-v|\,{\rm d}x+\beta\mathcal{L}^{d}(S_{i}).

We set
S\coloneq(O\setminus O^{\prime})\cap O^{\prime\prime}
, sum the previous inequality over
i\in\{1,\dots,N\}
and obtain

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{F}(w^{i},W,S_{i})\leq\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\Big(\mathcal{F}(u,W,S)+\mathcal{F}(v,W,S)\Big)+\frac{2\beta Nk}{\delta}\int_{S}|u-v|\,{\rm d}x+\beta\mathcal{L}^{d}(S).

Therefore, there exists
i^{*}\in\{1,\dots,N\}
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(w^{i^{*}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W,S_{i^{*}})\leq\frac{\beta}{\alpha N}\Big(\mathcal{F}(u,W,S)+\mathcal{F}(v,W,S)\Big)+\frac{2\beta k}{\delta}\int_{S}|u-v|\,{\rm d}x+\frac{\beta}{N}\mathcal{L}^{d}(S).

Plugging this inequality back in (
5.3
), we deduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(w^{i^{*}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W,O^{\prime}\cup O^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{F}(u,W,O)+\mathcal{F}(v,W,O^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\beta}{\alpha N}\Big(\mathcal{F}(u,W,S)+\mathcal{F}(v,W,S)\Big)+\frac{2\beta k}{\delta}\int_{S}|u-v|\,{\rm d}x+\frac{\beta}{N}\mathcal{L}^{d}(S).

We now let
N\in\mathbb{N}
be so large that
\max\{\beta/(\alpha N),\beta/N\}\leq\eta
and set
M\coloneq 2\beta k/\delta
, so that the previous inequality yields (
5.2
). Note that by construction
w^{i^{*}}
satisfies (
5.1
).

Finally, the remark concerning the behaviour of the constant
M
under rescaling can be proved by using as cut-off functions
\hat{\varphi}^{i}(y)\coloneq\varphi^{i}(x+\frac{1}{\rho}(y-x))
for all
y\in(O_{i+1})_{\rho,x}
.
∎

We now prove a lemma that allows to compare the value of
\mathcal{F}
on two nested open sets. This observation will be used to modify, in an energetically convenient way, the matrix-valued fields
U
so as to impose suitable integral constraints.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
(u,U)\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and let
O_{1},O_{2}\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
with
O_{1}\subset\subset O_{2}
. Assume that
\mathcal{F}
satisfies
(

H

1)
and
(

H

4)
. Then

(5.5)

\mathcal{F}(u,U,O_{2})\leq\mathcal{F}(u,U,O_{1})+\beta\Big(\mathcal{V}(u,O_{2}\setminus O_{1})+\|U\|_{L^{p}(O_{2}\setminus O_{1})}^{p}+\mathcal{L}^{d}(O_{2}\setminus O_{1})\Big).

Proof.

It is enough to use properties
(

H

1)
and
(

H

4)
. ∎

With the previous lemma at our disposal, we can prove a result that allows to compare the value of the minimisation problems
\mathfrak{m}
defined in (
3.3
) on cubes of different size.

Lemma 5.3
.

Assume that
\mathcal{F}
satisfies properties
(H1)
and
(H4)
. Then for every
(u,U)\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
the following two conditions hold:

(a)

if
p>1
and
Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\subset\Omega
, we have

\limsup_{\theta\to 0^{+}}\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q_{\nu}(x,(1-\theta)\rho))\leq\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho));

(b)

if
p=1
and
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
with Lipschitz boundary, we have

\limsup_{\theta\to 0^{+}}\mathfrak{m}(u,U,O_{\theta})\leq\mathfrak{m}(u,U,O),

where
O_{\theta}=\{x\in O\colon\,{\rm dist}(x,\partial O)>\theta\}
.

Proof.

It is enough to repeat the arguments of
[
42
, Lemma 3.2]
, replacing their Remark 3.1 by our Lemma
5.2
, the total variation of the symmetric distributional gradients with the reference functional
\mathcal{V}
, and the use of
[
12
, Proposition 2.5]
by our Lemma
A.7
.∎

To proceed with the Global Method, it is convenient to introduce the collection of cubes

\mathcal{Q}(\Omega)\coloneq\big\{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\subset\Omega\colon\,x\in\Omega,\,\,\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\text{ and }\rho>0\big\}.

Given
(u,U)\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, we introduce the measure

(5.6)

\mu\coloneq\mathcal{L}^{d}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\Omega+|D^{c}u|+\mathcal{V}(u,\cdot)\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}J_{u},

and consider the minimisation problem
\mathfrak{m}^{\delta}(u,U,O)
defined for
\delta>0
and
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\delta}(u,U,O)\coloneq\inf

\displaystyle\Big\{\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{\infty}\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q_{i})\colon\,Q_{i}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{Q}(\Omega),\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\,Q_{i}\subset O,\,\,

\displaystyle\text{ with }Q_{i}\cap Q_{j}=\mathchar 31\relax\,\,\,{\rm if}\,i\neq j,\,\,{\rm diam}(Q_{i})<\delta,\text{ and }\ \mu\Big(O\setminus\bigcup\nolimits_{i=1}^{\infty}Q_{i}\Big)=0\Big\}.

Clearly, the function
\delta\mapsto\mathfrak{m}^{\delta}(u,U,O)
is non-increasing, so that the quantity

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(u,U,O)\coloneq\sup_{\delta>0}\mathfrak{m}^{\delta}(u,U,O)=\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\mathfrak{m}^{\delta}(u,U,O)

is well-defined.

The first step of the Global Method consists in proving that
\mathcal{F}(u,U,O)=\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(u,U,O)
. This is accomplished in the following lemma, whose proof is along the lines of
[
28
, Lemma 4.4]
and
[
40
, Lemma 4.2]
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Assume that
\mathcal{F}\in\mathfrak{F}^{p}_{\star}
. Then,

\mathcal{F}(u,U,O)=\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(u,U,O)

for all
(u,U)\in{\rm SD}_{\star}^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and all

O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
.

Proof.

Let us fix
(u,U)\in{\rm SD}_{\star}^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
. Since for each cube
Q\in\mathcal{Q}(\Omega)
with
Q\subset O
, we have
\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q)\leq\mathcal{F}(u,U,Q)
by definition, the measure property
(

H

1)
then implies that
\mathfrak{m}^{\delta}(u,U,O)\leq\mathcal{F}(u,U,O)
for all
\delta>0
, and, as a consequence,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(u,U,O)\leq\mathcal{F}(u,U,O)
.

We now prove the converse inequality. To this end, let us fix
\delta>0
and a family of cubes
\{Q^{\delta}_{i}\}_{i}\subset\mathcal{Q}(\Omega)
as in the definition of
\mathfrak{m}^{\delta}(u,U,O)
such that

(5.7)

\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q^{\delta}_{i})\leq\mathfrak{m}^{\delta}(u,U,O)+\delta.

By definition of
\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q^{\delta}_{i})
, for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
we find
(v_{i}^{\delta},V^{\delta}_{i})\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(Q^{\delta}_{i};\mathbb{R}^{k})
such that
v_{i}^{\delta}=u
near
\partial Q_{i}^{\delta}
,
\int_{Q^{\delta}_{i}}(V^{\delta}_{i}-U)\,{\rm d}x=0
, and

(5.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(v_{i}^{\delta},V^{\delta}_{i},Q_{i}^{\delta})\leq\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q_{i}^{\delta})+\delta\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q_{i}^{\delta}).

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
we then define

(5.9)

\displaystyle v^{\delta,n}\coloneq\sum_{i=1}^{n}v^{\delta}_{i}\chi_{Q^{\delta}_{i}}+u\chi_{N^{\delta,n}}\quad\text{ and }\quad v^{\delta}\coloneq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}v^{\delta}_{i}\chi_{Q^{\delta}_{i}}+u\chi_{N^{\delta}},

\displaystyle V^{\delta,n}\coloneq\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{\delta}_{i}\chi_{Q^{\delta}_{i}}+U\chi_{N^{\delta,n}}\quad\text{ and }\quad V^{\delta}\coloneq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}V^{\delta}_{i}\chi_{Q^{\delta}_{i}}+U\chi_{N^{\delta}},

where we have set
N^{\delta,n}\coloneq\Omega\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}Q^{\delta}_{i}
and
N^{\delta}\coloneq\Omega\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}Q^{\delta}_{i}
. Since
v^{\delta}_{i}=u
near
\partial Q^{\delta}_{i}
, it follows that
(v^{\delta,n},V^{\delta,n})\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
by Lemma
A.7
. Moreover, by (
5.7
), (
5.8
), the measure property
(

H

1)
, the locality property
(

H

2)
, and the upper and lower bounds in
(

H

4)
we have

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Big(\mathcal{V}(v^{\delta,n},\Omega)+\|V^{\delta,n}\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Big(\mathcal{F}(u,U,N^{\delta,n})+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{F}(v^{\delta,n}_{i},V^{\delta,n}_{i},Q^{\delta}_{i})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Big(\mathcal{F}(u,U,\Omega)+\mathfrak{m}^{\delta}(u,U,O)+\delta\big(1+\mathcal{L}^{d}(O)\big)\Big)

(5.10)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\beta}{\alpha}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Big(\mathcal{V}(u,\Omega)+\|U\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+2+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega)\Big)=:S<+\infty.

As
v^{\delta,n}\to v^{\delta}
and
V^{\delta,n}\to V^{\delta}
pointwise
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e. in
\Omega
as
n\to+\infty
, by
[
39
, Theorem 4.7]
and (
5.10
) we deduce that
\{(v^{\delta,n},V^{\delta,n})\}_{n}
converges to
(v^{\delta},V^{\delta})
in
{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
as
n\to+\infty
if
p>1
. To obtain the same property for
p=1
, it is enough to observe that computations similar to those that led to (
5.10
) give

\int_{O}|V^{\delta,n}-V^{\delta}|\,{\rm d}x=\sum_{i=n+1}^{\infty}\int_{Q_{i}^{\delta}}|V^{\delta}_{i}|\,{\rm d}x+\int_{N^{\delta}}|U|\,{\rm d}x\leq\frac{2\beta}{\alpha}\Big(\mathcal{V}(u,N^{\delta,n+1})+\|U\|_{L^{1}(N^{\delta,n+1})}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{L}^{d}(N^{\delta,n+1})\Big)

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, where we have also used that
(u,U)
is a competitor for the minimisation problems
\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q^{\delta}_{i})
. As
\lim_{n\to\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu(N^{\delta,n})=\mu(N^{\delta})=0
, this implies that
\{V^{\delta,n}\}_{n}
converges to
V^{\delta}
strongly in
L^{1}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
.

Therefore, by locality
(

H

2)
, the lower semicontinuity property
(

H

3)
, (
5.7
), and (
5.8
)
it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(v^{\delta},V^{\delta},O)

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}(v^{\delta,n},V^{\delta,n},O)=\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{F}(v_{i}^{\delta},V^{\delta}_{i},Q_{i}^{\delta})+\mathcal{F}(u,U,N^{\delta,n}\cap O)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{F}(v_{i}^{\delta},V^{\delta}_{i},Q_{i}^{\delta})+\mathcal{F}(u,U,N^{\delta}\cap O)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\big(\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q_{i}^{\delta})+\delta\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q_{i}^{\delta})\big)

(5.11)

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{m}^{\delta}(u,U,O)+\delta(1+\mathcal{L}^{d}(O)),

where in the second step we used
\mu(\partial Q^{\delta}_{i})=0
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
and in the fourth step we have used that
\mu(N^{\delta}\cap O)=\mathcal{F}(u,U,N^{\delta}\cap O)=0
, which are a simple consequence of the definition of
\{Q^{\delta}_{i}\}_{i}
and the upper bound in
(

H

4)
.

To conclude the proof, it is now enough to show that
\{(v^{\delta},V^{\delta})\}_{\delta}
converges to
(u,U)
in
{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
as
\delta\to 0^{+}
, since passing to the liminf for
\delta\to 0^{+}
in (
5.11
) and using the lower semicontinuity
(

H

3)
we would then conclude
\mathcal{F}(u,U,O)\leq\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(u,U,O)
.

We observe that by Remark
2.1
and (
5.10
) there exists a subsequence, still denoted by
\delta
for convenience, such that

(5.12)

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}(u-v^{\delta},O)\leq 2S,

which, up to passing to a further subsequence, implies

(5.13)

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sup_{\delta>0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{1}_{u-v^{\delta}}\cap O)\leq 3S.

We now show that
\{v^{\delta}\}_{\delta}
converges to
u
in
L^{0}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
as
\delta\to 0^{+}
. To this end, we apply Corollary
C.7
and Remark
C.8
on each cube
Q^{\delta}_{i}
to the function
u-v_{i}^{\delta}
, which equals zero near
\partial Q^{\delta}_{i}
. We get Borel sets
\omega^{\delta}_{i}\subset Q^{\delta}_{i}
such that

(5.14)

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega^{\delta}_{i})\big)^{(d-1)/d}\leq C_{\rm Poin}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(J^{1}_{u-v^{\delta}}\cap Q^{\delta}_{i}\big),

(5.15)

\displaystyle\|u-v^{\delta}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|_{L^{1}(Q^{\delta}_{i}\setminus\omega^{\delta}_{i})}\leq C_{\rm Poin}{\delta}\mathcal{V}(u-v^{\delta},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{\delta}_{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus J^{1}_{u-v^{\delta}})

for a constant
C_{\rm Poin}>0
, independent of
i
and of
\delta
. In light of (
5.9
) and the fact that
\mathcal{L}^{d}(N^{\delta})=0
, we can estimate

(5.16)

\displaystyle\int_{O}|u-v^{\delta}|\land 1\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{Q^{\delta}_{i}}|u-v_{i}^{\delta}|\land 1\,{\rm d}x\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\Big(\|u-v^{\delta}_{i}\|_{L^{1}(Q^{\delta}_{i}\setminus\omega^{\delta}_{i})}+\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega^{\delta}_{i})\Big).

Recalling that the sets
Q^{\delta}_{i}
are pairwise disjoint, we may use (
5.15
) and (
5.12
) to get

(5.17)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\|u-v^{\delta}_{i}\|_{L^{1}(Q^{\delta}_{i}\setminus\omega^{\delta}_{i})}\leq C\delta

for a constant
C
independent of
\delta
.
Finally, since
\omega^{\delta}_{i}\subset Q^{i}_{\delta}
and
{\rm diam}(Q^{\delta}_{i})\leq\delta
, we may use (
5.13
), (
5.14
), and Hölder’s inequality to obtain

(5.18)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega^{\delta}_{i})\leq\delta\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\big(\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega^{\delta}_{i})\big)^{(d-1)/d}\leq C_{\rm Poin}\delta\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(J^{1}_{u-v^{\delta}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap O\big)\leq C\delta.

Putting together (
5.16
)–(
5.18
), we get

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{O}|u-v^{\delta}|\land 1\,{\rm d}x=0.

Since the equality above is equivalent to the
L^{0}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
-convergence of
\{v^{\delta}\}_{\delta}
to
u
and the limit function
u
does not depend on the chosen subsequence of
\delta
, by Urysohn’s principle we get that the entire sequence
\{v^{\delta}\}_{\delta}
converges to
u
in
L^{0}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
as
\delta\to 0^{+}
.

To conclude, we are left with showing that
\{V^{\delta}\}_{\delta}
converges to
U
weakly in
L^{p}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
if
p>1
and weakly
∗
in
\mathcal{M}_{b}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
if
p=1
. To prove this, exploiting
\int_{Q^{\delta}_{i}}(V^{\delta}_{i}-U)\,{\rm d}x=0
, it is enough to use
[
40
, Lemma 3.3]
(note that the arguments work also for
\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
in place of
\mathbb{R}^{d\times N\times N}
), which implies that
\{V^{\delta}\}_{\delta}
converges to
U
weakly
∗
in
\mathcal{M}_{b}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
. This gives the desired convergence if
p=1
. If
p>1
, it is enough to note that by (
5.10
) the sequence
\{V^{\delta}\}_{\delta}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{p}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
, and thus converges weakly in
L^{p}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
as well. This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

The following result shows that for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in\Omega
, the Radon-Nikodým derivative of
\mathcal{F}
with respect to
\mu
can be characterised by means of the minimum values of the problems
\mathfrak{m}
on small cubes.

Theorem 5.5
.

Assume that
\mathcal{F}\in\mathfrak{F}^{p}_{\star}
. Then, for every
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
(u,U)\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
,
we have

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{{\mathcal{F}}(u,U,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\mu(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}=\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\mu(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}

for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in\Omega
, where
\mu
is the measure introduced in (
5.6
).

Proof.

The result can be proved arguing exactly as in
[
40
, Theorem 4.3]
, replacing
[
40
, Lemmas 4.1 and 4.2]
by our Lemmas
5.3
and
5.4
, respectively. Indeed, the proof in
[
40
]
relies on measure theoretical arguments alone and does not require the use of the specific structure of the underlying function space.
∎

5.2.
Characterisation of bulk and surface densities

In the rest of the section we prove Theorem
3.7
. We prove separately equalities (
3.7
) and (
3.8
), addressing first the integral representation of the absolutely continuous part of
\mathcal{F}
.

Proposition 5.6
.

Assume that
\mathcal{F}\in\mathfrak{F}^{p}_{\star}
. Then for every
(u,U)\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega)
we have

(5.19)

\mathcal{F}^{a}(u,U,B)=\int_{B}f_{\rm bulk}\big(x,u,\nabla u,U\big)\,{\rm d}x,

where
f_{\rm bulk}
is the function defined by (
3.5
).

Proof.

To prove (
5.19
), we will characterise the Radon-Nikodým derivative of
\mathcal{F}
with respect to the Lebesgue measure, by showing that

(5.20)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\mathcal{F}(u,U,\cdot)}{{\rm d}\mathcal{L}^{d}}(x)=f_{\rm bulk}\big(x,\widetilde{u}(x),\nabla u(x),\widetilde{U}(x)\big)

for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e.
x\in\Omega
, where here and in the following
\sim
refers to the approximate limit, see (
A.2
) below. Let us fix a point
x\in\Omega
of approximate continuity of
u
, satisfying

(5.21)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q(x,\rho)}|\nabla u(y)-\nabla u(x)|\,{\rm d}y=0,

(5.22)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u}\cap Q(x,\rho))=0\quad\text{ and }\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}|D^{c}u|(Q(x,\rho))=0,

and

(5.23)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\mathcal{F}(u,U,\cdot)}{{\rm d}\mathcal{L}^{d}}(x)=\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathcal{F}(u,U,Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}=\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}},

(5.24)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q(x,\rho)}|U(y)-\widetilde{U}(x)|\,{\rm d}x=0,

and such that the statement of Lemma
D.1
holds. Note that these conditions are satisfied by
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e. point of
\Omega
thanks to Proposition
A.1
, Besicovitch and Lebesgue Derivation Theorems, and Theorem
5.5
, so that it will be sufficient to show (
5.20
) for such a point
x
.

Recalling the definition (
3.5
) of the bulk density
f_{\rm bulk}
, equality (
5.23
) implies that (
5.20
) is equivalent to

(5.25)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}=\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}(\ell_{x},\widetilde{U}(x),Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}},

where, for brevity, we write
\ell_{x}\coloneq\ell_{x,\widetilde{u}(x),\nabla u(x)}=\widetilde{u}(x)+\nabla u(x)(\cdot-x)
.
This equality is proved in two steps, dealing with the two inequalities in (
5.25
) separately. Before doing so, we introduce some further notation related to the statement of Lemma
D.1
. We let
u_{\rho}
and
\overline{u}_{\rho}
be the functions defined for
y\in Q(x,\rho)
by
u_{\rho}(y)\coloneq({u}(y)-\widetilde{u}(x))/\rho
and
\overline{u}_{\rho}(y)\coloneq(u_{\rho}(y)\lor\tau^{\prime}(u_{\rho},Q(x,\rho))\land\tau^{\prime\prime}(u_{\rho},Q(x,\rho))
. Thanks to the lemma, we have

(5.26)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d+1}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{u_{\rho}\neq\overline{u}_{\rho}\})=0,

(5.27)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q(x,\rho)}|\overline{u}_{\rho}(y)-\nabla u(x)\frac{(y-x)}{\rho}|\,{\rm d}y=0,

(5.28)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q(x,\rho)}|\rho\nabla\overline{u}_{\rho}(y)-\nabla u(x)|\,{\rm d}y=0,

(5.29)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{\overline{u}_{\rho}}\cap Q(x,\rho))=0\quad\text{ and }\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}|D^{c}\overline{u}_{\rho}|(Q(x,\rho))=0.

Step 1.
(
\leq
) In this step we prove that

(5.30)

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}\leq\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}(\ell_{x},\widetilde{U}(x),Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}.

To this end, let us fix
\theta\in(0,1/4)
, set
Q^{\theta,\rho}\coloneq Q(x,(1-3\theta)\rho)
, and consider
(v_{\rho},V_{\rho})
belonging to
\mathcal{C}^{p}_{\star}(\ell_{x},\widetilde{U}(x),Q^{\theta,\rho})
such that

(5.31)

\mathcal{F}\big(v_{\rho},V_{\rho},Q^{\theta,\rho}\big)\leq\mathfrak{m}\big(\ell_{x},\widetilde{U}(x),Q^{\theta,\rho}\big)+\rho^{d+1}.

We extend the function by setting
v_{\rho}=\ell_{x}
on
Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}
.
In view of (
5.26
), Fubini’s Theorem implies that for every
\rho>0
there exists
s_{\rho}\in((1-\theta)\rho,\rho)
such that

(5.32)

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial Q(x,s_{\rho})\cap\{\overline{u}_{\rho}\neq u_{\rho}\})=0.

We introduce the functions
\{z_{\rho}\}_{\rho}
defined by

(5.33)

z_{\rho}(y)\coloneq\begin{cases}\overline{u}_{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(y)&\text{ if $y\in Q(x,s_{\rho})$,}\\
u_{\rho}(y)&\text{ if $y\in Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q(x,s_{\rho})$,}\end{cases}

which, thanks to Lemma
A.7
, belong to
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(Q(x,\rho);\mathbb{R}^{k})
. We note that by (
5.21
)–(
5.22
), (
5.27
)–(
5.29
), and (
5.32
) they satisfy

(5.34)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q(x,(1-\theta)\rho)}|{z}_{\rho}(y)-\nabla u(x)\frac{(y-x)}{\rho}|\,{\rm d}y=0,

(5.35)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q(x,\rho)}|\rho\nabla{z}_{\rho}(y)-\nabla u(x)|\,{\rm d}y=0,

(5.36)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{{z}_{\rho}}\cap Q(x,\rho))=0\quad\text{ and }\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}|D^{c}{z}_{\rho}|(Q(x,\rho))=0.

Indeed, property (
5.34
) is an immediate consequence of (
5.27
) and of (
5.33
), and (
5.35
) follows from (
5.21
) and (
5.28
). As for the remaining property (
5.36
), we use (
5.22
), (
5.29
), (
5.32
), and

J_{z_{\rho}}\subset\big(J_{u}\cap Q(x,\rho)\big)\cup\big(\partial Q(x,s_{\rho})\cap\{\overline{u}_{\rho}\neq u_{\rho}\}\big).

We set

(5.37)

O^{\prime}\coloneq Q(x,{1-2\theta}),\quad O\coloneq Q(x,1-\theta),\quad\text{ and }O^{\prime\prime}\coloneq Q(x,1)\setminus\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q(x,{1-3\theta})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

and

(5.38)

W_{\rho}(y)\coloneq\begin{cases}V_{\rho}(y)&\text{ in }Q^{\theta,\rho},\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho})}\Big(\int_{Q(x,\rho)}U(y)\,{\rm d}y-\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q^{\theta,\rho})\widetilde{U}(x)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Big)&\text{ in }Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}.\end{cases}

Since
(v_{\rho},V_{\rho})\in\mathcal{C}^{p}_{\star}(\ell_{x},\widetilde{U}(x),Q^{\theta,\rho})
, we have

\int_{Q^{\theta,\rho}}V_{\rho}(y)\,{\rm d}y=\int_{Q^{\theta,\rho}}\widetilde{U}(x)\,{\rm d}y=\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q^{\theta,\rho})\widetilde{U}(x),\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

so that

(5.39)

\int_{Q(x,\rho)}W_{\rho}(y)\,{\rm d}y=\int_{Q(x,\rho)}{U}(y)\,{\rm d}y.

We now want to apply Lemma
5.1
to
v_{\rho}
with
u
as a boundary datum near
\partial Q(x,\rho)
. We begin by observing that
v_{\rho}\in L^{1}(O_{\rho,x}\setminus O^{\prime}_{\rho,x};\mathbb{R}^{k})
since
v_{\rho}=\ell_{x}

\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e. in
Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}
. Moreover, from the definition (
5.33
) of
z_{\rho}
, the fact that
s_{\rho}\geq(1-\theta)\rho
, and the fact that
\bar{u}_{\rho}
is bounded it follows immediately that
\widetilde{u}(x)+\rho z_{\rho}\in L^{1}(O_{\rho,x}\setminus O^{\prime}_{\rho,x};\mathbb{R}^{k})
. Hence,
for fixed
\eta>0
, we may apply Lemma
5.1
to
v_{\rho}
(in place of
u
) and to
\widetilde{u}(x)+\rho z_{\rho}
(in place of
v
) and with
O^{\prime},O^{\prime\prime},O
as in (
5.37
). We obtain a constant
M>0
, depending on
\theta
but independent of
\rho
, and
w_{\rho}\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(Q(x,\rho);\mathbb{R}^{k})
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(w_{\rho},W_{\rho},Q(x,\rho))

\displaystyle\leq(1+\eta)\big(\mathcal{F}(v_{\rho},W_{\rho},O_{\rho,x})+\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{u}(x)+\rho z_{\rho},W_{\rho},O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})\big)

(5.40)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{M}{\rho}\|(\widetilde{u}(x)+\rho z_{\rho})-v_{\rho}\|_{L^{1}(O_{\rho,x}\setminus O^{\prime}_{\rho,x})}+\eta\rho^{d}.

Moreover, the same lemma and (
5.33
) ensure that

(5.41)

w_{\rho}=v_{\rho}\quad\text{ $\mathcal{L}^{d}$-a.e.\ in $O_{\rho,x}^{\prime}$}\quad\text{ and }\quad w_{\rho}=\widetilde{u}(x)+\rho z_{\rho}=u\quad\text{ $\mathcal{L}^{d}$-a.e.\ in $Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q(x,s_{\rho})$}.

From the second equality in (
5.41
) and (
5.39
) we deduce that
(w_{\rho},W_{\rho})\in\mathcal{C}^{p}_{\star}(u,U,Q(x,\rho))
. Therefore, by (
5.40
) and the fact that
v_{\rho}=\ell_{x}

\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e. in
Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}
we infer

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q(x,\rho))

\displaystyle\leq(1+\eta)\big(\mathcal{F}(v_{\rho},W_{\rho},O_{\rho,x})+\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{u}(x)+\rho z_{\rho},W_{\rho},O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})\big)

(5.42)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{M}{\rho}\|\rho z_{\rho}-\nabla u(x)(\cdot-x)\|_{L^{1}(O_{\rho,x}\setminus O^{\prime}_{\rho,x})}+\eta\rho^{d}.

We now estimate each term on the right-hand side of (
5.42
). First, note that (
5.34
) implies

(5.43)

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{M}{\rho^{d+1}}\|\rho z_{\rho}-\nabla u(x)(\cdot-x)\|_{L^{1}(O_{\rho,x}\setminus O^{\prime}_{\rho,x})}=0.

We use Lemma
5.2
, with
O_{1}=Q^{\theta,\rho}
and
O_{2}=O_{\rho,x}
, locality
(

H

2)
, and (
5.38
) to get

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(v_{\rho},W_{\rho},O_{\rho,x})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{F}(v_{\rho},V_{\rho},Q^{\theta,\rho})+\beta\mathcal{V}(v_{\rho},Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho})

(5.44)

\displaystyle\quad+\beta\|W_{\rho}\|_{L^{p}(Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho})}^{p}+\beta(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})\rho^{d}.

Recalling (
5.31
) and that

v_{\rho}=\ell_{x}
on
Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}
, we then get

(5.45)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(v_{\rho},V_{\rho},Q^{\theta,\rho})\leq\mathfrak{m}(\ell_{x},\widetilde{U}(x),Q^{\theta,\rho})+\rho^{d+1}\leq\beta k(|\nabla u(x)|+|\widetilde{U}(x)|^{p}+1)\rho^{d}+\rho^{d+1},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(5.46)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}(v_{\rho},Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho})\leq(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})k|\nabla u(x)|\rho^{d}.

Here, in the second inequality of (
5.45
) we used that
(\ell_{x},\widetilde{U}(x))
is a competitor for
\mathfrak{m}(\ell_{x},\widetilde{U}(x),Q^{\theta,\rho})
and thus
\mathfrak{m}(\ell_{x},\widetilde{U}(x),Q^{\theta,\rho})\leq\beta k(|\nabla u(x)|+|\widetilde{U}(x)|^{p}+1)\rho^{d}
by the upper bound in (H4). As for the remaining term in (
5.44
), we observe that

\displaystyle\int_{Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}}|W_{\rho}|^{p}\,{\rm d}y\leq\frac{1}{\rho^{d(p-1)}(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})^{p-1}}\Big|\int_{Q(x,\rho)}U(y)\,{\rm d}y-\int_{Q^{\theta,\rho}}\widetilde{U}(x)\,{\rm d}y\Big|^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq\frac{C}{\rho^{d(p-1)}(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})^{p-1}}\Big(\Big|\int_{Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}}\hskip-14.22636ptU(y)\,{\rm d}y\Big|^{p}+\Big|\int_{Q^{\theta,\rho}}(U(y)-\widetilde{U}(x))\,{\rm d}y\Big|^{p}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq\frac{C\rho^{dp}}{\rho^{d(p-1)}(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})^{p-1}}\Big(\Big|\fint_{Q(x,\rho)}\hskip-2.84544ptU(y)\,{\rm d}y-(1-3\theta)^{d}\fint_{Q^{\theta,\rho}}\hskip-2.84544ptU(y)\,{\rm d}y\Big|^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\Big|(1-3\theta)^{d}\fint_{Q^{\theta,\rho}}\hskip-2.84544pt(U(y)-\widetilde{U}(x))\,{\rm d}y\Big|^{p}\Big)

for a constant
C>0
depending only on
p
.
In view of (
5.24
), this implies

(5.47)

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}}|W_{\rho}|^{p}\,{\rm d}y\leq{C}|(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})|\widetilde{U}(x)|^{p}.

In a similar fashion, observing that
\mathcal{L}^{d}(O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})\rho^{d}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and that
\mathcal{V}
is invariant by adding constants, we may estimate

(5.48)

\ \mathcal{F}(\widetilde{u}(x)+\rho z_{\rho},W_{\rho},O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})\leq\beta\mathcal{V}(\rho z_{\rho},O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})+\beta\|W_{\rho}\|_{L^{p}(Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho})}^{p}+\beta(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})\rho^{d},

and observe that

\mathcal{V}(\rho z_{\rho},O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})\leq k\Big(\int_{O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x}}|\rho\nabla z_{\rho}|\,{\rm d}x+\rho|D^{c}z_{\rho}|(O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})+\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{z_{\rho}}\cap O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})\Big).

Note that by (
5.35
) and (
5.36
) we have

(5.49)

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{V}(\rho z_{\rho},O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})\leq k(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})|\nabla u(x)|.

We now combine (
5.42
)–(
5.46
) and (
5.47
)–(
5.49
), divide by
\rho^{d}
, pass to the limsup as
\rho\to 0^{+}
, and obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}\leq(1-3\theta)^{d}\limsup_{\rho^{\prime}\to 0^{+}}\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{m}(\ell_{x},\widetilde{U}(x),Q(x,\rho^{\prime}))}{(\rho^{\prime})^{d}}\Big)+\eta\beta k(|\nabla u(x)|+|\widetilde{U}(x)|^{p}+1)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(1+\eta)\Big(\beta(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})(2+2k|\nabla u(x)|)+\frac{2C((1-(1-3\theta)^{d})^{p}}{(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})^{p-1}}|\widetilde{U}(x)|^{p}\Big)+\eta,

where we have set
\rho^{\prime}\coloneq(1-3\theta)\rho
.
Letting
\theta\to 0^{+}
and
\eta\to 0^{+}
, we obtain (
5.30
).

Step 2.
(
\geq
) The proof of this inequality follows along the same lines of Step 1 by interchanging the roles of
(u,U)
and
(\ell_{x},\widetilde{U}(x))
. The essential difference is that the Fubini-type argument in (
5.32
) is performed for some
s_{\rho}\in((1-4\theta)\rho,(1-3\theta)\rho)
, we choose a competitor
(v_{\rho},V_{\rho})\in\mathcal{C}^{p}_{\star}(u,U,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q(x,s_{\rho}))\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
satisfying

\mathcal{F}(v_{\rho},V_{\rho},Q(x,s_{\rho}))\leq\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q(x,s_{\rho}))+\rho^{d+1},

we extend
v_{\rho}
to the whole
Q(x,\rho)
by setting
v_{\rho}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{u}(x)+\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{u}_{\rho}
outside of
Q(x,s_{\rho})
and we define
W_{\rho}
outside of
Q(x,s_{\rho})
by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W_{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q(x,\rho)\setminus Q(x,s_{\rho}))}\Big(\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q(x,\rho))\widetilde{U}(x)-\int_{Q(x,s_{\rho})}U(y)\,{\rm d}y\Big)}

This ensures that the Fundamental Estimate can again be employed to adjust the boundary values. We omit the details.
∎

We proceed by addressing the integral representation of the jump part of
\mathcal{F}
, concluding the proof of Theorem
3.7
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Assume that
\mathcal{F}\in\mathfrak{F}^{p}_{\star}
. Then for every
(u,U)\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega)
we have

(5.50)

\mathcal{F}^{j}(u,U,B)=\int_{J_{u}\cap B}\!f_{\rm surf}\big(x,u^{+},u^{-},\nu_{u})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}.

where
f_{\rm surf}
is the function defined by (
3.6
).

Proof.

To prove (
5.50
), we characterise the Radon-Nikodým derivative of
\mathcal{F}
with respect to the measure
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}J_{u}
by showing that

(5.51)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\mathcal{F}(u,U,\cdot)}{{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}J_{u}}(x)=f_{\rm surf}\big(x,u^{+}(x),u^{-}(x),\nu_{u}(x)\big),

for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
x\in J_{u}
.
Let us fix a point
x\in J_{u}
satisfying

(5.52)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\int_{Q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho)}|\nabla u|\,{\rm d}y=0\quad\text{ and }\quad\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}|D^{c}u|(Q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))=0,

(5.53)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u}\cap E_{\rho,x})=\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Pi^{\nu_{u}(x)}_{x}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap E)\quad\text{for all Borel sets }E\subset Q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nu_{u}(x)}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0},1),

where
\Pi^{\nu}_{x}\coloneq\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\colon\,(y-x)\cdot\nu=0\}
for all
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and

(5.54)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\mathcal{F}(u,U,\cdot)}{{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}J_{u}}(x)=\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathcal{F}(u,U,Q_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}=\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}},

(5.55)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\int_{Q_{\nu_{u}(x)}(x,\rho)}|U(y)|^{p}\,{\rm d}y=0,

and such that the statement of Lemma
D.2
holds. Note that these conditions are satisfied by
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e. point of
J_{u}
thanks to Proposition
A.1
,
[
5
, Theorem 2.56]
, Besicovitch and Lebesgue Derivation Theorems, and Theorem
5.5
, where for (
5.53
) we can argue as in (
D.21
)–(
D.22
) below. Thus, it is sufficient to show (
5.51
) for such a point
x
.

Recalling the definition (
3.6
) of the surface density
f_{\rm surf}
, equality (
5.54
) implies that (
5.51
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\mathcal{\rho}^{d-1}}=\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}(v_{x},0,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\mathcal{\rho}^{d-1}},

where, for brevity, we set
v_{x}\coloneq v_{x,u^{+}(x),u^{-}(x),\nu_{u}(x)}
and
\nu=\nu_{u}(x)
.
This equality is proved in two steps, dealing with the two inequalities separately. To this end, we apply Lemma
D.2
to get
u_{\rho}\in{\rm BV}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);\mathbb{R}^{k})
which satisfy

(5.56)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{u_{\rho}\neq u\}\cap Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))=0,

(5.57)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}|u_{\rho}-v_{x}|\,{\rm d}y=0,

(5.58)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\ \frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\,\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u_{\rho}}\cap E_{\rho,x})\leq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\Pi_{x}^{\nu}\cap E)\quad\text{for all Borel sets }E\subset Q_{\nu}(x,1),

(5.59)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\ \frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\int_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}|\nabla u_{\rho}|\,\mathrm{d}y=0\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\ \frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}|D^{c}u_{\rho}|(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))=0.

Step 1.
("
\leq
")
In this step we prove that

(5.60)

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\mathcal{\rho}^{d-1}}\leq\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}(v_{x},0,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\mathcal{\rho}^{d-1}}.

To this end, we fix
\theta\in(0,1/4)
, set
Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu}\coloneq Q_{\nu}(x,(1-3\theta)\rho)
, and consider
(v_{\rho},V_{\rho})\in\mathcal{C}^{p}_{\star}(v_{x},0,Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu})
such that

(5.61)

\mathcal{F}(v_{\rho},V_{\rho},Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu})\leq\mathfrak{m}(v_{x},0,Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu})+\rho^{d}.

We extend
v_{\rho}
to the whole cube
Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)
by setting
v_{\rho}=v_{x}
on
Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu}
. Moreover, we set

(5.62)

\hskip-11.38092ptW_{\rho}(y)\coloneq\begin{cases}V_{\rho}(y)&\text{ in }Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu},\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu})}\int_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}U(y)\,{\rm d}y&\text{ in }Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu}.\end{cases}

Since
(v_{\rho},V_{\rho})\in\mathcal{C}^{p}_{\star}(v_{x},0,Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu})
, we have

(5.63)

\int_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}W_{\rho}(y)\,{\rm d}y=\int_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}U(y)\,{\rm d}y.

In light of (
5.56
), Fubini’s Theorem implies that for every
\rho>0
there exists
s_{\rho}\in((1-\theta)\rho,\rho)
such that

(5.64)

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial Q_{\nu}(x,s_{\rho})\cap\{{u}_{\rho}\neq u\})=0.

We also introduce
z_{\rho}
defined by

(5.65)

z_{\rho}(y)\coloneq\begin{cases}u_{\rho}(y)&\text{ if $y\in Q_{\nu}(x,s_{\rho})$,}\\
u(y)&\text{ if $y\in Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus Q_{\nu}(x,s_{\rho})$,}\end{cases}

which by Lemma
A.7
belong to
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);\mathbb{R}^{k})
. Note that by (
5.52
)–(
5.53
), (
5.57
)–(
5.59
), and (
5.64
) it holds that

(5.66)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q_{\nu}(x,(1-\theta)\rho)}|{z}_{\rho}(y)-v_{x}|\,{\rm d}y=0,

(5.67)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\mathcal{V}(z_{\rho},E_{\rho,x}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap J_{z_{\rho}})\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}k\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\Pi^{\nu}_{x}\cap E)\quad\text{ for all Borel sets $E\subset Q_{\nu}(x,1)$},

(5.68)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\int_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}|\nabla{z}_{\rho}(y)|\,{\rm d}y=0\quad\text{ and }\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}|D^{c}{z}_{\rho}|(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))=0.

We set

(5.69)

O^{\prime}\coloneq Q_{\nu}(x,{1-2\theta}),\quad O\coloneq Q_{\nu}(x,1-\theta),\quad\text{ and }O^{\prime\prime}\coloneq Q_{\nu}(x,1)\setminus\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{\nu}(x,1-3\theta)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}.

Observe that
v_{\rho}\in L^{1}(O_{\rho,x}\setminus O^{\prime}_{\rho,x};\mathbb{R}^{k})
since
v_{\rho}=v_{x}

\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e. in
Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu}
. Moreover, from definition (
5.65
) and the fact that
s_{\rho}\geq(1-\theta)\rho
, it follows that
z_{\rho}\in L^{1}(O_{\rho,x}\setminus O^{\prime}_{\rho,x};\mathbb{R}^{k})
. Hence,
for fixed
\eta>0
, we may apply Lemma
5.1
to
v_{\rho}
(in place of
u
) and to
z_{\rho}
in place of
v
and with
O^{\prime},O^{\prime\prime},O
as in (
5.69
). We obtain a constant
M>0
depending on
\theta
but independent of
\rho
, and functions
w_{\rho}\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(Q_{\nu\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(x,\rho);\mathbb{R}^{k})
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(w_{\rho},W_{\rho},Q(x,\rho))

\displaystyle\leq(1+\eta)\big(\mathcal{F}(v_{\rho},W_{\rho},O_{\rho,x})+\mathcal{F}(z_{\rho},W_{\rho},O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})\big)+\frac{M}{\rho}\|z_{\rho}-v_{\rho}\|_{L^{1}(O_{\rho,x}\setminus O^{\prime}_{\rho,x})}+\eta\rho^{d},

(5.70)

\displaystyle=(1+\eta)\big(\mathcal{F}(v_{\rho},W_{\rho},O_{\rho,x})+\mathcal{F}(z_{\rho},W_{\rho},O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})\big)+\frac{M}{\rho}\|z_{\rho}-v_{x}\|_{L^{1}(O_{\rho,x}\setminus O^{\prime}_{\rho,x})}+\eta\rho^{d},

where in the equality we have used that
v_{\rho}=v_{x}

\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e. in
Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu}
.
Moreover, the same lemma and (
5.65
) gives

(5.71)

w_{\rho}=v_{\rho}\quad\text{ $\mathcal{L}^{d}$-a.e.\ in $O_{\rho,x}^{\prime}$}\quad\text{ and }\quad w_{\rho}=u\quad\text{ $\mathcal{L}^{d}$-a.e.\ in $Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus Q_{\nu}(x,s_{\rho})$}.

From the second equality in (
5.71
) and (
5.63
) we deduce that
(w_{\rho},W_{\rho})\in\mathcal{C}^{p}_{\star}(u,U,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))
. Therefore, by (
5.2
) we infer

(5.72)

\displaystyle\hskip-4.26773pt\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))

\displaystyle\leq(1+\eta)\big(\mathcal{F}(v_{\rho},W_{\rho},O_{\rho,x})+\mathcal{F}(z_{\rho},W_{\rho},O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})\big)+\frac{M}{\rho}\|z_{\rho}-v_{x}\|_{L^{1}(O_{\rho,x}\setminus O^{\prime}_{\rho,x})}+\eta\rho^{d}.

We estimate each term on the right-hand side of (
5.72
). First, we note that (
5.66
) implies

(5.73)

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{M}{\rho^{d}}\|z_{\rho}-v_{x}\|_{L^{1}(O_{\rho,x}\setminus O^{\prime}_{\rho,x})}=0.

We use Lemma
5.2
, with
O_{1}=Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu}
and
O_{2}=O_{\rho,x}
, locality (H2), and (
5.62
) to get

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(v_{\rho},W_{\rho},O_{\rho,x})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{F}(v_{\rho},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}V_{\rho},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu})+\beta\mathcal{V}(v_{\rho},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu})

(5.74)

\displaystyle\quad+\beta\|W_{\rho}\|_{L^{p}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu})}^{p}+\beta(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})\rho^{d}.

Recalling (
5.61
) and that

v_{\rho}=v_{x}
on
Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu}
, we have

(5.75)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(v_{\rho},V_{\rho},Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu})\leq\mathfrak{m}(v_{x},0,Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu})+\rho^{d}\leq\beta\rho^{d}+\beta k\rho^{d-1}+\rho^{d},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(5.76)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}(v_{\rho},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu})\leq(1-(1-3\theta)^{d-1})k\rho^{d-1}.

Here, in the second inequality of (
5.75
) we used that
(v_{x},0)
is a competitor for
\mathfrak{m}(v_{x},0,Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu})
and thus
\mathfrak{m}(v_{x},0,Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu})\leq\beta\rho^{d}+\beta k\rho^{d-1}
by the upper bound in (H4). As for the remaining term in (
5.74
), using Hölder’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu}}|W_{\rho}|^{p}\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho^{d(p-1)}(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})^{p-1}}\Big|\int_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}U(y)\,{\rm d}y\Big|^{p}

(5.77)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho^{d(p-1)}}{\rho^{d(p-1)}(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})^{p-1}}\|U\|^{p}_{L^{p}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\|U\|^{p}_{L^{p}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}

for some universal
C>0
, where we used
\theta<1/4
. Similarly, we may estimate

(5.78)

\mathcal{F}(z_{\rho},W_{\rho},O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})\leq\beta\mathcal{V}(z_{\rho},O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})+\beta\|W_{\rho}\|_{L^{p}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus Q^{\theta,\rho}_{\nu})}^{p}+\beta(1-(1-3\theta)^{d})\rho^{d}

and observe that

(5.79)

\mathcal{V}(z_{\rho},O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})\leq k\Big(\int_{O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x}}|\nabla z_{\rho}|\,{\rm d}x+|D^{c}z_{\rho}|(O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})+\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{z_{\rho}}\cap O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})\Big).

Hence, using (
5.67
) with
E=O^{\prime\prime}
and (
5.68
), we get

(5.80)

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\mathcal{V}(z_{\rho},O^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,x})\leq(1-(1-3\theta)^{d-1})k.

Putting together (
5.72
)–(
5.80
), dividing by
\rho^{d-1}
, letting
\rho\to 0^{+}
, and using (
5.55
) we get

\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-3\theta)^{d-1}\limsup_{\rho^{\prime}\to 0^{+}}\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{m}(v_{x},0,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho^{\prime}))}{(\rho^{\prime})^{d-1}}\Big)+\eta\beta k

\displaystyle\quad+(1+\eta)\Big(\beta(1-(1-3\theta)^{d-1})2k\Big),

where we have set
\rho^{\prime}\coloneq(1-3\theta)\rho
.
Letting
\theta\to 0^{+}
and
\eta\to 0^{+}
, we obtain (
5.60
).

Step 2.
(
\geq
) As in the previous proof, the other inequality can be proven along similar lines. We therefore omit the full proof and indicate only the essential changes. We follow Step 1 by interchanging the roles of
(u,U)
and
(v_{x},0)
. The Fubini-type argument in (
5.64
) is performed for some
s_{\rho}\in((1-4\theta)\rho,(1-3\theta)\rho)
, we choose a competitor
(v_{\rho},V_{\rho})\in\mathcal{C}^{p}_{\star}(u,U,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{\nu\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(x,s_{\rho}))\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
satisfying

\mathcal{F}(v_{\rho},V_{\rho},Q_{\nu\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(x,s_{\rho}))\leq\mathfrak{m}(u,U,Q_{\nu\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(x,s_{\rho}))+\rho^{d},

and we extend
v_{\rho}
to the whole
Q_{\nu\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(x,\rho)
by setting
v_{\rho}=u_{\rho}
outside of
Q_{\nu\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(x,s_{\rho})
, as well as

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W_{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=-\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q_{\nu\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(x,\rho)\setminus Q_{\nu\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(x,s_{\rho}))}\int_{Q_{\nu\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(x,s_{\rho})}U(y)\,{\rm d}y

outside of
Q_{\nu\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(x,s_{\rho})
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.7
.

The proof follows by combining Propositions
5.6
and
5.7
.
∎

Remark 5.8
.

In the recent papers
[
33
]
and
[
30
]
, the integral representation of a family of functionals related to
\mathfrak{F}^{p}_{\star}
is addressed. In particular, in the latter work, the authors study a class
\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
of functionals
E\colon L^{0}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})\times\mathcal{B}(\Omega)\to[0,+\infty]
whose domain is
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and that satisfy, among others, the following property: there exists a constant
C_{\rm Tr}>0
such that for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
u\in L^{0}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
,
B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega)
,
w\in W^{1,1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, and
R>0
it holds (see (
A.6
))

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}E(w+\Phi_{R_{i}}\circ(u-w),B)

\displaystyle\leq E(u,B)+C_{\rm Tr}\int_{B^{R}_{u,w}}|\nabla w|\,{\rm d}x+C_{\rm Tr}\mathcal{L}^{d}(B^{R}_{u,w})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C_{\rm Tr}}{N}\Big(E(u,B)+\mathcal{L}^{d}(B)+\int_{B}|\nabla w|\,{\rm d}x\Big),

where
R_{i}\coloneq\tau^{i-1}R
(see (
A.5
), for some
\tau
large enough) and
B^{R}_{u,w}\coloneq\{x\in B\colon|u(x)-w(x)|\geq R\}
. Relying on truncation arguments based on the previous property, it is shown in
[
30
, Theorem 5.16]
that every functional
E\in\mathfrak{E}_{\rm sc}
satisfies a property similar to the one described in Theorem
3.7
for functionals in
\mathfrak{F}^{p}_{\star}
, in the sense that, for every bounded open set
O\subset\Omega
, the bulk and surface parts of
E(\cdot,O)
can be represented as integral functionals on
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
.

The methods used in this section show that the result
[
30
, Theorem 5.16]
can actually be derived
without
the above truncation property.

6.
Relaxation of bulk and surface energies: Proof of Theorems
3.13
,
3.14
,
3.16

This section is devoted to the proof of the Relaxation Theorems
3.13
,
3.14
, and
3.16
, concerning the integral representation of the relaxed functional
I^{p}_{\star}
defined by (
3.11
) and (
3.12
).

6.1.
Proof of Theorem
3.13

We start the section by presenting a useful consequence of the Approximation Theorem
3.1
, which will be exploited in the proof of Theorem
3.13
. Recall the structural constants
c_{\Psi}
,
C_{\Psi}
,
c_{\gamma}
, and
C_{\gamma}
in the assumptions on
\Psi
and
\gamma
, as well as
\overline{C}_{\Psi}
in (
3.13
).

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
(g,G)\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and let
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
. Assume that
\Psi
and
\gamma
satisfy
(

Ψ

1)
–
(

Ψ

4)
,
(

γ

1)
, and
(

γ

2)
. Then

\alpha\big(\mathcal{V}(g,O)+\|G\|^{p}_{L^{p}(O)}-\mathcal{L}^{d}(O)\big)\leq I^{p}_{\star}(g,G,O)\leq\beta\big(\mathcal{V}(g,O)+\|G\|^{p}_{L^{p}(O)}+\mathcal{L}^{d}(O)\big)

for two suitable positive constants
\alpha,\beta
, depending only on
c_{\Psi}
,
C_{\Psi}
,
\overline{C}_{\Psi}
,
c_{\gamma}
,
C_{\gamma}
, and
p
.

Proof.

Using
(

Ψ

4)
we find
\Psi(x,A)\geq\frac{c_{\Psi}}{2}(|A|^{p}+|A|)-C_{\Psi}^{\prime}
for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e.
x\in\Omega
and all
A\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}

for some
C_{\Psi}^{\prime}>0
sufficiently large. Then, the lower bound follows by
(

γ

2)
, together with the lower semicontinuity of
\mathcal{V}
(see Remark
2.1
) and of
\|G\|^{p}_{L^{p}(O)}
under weak convergence.

To prove the upper bound, we use the Approximation Theorem
3.1
to obtain a sequence
\{u_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
with
\nabla u_{n}=G

\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e. in
O
and such that

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{V}(u_{n},O)\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C\big(\mathcal{V}(g,O)+\|G\|_{L^{1}(O)}\big).\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Thanks to Remark
3.9
, the previous inequality, and
(

γ

2)
, we then have

\displaystyle I^{p}_{\star}(g,G,O)\leq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}(u_{n},O)

\displaystyle\leq\beta\big(\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{V}(u_{n},O)+\|G\|^{p}_{L^{p}(O)}+\mathcal{L}^{d}(O)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C\beta\big(\mathcal{V}(g,O)+\|G\|_{L^{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(O)}+\|G\|^{p}_{L^{p}(O)}+\mathcal{L}^{d}(O)\big).\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Using that
|A|\leq 1+|A|^{p}
for all
A\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
, the proof is concluded. ∎

We are now ready to prove Theorem
3.13
.

Proof of Theorem
3.13
.

Thanks to Theorem
3.7
, to conclude the proof, it is enough to show that
I^{p}_{\star}\in\mathfrak{F}^{p}_{\star}
.

Let us fix
(g,G)\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
.
Given
O_{1},O_{2},O_{3}\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
with

O_{1}\subset\subset O_{2}\subset O_{3}\subset\Omega
, we claim that

(6.1)

I^{p}_{\star}(g,G,O_{3})\leq I^{p}_{\star}(g,G,O_{2})+I^{p}_{\star}(g,G,O_{3}\setminus\overline{O_{1}}).

This inequality can be obtained with an argument similar to, for instance,
[
42
, Theorem 2.8]
, but for the sake of completeness we present here the full proof.
Without loss of generality, we prove this inequality only in the case
p=1
as the case
p>1
follows by similar, but easier, arguments. Let
\{u_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(O_{2};\mathbb{R}^{k})
and

\{v_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(O_{3}\setminus\overline{O_{1}};\mathbb{R}^{k})
be two sequences satisfying the following properties:
u_{n}\mathbin{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}(g,G)
in
O_{2}
,
v_{n}\mathbin{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}(g,G)
in
O_{3}\setminus\overline{O_{1}}
, and

(6.2)

I^{p}_{\star}(g,G,O_{2})=\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}(u_{n},O_{2})\quad\text{ and }\quad I^{p}_{\star}(g,G,O_{3}\setminus\overline{O_{1}})=\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}(v_{n},O_{3}\setminus\overline{O_{1}}).

Note also that

(6.3)

u_{n}-v_{n}\to\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0\text{ in }\;L^{0}(O_{2}\setminus\overline{O_{1}};{\mathbb{R}}^{k})\text{ as $n\to+\infty$}.

Let us fix
\delta>0
and consider
O_{\delta}\coloneq\{x\in O_{2}\colon\,\mbox{dist}(x,O_{1})<\delta\}
. We let
{\rm d}(x)\coloneq\mbox{dist}(x,O_{1})
be the distance function from
O_{1}
, which we recall is Lipschitz continuous. Therefore, by the Area Formula
[
5
, (2.47)]
we have

\int_{O_{\delta}\setminus\overline{O_{1}}}|u_{n}(x)-v_{n}(x)|\land 1\,J{\rm d}(x)\,{\rm d}x=\int_{0}^{\delta}\Big(\int_{{\rm d}^{-1}(y)}|u_{n}(x)-v_{n}(x)|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(x)\Big)\,{\rm d}y,

where we let
J{\rm d}
denote the Jacobian of
{\rm d}
. In light of (
6.3
) and of the fact that
J{\rm d}
is bounded, we may apply Fatou’s Lemma to obtain, for
\mathcal{L}^{1}
-a.e.
\rho\in[0,\delta]
,

(6.4)

\liminf_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{{\rm d}^{-1}(\rho)}|u_{n}-v_{n}|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(x)=\liminf_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{\partial O_{\rho}}|u_{n}-v_{n}|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(x)=0.

Let us fix
\delta_{0}\in[0,\delta]
such that (
6.4
) holds and
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial O_{\delta_{0}})<+\infty
. The latter can be assumed by the Coarea Formula. By passing to a non-relabelled subsequence of
\{u_{n}-v_{n}\}_{n}
, we may also assume that the liminf in (
6.4
) is actually a limit. Since
O_{\delta_{0}}
is a set with
locally Lipschitz boundary (being the level set of a Lipschitz function), we can consider
u_{n},v_{n}

on
\partial O_{\delta_{0}}
in the sense of traces of
{\rm GBV_{\star}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
-functions, and define

\displaystyle w_{n}\coloneq\begin{cases}u_{n}&\text{ in}\;\overline{O_{\delta_{0}}},\\
v_{n}&\text{ in}\;O_{3}\setminus\overline{O_{\delta_{0}}}.\end{cases}

It follows from Lemma
A.7
that
\{w_{n}\}\subset{\rm GBV}_{\star}(O_{3};\mathbb{R}^{k})
,

|[w_{n}]|=|u_{n}-v_{n}|

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e. on
\partial O_{\delta_{0}}
, and

w_{n}\mathbin{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}(g,G)
in
O_{3}
. Hence, by
(

γ

2)
,
(
6.4
), and (
6.2
) we get

\displaystyle I^{p}_{\star}(g,G,O_{3})

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\Big(\int_{O_{3}}\Psi(x,\nabla w_{n})\,{\rm d}x+\int_{J_{w_{n}}\cap O_{3}}\gamma(x,[w_{n}],\nu_{w_{n}})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\Big(_{O_{2}}\Psi(x,\nabla u_{n})\,{\rm d}x+\int_{J_{u_{n}}\cap O_{2}}\gamma(x,[u_{n}],\nu_{u_{n}})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{O_{3}\setminus\overline{O_{1}}}\Psi(x,\nabla v_{n})\,{\rm d}x+\int_{J_{v_{n}}\cap(O_{3}\setminus\overline{O_{1}})}\gamma(x,[v_{n}],\nu_{v_{n}})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{J_{w_{n}}\cap\partial O_{\delta_{0}}}C_{\gamma}|u_{n}-v_{n}|\land 1\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\Big)

\displaystyle=I^{p}_{\star}(g,G,O_{2})+I^{p}_{\star}(g,G,O_{3}\setminus\overline{O_{1}}).

This concludes the proof of (
6.1
).

Next, one can check that
O\mapsto I^{p}_{\star}(g,G,O)
coincides with the restriction to
\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
of a bounded Radon measure on
\Omega
. We omit the exact argument which can be found in
[
25
, Proposition 2.22, Step 1]
, using Lemma
6.1
and (
6.1
) in place of
[
25
, Lemma 2.18, Lemma 2.21]
. This yields (H1)
and proves that definition (
3.12
) is well-posed (see the comment after (
3.12
)).

Moreover, by inner regularity on open sets, we have that
I^{p}_{\star}
satisfies the upper bound (
3.2
) with
B
replaced by
O
, for every
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
. Combining this observation with (
3.12
) and Lemma
6.1
we get that
I^{p}_{\star}
satisfies
(

H

4)
as well (up to a shift).

To prove that
I^{p}_{\star}
satisfies the lower semicontinuity property
(

H

3)
, it is enough to repeat exactly the arguments used in
[
25
, Proposition 5.1]
. Finally, locality property
(

H

2)
is a simple consequence of lower semicontinuity property
(

H

3)
as observed in
[
18
, Remark 2.1]
. This concludes the proof of
I^{p}_{\star}\in\mathfrak{F}^{p}_{\star}
and thus of the theorem.
∎

6.2.
Proof of Theorem
3.14

Throughout the rest of the section, we let
\Phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{k};\mathbb{R}^{k})
be a function satisfying (
A.5
), for a fixed
\tau\geq\max\{3,\beta_{\gamma}+1\}
, where
\beta_{\gamma}
is the constant appearing in
(

γ

5)
. For
R>0
, we let
\Phi_{R}
be the function associated to
\Phi
via (
A.6
). The next truncation result will be useful for proving Theorem
3.14
as it allows to truncate competitors appearing in suitable minimisation problems. It will also be crucial in the proof of Lemma
6.5
below, a key ingredient in the proof of Theorem
3.16
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Assume that
\Psi
and
\gamma
satisfy conditions
(

Ψ

1)
–
(

Ψ

4)
,
(

γ

1)
,
(

γ

2)
, and
(

γ

5)
.
Let
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on the structural constants
c_{\Psi},\bar{C}_{\Psi},c_{\gamma},C_{\gamma},
and
\beta_{\gamma}
, such that for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
R>0
,
u\in{\rm SBV}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, and
B\in\mathcal{B}(O)
it holds

(6.5)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{E}(\Phi_{R_{i}}\circ u,B)\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)\mathcal{E}(u,B)+\mathcal{L}^{d}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\{|u|\geq R\}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})+\frac{C}{N}\mathcal{L}^{d}(B),

where we have set
R_{i}\coloneq\tau^{i-1}R
. If, in addition,
\gamma
satisfies
(

γ

6)
as well, then for every
x\in\Omega
and
\rho>0
the same property holds with the same constant
C
, for the functional
\mathcal{E}^{x}_{\rho}
defined by

(6.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{x}_{\rho}(u,B)

\displaystyle\coloneq\int_{B}\Psi(x+\rho y,\nabla u(y))\,{\rm d}y+\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{J_{u}\cap B}\gamma(x+\rho y,\rho[u](y),\nu_{u}(y))\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)

for every
u\in{\rm SBV}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(O)
.

Proof.

The proof of inequality (
6.5
) for
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{E}^{x}_{\rho}
can be obtained arguing exactly as in the section of the proof of
[
30
, Proposition 3.14]
devoted to property (g) of
[
30
, Definition 3.8]
.
∎

Later, given a sequence
\{u_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
converging in
{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}
, it will be essential to determine the limit of the truncated sequence
\Phi_{R}\circ u_{n}
in a related topology. This is taken care of in the next lemma. We remark that the proof below cannot be reproduced in the case
p=1
, unless we replace in Definition
3.2
weak
∗
-convergence in
\mathcal{M}_{b}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
with weak
L^{1}
-convergence.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
p>1
, let
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
, let
(g,G)\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, let
\{u_{n}\}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset{\rm SBV}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
with
u_{n}\to_{SD_{\star}}(g,G)
in
O
, and let
R>0
. Consider the functions
g^{R}\coloneq\Phi_{R}\circ g
,
u^{R}_{n}\coloneq\Phi_{R}\circ u_{n}
, and
G^{R}\coloneq\nabla\Phi_{R}(g)G
. Then
\{u_{n}^{R}\}_{n}
converges to
g^{R}
strongly in
L^{1}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
\{\nabla u^{R}_{n}\}_{n}
converges weakly in
L^{p}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
to
G^{R}
. In particular, if
g\in L^{\infty}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
R>2\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})}
, then
g^{R}=g
and
G^{R}=G
.

Proof.

By the Chain Rule for
{\rm BV}
-functions (see
[
5
, Proposition 3.96]
) we have

(6.7)

\nabla u^{R}_{n}=\nabla\Phi_{R}(u_{n})\nabla u_{n}

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
We pass to a subsequence
\{u_{n_{\ell}}\}_{\ell}
that converges pointwise a.e. to
g
as
\ell\to+\infty
. As
\Phi_{R}
is a
C^{1}
-function, from the pointwise convergence of
\{u_{n_{\ell}}\}_{\ell}
and the Dominated Convergence Theorem it follows that
\{u^{R}_{n_{\ell}}\}_{\ell}
converges to
g^{R}
strongly in
L^{1}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and

\{\nabla\Phi_{R}(u_{n_{\ell}})\}_{\ell}
converges to
\nabla\Phi_{R}(g)
strongly in
L^{q}(\Omega;\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
for every
1\leq q<+\infty
and pointwise a.e. in
O
.

We now prove that
\{\nabla\Phi_{R}(u_{n_{\ell}})\nabla u_{n_{\ell}}\}_{\ell}
converges to
G^{R}
weakly in
L^{p}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
. To this end, let us fix
\varphi\in L^{q}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
, where
q\coloneq p/(p-1)
denotes the conjugate exponent of
p
. We have

\displaystyle\int_{O}\varphi:(\nabla\Phi_{R}(u_{n_{\ell}})\nabla u_{n_{\ell}}-\nabla\Phi_{R}(g)G)\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{O}\varphi:(\nabla\Phi_{R}(g)\nabla u_{n_{\ell}}-\nabla\Phi_{R}(g)G)\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{O}\varphi:(\nabla\Phi_{R}(u_{n_{\ell}})\nabla u_{n_{\ell}}-\nabla\Phi_{R}(g)\nabla u_{n_{\ell}}\big)\,{\rm d}x,

where for
A,L\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
the symbol
A:L
denotes the Frobenius scalar product between
A
and
L
. By weak convergence in
L^{p}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
of
\{\nabla u_{n_{\ell}}\}_{\ell}
to
G
, the first term converges to
0
as
\ell\to+\infty
. For the second term, we note instead that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{O}\varphi:(\nabla\Phi_{R}(u_{n_{\ell}})\nabla u_{n_{\ell}}

\displaystyle-\nabla\Phi_{R}(g)\nabla u_{n_{\ell}}\big)\,{\rm d}x\Big|\leq\|\nabla u_{n_{\ell}}\|_{L^{p}(O)}\Big(\int_{O}|\varphi|^{q}|\nabla\Phi_{R}(u_{n_{\ell}})-\nabla\Phi_{R}(g)|^{q}\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{1/q}.

Since
\sup_{\ell}\|\nabla u_{n_{\ell}}\|_{L^{p}(O)}<+\infty
by weak convergence,
|\nabla\Phi_{R}|\leq 1
by (
A.7
), and
\{\nabla\Phi_{R}(u_{n_{\ell}})\}_{\ell}
converges to
\nabla\Phi_{R}(g)
pointwise a.e. in
O
, an application of the Dominated Convergence Theorem shows that
\{\nabla\Phi_{R}(u_{n_{\ell}})\nabla u_{n_{\ell}}\}_{\ell}
converges to
G^{R}
weakly in
L^{p}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
as
\ell\to+\infty
. As the limit does not depend on the chosen subsequence
\{n_{\ell}\}_{\ell}
, by (
6.7
) the whole sequences
\{u^{R}_{n}\}_{n}
and
\{\nabla u^{R}_{n}\}_{n}
converge to
g^{R}
and
G^{R}
, respectively. This concludes the proof. ∎

We are now ready to prove Theorem
3.14
.

Proof of Theorem
3.14
.
Throughout the proof, given
x\in\Omega
, we let
\mathcal{E}^{x}_{0}
be the functional defined by

\mathcal{E}^{x}_{0}(u,B)\coloneq\int_{B}\Psi(x,\nabla u(y))\,{\rm d}y+\int_{J_{u}\cap B}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(x,[u](y),\nu_{u}(y))\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)

for every
u\in{\rm SBV}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(Q)
. We split the proof into two parts, addressing first the statement concerning the bulk energy density. We divide its proof into several steps.

Step 1.
(Bulk: Auxiliary cell formula) For every
x\in\Omega
and
A,L\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{p}(x,A,L)\coloneq\inf

\displaystyle\Big\{\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}^{x}_{0}(u_{n},Q)\colon\,\{u_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{k}),

(6.8)

\displaystyle\,\text{ }u_{n}\to\ell_{0,0,A}\text{ strongly in $L^{1}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{k})$},\,\,\nabla u_{n}\rightharpoonup L\text{ \color[rgb]{0,0,0}weakly \color[rgb]{0,0,0}in $L^{p}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})$}\Big\}.

We will prove that for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and for all
A,L\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
it holds

(6.9)

\widetilde{H}_{p}(x,A,L)\leq f_{\rm bulk}(x,A,L)\leq H_{p}(x,A,L).\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Since by
(

Ψ

2)
, (
3.16
), and Remark
3.12
the densities
\Psi(x,\cdot)
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(x,\cdot)
satisfy assumptions
{\rm(\mathscr{H}1)}_{p}
,
{\rm(\mathscr{H}2)}
, and
{\rm(\mathscr{H}4)}
of
[
25
]
, it follows from
[
25
, Proposition 3.1]
that
\widetilde{H}_{p}(x,A,L)={H}{}_{p}(x,A,L)
for every
x\in\Omega
and every
A,L\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
. Therefore, proving
(
6.9
) is sufficient to show that in (
3.18
) we can write
H_{p}
in place of
f_{\rm bulk}
.

By definition of
f_{\rm bulk}
(see also Remark
3.8
) and by Theorem
5.5
applied with
\mathcal{F}=I^{p}_{\star}
we have

(6.10)

f_{\rm bulk}(x,A,L)=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}(\ell_{x,0,A},L,Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{I^{p}_{\star}(\ell_{x,0,A},L,Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}<+\infty\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e.
x\in\Omega
and for all
A,L\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
. Therefore, to prove
(
6.9
) it suffices to show that

(6.11)

\widetilde{H}_{p}(x,A,L)\leq f_{\rm bulk}(x,A,L)\leq{H}_{p}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(x,A,L)

for all
x\in\Omega
such that (
6.10
) holds and for all
A,L\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
.

Step 2.
(Bulk:
\widetilde{H}_{p}\leq f_{\rm bulk}
) We let
x\in\Omega
be such that (
6.10
) holds, and let
A,L\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
.
For any
\rho>0
we consider a sequence
\{u^{\rho}_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
with
u^{\rho}_{n}\to_{SD_{\star}}(\ell_{x,0,A},L)
in
Q(x,\rho)
,
\{u^{\rho}_{n}\}_{n}
converging to
\ell_{x,0,A}
pointwise a.e. in
Q(x,\rho)
,
and

(6.12)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}(u^{\rho}_{n},Q(x,\rho))=I^{p}_{\star}(\ell_{x,0,A},L,Q(x,\rho)).

We introduce the functions
v^{\rho}_{n}\in{\rm SBV}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{k})
defined by
v_{n}^{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(y)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\coloneq\frac{1}{\rho}u^{\rho}_{n}(x+\rho y)
for
y\in Q
, which satisfy

(6.13)

\displaystyle\text{$v_{n}^{\rho}\mathbin{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}(\ell_{0,0,A},L)$ in $Q$ }\text{ and }v_{n}^{\rho}\to\ell_{0,0,A}\text{ pointwise a.e.\ as $n\to+\infty$},

(6.14)

\displaystyle\nabla v_{n}^{\rho}(y)=\nabla{u_{n}^{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(x+\rho y)\quad\text{for $\mathcal{L}^{d}$-a.e.\ $y\in Q$},

(6.15)
