\displaystyle[v_{n}^{\rho}](y)=\frac{1}{\rho}[u_{n}^{\rho}](x+\rho y)\quad\text{for $\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-a.e.\ $y\in J_{v^{n}_{\rho}}=(J_{u^{\rho}_{n}}-x)/\rho$}.

Using (
6.14
) and (
6.15
), a change of variables then shows that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\rho>0
we have

(6.16)

\mathcal{E}(u^{\rho}_{n},Q(x,\rho))=\rho^{d}\mathcal{E}_{\rho}^{x}(v_{n}^{\rho},Q),

where
\mathcal{E}^{\rho}_{x}
is the functional given by (
6.6
).

We set
R\coloneq 2\|\ell_{0,0,A}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{k})}\leq 2\sqrt{d}|A|
and
R_{i}\coloneq\tau^{i-1}R
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Given
N\in\mathbb{N}
, we may apply Lemma
6.2
to the functional
\mathcal{E}_{x}^{\rho}
and to
v^{\rho}_{n}
. We find
i_{n}=i(n,N,\rho)\in\{1,\dots,N\}
such that

(6.17)

\mathcal{E}_{\rho}^{x}(\Phi_{R_{i_{n}}}\circ v_{n}^{\rho},Q)\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)\mathcal{E}_{\rho}^{x}(v_{n}^{\rho},Q)+\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{|v^{\rho}_{n}|\geq R\})+\frac{C}{N}\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q)

for
C>0
independent of
n
,
\rho
,
N
,
A,
and
L
. We remark that by (
6.13
) we have

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\chi_{\{|v_{n}^{\rho}|\geq R\}}(y)

\leq\chi_{\{|\ell_{0,0,A}|\geq R\}}(y)

for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e.
y\in Q
, so that by Fatou’s lemma

(6.18)

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{L}^{d}\big(\{|v^{\rho}_{n}|\geq R\}\big)\leq\mathcal{L}^{d}\big(\{|\ell_{0,0,A}|\geq R\}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cap Q\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)=0\quad\text{ for all $\rho>0$}.

We set
w^{\rho}_{n}\coloneq\Phi_{R_{i_{n}}}\circ v_{n}^{\rho}\in{\rm SBV}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{k})
. Then, in view of (
6.13
), Lemma
6.3
gives

\displaystyle\text{$\{w^{\rho}_{n}\}_{n}$ converges to $\ell_{0,0,A}$ strongly in $L^{1}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{k})$ as $n\to+\infty$},

\{\nabla w^{\rho}_{n}\}_{n}
converges to
L
weakly in
L^{p}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
as
n\to+\infty
,

so that, for each
\rho>0
, the sequence
\{w^{\rho}_{n}\}_{n}
is a competitor for the minimisation problem in (
6.8
). Therefore,

(6.19)

\widetilde{H}_{p}(x,A,L)\leq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}^{x}_{0}(w^{\rho}_{n},Q)\quad\text{ for all $\rho>0$}.

In order to use (
6.16
) we still need to compare
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}^{x}_{0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(w^{\rho}_{n},Q)
and
\mathcal{E}_{\rho}^{x}(w^{\rho}_{n},Q)
. To this end, we begin by observing that, by the fact that
\vartheta
is nondecreasing,
(

γ

6)
, (
6.6
), (
6.17
), and the fact that
\|w^{\rho}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}\leq\tau^{N}R
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{J_{w^{\rho}_{n}}\cap Q}

\displaystyle\gamma^{0}\big(x+\rho y,[w^{\rho}_{n}](y),\nu_{w^{\rho}_{n}}(y)\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)-\int_{\ J_{w^{\rho}_{n}}\cap Q}\frac{1}{\rho}\gamma\big(x+\rho y,\rho[w^{\rho}_{n}](y),\nu_{w^{\rho}_{n}}(y)\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\vartheta\big(\tau^{N}2R\rho\big)\int_{J_{w^{\rho}_{n}}\cap Q}\frac{1}{\rho}\gamma\big(x+\rho y,\rho[w^{\rho}_{n}](y),\nu_{w^{\rho}_{n}}(y)\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)

(6.20)

\displaystyle\leq\vartheta\big(\tau^{N}2R\rho\big)\Big(\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)\mathcal{E}_{\rho}^{x}(v_{n}^{\rho},Q)+\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{|v^{\rho}_{n}|\geq R\})+\frac{C}{N}\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q)\Big).

In light of (
6.12
), (
6.16
), and (
6.18
), we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\,\,\vartheta\big(\tau^{N}2R\rho\big)\Big(\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}_{\rho}^{x}(v_{n}^{\rho},Q)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{|v^{\rho}_{n}|\geq R\})+\frac{C}{N}\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q)\Big)

(6.21)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\vartheta\big(\tau^{N}2R\rho\big)\Big(\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)\frac{I^{p}_{\star}(\ell_{x,0,A},L,Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}+\frac{C}{N}\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q)\Big)=0,

where in the last equality we have used that
\vartheta
is a continuous function with
\vartheta(0)=0
, and that
x
is such that (
6.10
) holds.
Exploiting (
3.17
), (
6.10
), the fact that
\|w^{\rho}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}\leq\tau^{N}R
, and
(

γ

2)
with a similar argument we can prove that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\Big|\int_{J_{w^{\rho}_{n}}\cap Q}\gamma^{0}\big(x+\rho y,

\displaystyle[w^{\rho}_{n}](y),\nu_{w^{\rho}_{n}}(y)\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)

(6.22)

\displaystyle-\int_{J_{w^{\rho}_{n}}\cap Q}\gamma^{0}\big(x,[w^{\rho}_{n}](y),\nu_{w^{\rho}_{n}}(y)\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\Big|=0.

In a similar fashion, using
(

Ψ

5)
we can also show that

(6.23)

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\Big|\int_{Q}\Psi\big(x+\rho y,\nabla w^{\rho}_{n}\big)\,{\rm d}y-\int_{Q}\Psi\big(x,\nabla w^{\rho}_{n}\big)\,{\rm d}y\Big|=0.

We are now ready to conclude the proof of Step 2. Combining (
6.10
), (
6.12
), and (
6.16
)–(
6.23
), we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{p}(x,A,L)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}^{x}_{0}(w_{n}^{\rho},Q)=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}^{x}_{\rho}(w_{n}^{\rho},Q)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)\mathcal{E}_{\rho}^{x}(v_{n}^{\rho},Q)+\frac{C}{N}\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)\frac{I^{p}_{\star}(\ell_{x,0,A},L,Q(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d}}+\frac{C}{N}\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q)=\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f_{\rm bulk}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(x,A,L)+\frac{C}{N}\mathcal{L}^{d}(Q).

Letting
N\to+\infty
, we obtain the first inequality in (
6.11
).

Step 3.
(Bulk:
f_{\rm bulk}\leq{H}_{p}
) The second inequality in (
6.11
) follows by an argument similar to the one presented in
Step 2
. Let
\eta>0
and let
u\in\mathcal{C}^{\rm bulk}_{p,\star}(A,L)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(see (
3.20
)) be such that

(6.24)

\int_{Q}\Psi\big(x,\nabla u(y)\big)\,{\rm d}y+\int_{J_{u}\cap Q}\gamma^{0}\big(x,[u](y),\nu_{u}(y)\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\leq{H}_{p}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(x,A,L)+\eta.

For every
\rho>0
, we consider the function
v^{\rho}\coloneq\rho u(\frac{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-x}{\rho})\in{\rm SBV}(Q(x,\rho);\mathbb{R}^{k})
and observe that

(6.25)

\displaystyle\nabla v^{\rho}(z)=\nabla u(\tfrac{z-x}{\rho})\quad\text{for $\mathcal{L}^{d}$-a.e.\ $z\in Q(x,\rho)$},

(6.26)

\displaystyle[v^{\rho}](z)=\rho[u](\tfrac{z-x}{\rho})\quad\text{for $\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-a.e.\ $z\in J_{v^{\rho}}$}.

In particular, from (
6.25
) and the fact that
u\in C^{\rm bulk}_{p,\star}(A,L)
we deduce that

v^{\rho}=\ell_{x,0,A}
close to
\partial Q\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(x,\rho)
and, by a change of variables,

\int_{Q(x,\rho)}\nabla v^{\rho}\,{\rm d}y=\int_{Q(x,\rho)}L\,{\rm d}y,

so that
v^{\rho}
is a competitor for the minimisation problem
\mathfrak{m}(\ell_{x,0,A},L,Q(x,\rho))
. As such,

(6.27)

\mathfrak{m}(\ell_{x,0,A},L,Q(x,\rho))\leq\mathcal{E}(v^{\rho},Q(x,\rho)).

Using (
6.25
) and (
6.26
), a change of variables gives

\mathcal{E}(v^{\rho},Q(x,\rho))=\rho^{d}\int_{Q}\Psi\big(x+\rho y,\nabla u(y)\big)\,{\rm d}y+\rho^{d-1}\int_{J_{u}\cap Q}\gamma\big(x+\rho y,\rho[u](y),\nu_{u}(y)\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y).

From
(

Ψ

5)
,
(

γ

4)
, and (
3.13
) it follows that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{Q}\Psi\big(x+\rho y,\nabla u(y)\big)\,{\rm d}y-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{J_{u}\cap Q}\gamma\big(x+\rho y,\rho[u](y),\nu_{u}(y)\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)

\displaystyle-\int_{Q}\Psi\big(x,\nabla u(y)\big)\,{\rm d}y+\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{J_{u}\cap Q}\gamma\big(x,\rho[u](y),\nu_{u}(y)\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\Big|

(6.28)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\omega_{\Psi}(\rho)\int_{Q}2\overline{C}_{\Psi}(|\nabla u|^{p}+1)\,{\rm d}y+\omega_{\gamma}(\rho)\int_{J_{u}\cap Q}|[u](y)|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y).

By inequality
(

γ

2)
we also get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\rho}\gamma\big(x,\rho[u](y),\nu_{u}(y)\big)\leq C_{\gamma}|[u](y)|

for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
y\in J_{u}\cap Q
. Hence, combining (
6.27
) with (
6.28
), dividing by
\rho^{d}
, letting
\rho\to 0^{+}
, and using (
3.14
), the Dominated Convergence Theorem, and the fact that
\omega_{\Psi}
and
\omega_{\gamma}
are continuous functions with
\omega_{\Psi}(0)=0
and
\omega_{\gamma}(0)=0
, we get

f_{\rm bulk}(x,A,L)\leq\int_{Q}\Psi\big(x,\nabla u(y)\big)\,{\rm d}y+\int_{J_{u}\cap Q}\gamma^{0}\big(x,[u](y),\nu_{u}(y)\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y).

Finally, recalling (
6.24
), we conclude
f_{\rm bulk}(x,A,L)\leq{H}_{p}(x,A,L)+\eta
.
Letting
\eta\to 0^{+}
we conclude the proof of the second inequality in (
6.11
) and thus of the part of the statement concerning the bulk energy density.

Step 4.
(Surface: Auxiliary cell formula) We are left with proving the part of the statement concerning the surface energy density. The proof can be obtained arguing similarly to
[
25
, Theorem 4.5]
, taking into account some modifications due to the different growth conditions.

The first step is to define an auxiliary cell formula. For every
x\in\Omega
,
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{h}_{p}(x,\zeta,\nu)\coloneq\inf

\displaystyle\Big\{\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\int_{J_{u_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\cap Q_{\nu}}\gamma(x,[u_{n}](y),\nu_{u_{n}}(y))\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\colon\,\{u_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(Q_{\nu};\mathbb{R}^{k})

\displaystyle\quad u_{n}\to v_{\zeta,\nu}\text{ in }L^{1}(Q_{\nu};\mathbb{R}^{k}),\,\,\nabla u_{n}\to 0\text{ strongly in $L^{p}(Q_{\nu};\mathbb{R}^{k})$}\Big\},

where we have set
v_{\zeta,\nu}\coloneq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v_{0,\zeta,0,\nu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(see (
3.4
)).
Using arguments very similar to
[
25
, Proposition 4.2]
, properly taking into account also the
\nu
-dependence (see
[
45
, Remark 3.5, Point 5]
), one can show that

\widetilde{h}_{p}(x,\zeta,\nu)=h_{p}(x,\zeta,\nu)\quad\text{ for all $x\in\Omega$, $\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{k}$, and $\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}$.}

Hence, in view of Theorem
5.5
and the definition of
f_{\rm surf}
(see also Remark
3.8
), to conclude, it is sufficient to show that

(6.29)

\widetilde{h}_{p}(x,\zeta,\nu)\leq f_{\rm surf}(x,\zeta,\nu)\leq h_{p}(x,\zeta,\nu)\quad\

for every
x\in\Omega
,
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
such that

(6.30)

f_{\rm surf}(x,\zeta,\nu)=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}(v_{x,\zeta,0,\nu},0,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{I^{p}_{\star}(v_{x,\zeta,0,\nu},0,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}<+\infty.

Step 5.
(Surface:
\widetilde{h}_{p}\leq f_{\rm surf}\leq h_{p}
) Let us fix
x\in\Omega
,
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
as above. Since for any
u\in\mathcal{C}^{\rm surf}_{p,\star}(\zeta,\nu)
(see (
3.21
)) the function defined by
v(y)\coloneq u((y-x)/\rho)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for
y\in Q_{\nu\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}(x,\rho)
is a competitor for
\mathfrak{m}(v_{x,\zeta,0,\nu},0,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))
, the first equality in (
6.30
) and a change of variables prove the second inequality in (
6.29
).

To prove
\widetilde{h}_{p}\leq f_{\rm surf}
, we argue as follows. For every
\rho>0
we let
\{u^{\rho}_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);\mathbb{R}^{k})
be such that
u_{n}^{\rho}\mathbin{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}(v_{x,\zeta,0,\nu},0)
in
Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)
and

(6.31)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}(u_{n}^{\rho},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))=I^{p}_{\star}(v_{x,\zeta,0,\nu},0,Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)).

We let
R\coloneq 2|\zeta|
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
, and apply Lemma
6.2
to obtain
i_{n}=i(n,N,\rho)
such that, setting
v_{n}^{\rho}\coloneq\Phi_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R_{i_{n}}}\circ u_{n}\in{\rm SBV}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);\mathbb{R}^{k})
, we have

(6.32)

\mathcal{E}(v_{n}^{\rho},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)\mathcal{E}(u_{n}^{\rho},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))+C\rho^{d}

and
\|v^{\rho}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}\leq\tau^{N}R
. Thanks to Lemma
6.3
, the sequence
\{v_{n}^{\rho}\}_{n}
converges to
v_{x,\zeta,0,\nu}
strongly in
L^{1}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
\{\nabla v^{\rho}_{n}\}_{n}
converges to
0
weakly in
L^{p}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))
. Moreover, from (
6.30
)–(
6.32
) we deduce that

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{J_{v^{\rho}_{n}}\cap Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma(y,[v^{\rho}_{n}](y),\nu_{v^{\rho}_{n}}(y))\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)f_{\rm surf}(x,\zeta,\nu),

which by
(

γ

2)
,
(

γ

4)
, and (
6.30
) implies

(6.33)

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{J_{v^{\rho}_{n}}\cap Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma(x,[v^{\rho}_{n}](y),\nu_{v^{\rho}_{n}}(y))\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)f_{\rm surf}(x,\zeta,\nu).

We now consider the functions
\{w^{\rho}_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(Q_{\nu};\mathbb{R}^{k})
defined by
w^{\rho}_{n}(y)\coloneq v^{\rho}_{n}(x+\rho y)
for
y\in Q_{\nu}
, such that

\displaystyle\nabla w^{\rho}_{n}(y)=\rho\nabla v^{\rho}_{n}(x+\rho y)\quad\text{ for $\mathcal{L}^{d}$-a.e. $y\in Q_{\nu}$},

\displaystyle[w^{\rho}_{n}](y)=[v^{\rho}_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}](x+\rho y)\quad\text{ for $\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-a.e.\ $y\in Q_{\nu}\cap J_{w^{\rho}_{n}}={(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J_{v^{\rho}_{n}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-x)/\rho$}.

As
\{v_{n}^{\rho}\}_{n}
converges to
v_{x,\zeta,0,\nu}
, for every
\rho>0
the sequence
\{w_{n}^{\rho}\}_{n}
converges to
v_{\zeta,\nu}
strongly in
L^{1}(Q_{\nu};\mathbb{R}^{k})
.
A change of variables,
(

Ψ

4)
, and (
6.30
)–(
6.32
) then show that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\int_{Q_{\nu}}|\nabla w^{\rho}_{n}|^{p}\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\rho^{p}}{\rho^{d}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}|\nabla v^{\rho}_{n}|^{p}\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\frac{\rho^{p-1}}{c_{W}}\Big(\frac{\mathcal{E}(v^{\rho}_{n},Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))}{\rho^{d-1}}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\rho}{c_{W}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\rho^{p-1}}{c_{W}}f_{\rm surf}(x,\zeta,\nu)=0.

Finally, with a diagonal argument we can find sequences
\{\rho_{\ell}\}_{\ell}
and
\{n_{\ell}\}_{\ell}
, with
\rho_{\ell}\to 0^{+}
and
n_{\ell}\to+\infty
as
\ell\to+\infty
, such that the functions defined by
z_{\ell}\coloneq w^{\rho_{\ell}}_{n_{\ell}}
are competitors for the minimisation problem defining
\widetilde{h}_{p}(x,\zeta,\nu)
.
Therefore, (
6.33
) and a change of variables give

\widetilde{h}_{p}(x,\zeta,\nu)\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)f_{\rm surf}(x,\zeta,\nu).

Letting
N\to+\infty
, we obtain the first inequality in (
6.29
). This concludes the proof. ∎

We now prove Proposition
3.15
.

Proof of Proposition
3.15
.
The properties concerning the bulk energy density
H_{p}
are a consequence of
[
14
, Theorem 2.10]
.
It is immediate to check that
h_{p}
satisfies
(

γ

1)
–
(

γ

3)
, while
(

γ

4)
can be obtained arguing exactly as in
[
14
, Theorem 2.10]
.

We now check that
h_{p}
satisfies
(

γ

5)
. To this end, we let
x\in\Omega
,
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, with
\beta_{\gamma}|\zeta_{1}|\leq|\zeta_{2}|
and
\zeta_{2}\neq 0
, and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. We consider
u_{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{\rm surf}_{p,\star}(\zeta_{2},\nu)
(recall (
3.21
)), set
\lambda\coloneq|\zeta_{1}|/|\zeta_{2}|
, and let
R\in{\rm SO}(k)
be such that
R(\lambda\zeta_{2})=\zeta_{1}
. Then,
u_{1}\coloneq\lambda R(u_{2})\in\mathcal{C}^{\rm surf}_{p,\star}(\zeta_{1},\nu)
, and clearly
\beta_{\gamma}|[u_{1}]|\leq\beta_{\gamma}\lambda|R[u_{2}]|\leq|[u_{2}]|
, where we have used that
\beta_{\gamma}\lambda\leq 1
. Since
\gamma
satisfies
(

γ

5)
, this implies

h_{p}(x,\zeta_{1},\nu)\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{J_{u_{1}}\cap Q_{\nu}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma(x,[u_{1}](y),\nu_{u_{1}}(y))\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{J_{u_{2}}\cap Q_{\nu}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma(x,[u_{2}](y),\nu_{u_{2}}(y))\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y).

From the arbitrariness of
u_{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{\rm surf}_{p,\star}(\zeta_{2},\nu)
, we conclude that
h_{p}
satisfies
(

γ

5)
.
∎

6.3.
Proof of Theorem
3.16

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
3.16
. We have already shown in Theorems
3.13
and
3.14
that the bulk and surface parts
(I^{p}_{\star})^{a}
and
(I^{p}_{\star})^{j}
admit an integral representation, so that we are left with proving that
(I^{p}_{\star})^{c}
can be represented as an integral functional, as well (see Definition
3.5
). This is done in three steps. We first use Lemma
6.4
below to deduce that
(I^{p}_{\star})^{c}(g,G,\cdot)=(I^{p}_{\star})^{c}(g,0,\cdot)
for all
(g,G)\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
. Then, for
\delta>0
we will consider a perturbed functional
I^{p,\delta}_{\star}(g,B)
defined for
g\in{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega)
which satisfies all the hypotheses of
[
18
, Theorem 3.12]
, and thus
admits a complete integral representation, including its Cantor part. Letting
\delta\to 0^{+}
and using Lemma
E.1
, we recover an integral representation for
(I^{p}_{\star})^{c}(g,0,B)
for all
g\in{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, which can then be extended to all
g\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
by a truncation argument based on Lemma
6.5
below.

We now present Lemma
6.4
, which states that
I^{p}_{\star}
is locally Lipschitz. Note that both in this lemma and in Lemma
6.5
below we do not assume that
\Psi
and
\gamma
are independent of the
x
-variable, a property that will only be used to apply
[
18
, Theorem 3.12]
in the proof of Theorem
3.16
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Assume that
\Psi
and
\gamma
satisfy
(

Ψ

1)
,
(

Ψ

2)
,
(

Ψ

4)
and
(

γ

1)
–
(

γ

3)
. Let
(g_{1},G_{1}),(g_{2},G_{2})\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
.
Then for every
B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega)
it holds

(6.34)

\displaystyle\big|I^{p}_{\star}(g_{1},G_{1},B)-I^{p}_{\star}(g_{2},G_{2},B)\big|\leq C_{\gamma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}(g_{1}-g_{2},B)+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C\|G_{1}-G_{2}\|^{p}_{L^{p}(B)}+\widehat{C}C\|G_{1}-G_{2}\|_{L^{p}(B)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

where
\widehat{C}\geq 0
is given by

(6.35)

\widehat{C}\coloneq I^{p}_{\star}(g_{1},G_{1},B)^{(p-1)/p}+I^{p}_{\star}(g_{2},G_{2},B)^{(p-1)/p}+(\mathcal{L}^{d}(B))^{(p-1)/p}

and
C>0
is a constant depending only on
c_{\Psi}
,
{C}_{\Psi}
,
C_{\gamma}
,
p
, and
d
.

Proof.

As
I^{p}_{\star}\in\mathfrak{F}^{p}_{\star}
, in view of (
3.12
), it is enough to prove (
6.34
) for a fixed
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
.
Arguing by symmetry, it is enough to show that

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359ptI^{p}_{\star}(g_{1},G_{1},O)

\displaystyle\leq I^{p}_{\star}(g_{2},G_{2},O)+C_{\gamma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}(g_{1}-g_{2},O)

(6.36)

\displaystyle\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+C\|G_{1}-G_{2}\|^{p}_{L^{p}(O)}+C\Big(I^{p}_{\star}(g_{2},G_{2},O)^{(p-1)/p}+(\mathcal{L}^{d}(O))^{(p-1)/p}\Big)\|G_{1}-G_{2}\|_{L^{p}(O)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

for
C>0
depending only on
c_{\Psi}
,
{C}_{\Psi}
,
C_{\gamma}
,
p
, and
d
.

We consider a sequence of functions
\{u_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
with
u_{n}\mathbin{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}(g_{2},G_{2})
in
O
and

(6.37)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}(u_{n},O)=I^{p}_{\star}(g_{2},G_{2},O).

We use Alberti’s Theorem
4.5
to find
h\in{\rm SBV}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
such that
\nabla h=G_{1}-G_{2}

\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e. in
O
. We then apply Lemma
4.3
(see Remark
4.4
) to
g_{1}-g_{2}-h
and obtain a sequence of piecewise constant functions
\{h_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
converging to
g_{1}-g_{2}-h
in
L^{0}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and such that

(6.38)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{V}(h_{n},O)=\mathcal{V}(g_{1}-g_{2}-h,O)\leq\mathcal{V}(g_{1}-g_{2},O)+(C(d)+1)\|G_{1}-G_{2}\|_{L^{1}(O\color[rgb]{0,0,0})},

where
C(d)
is the constant of Theorem
4.5
.
We set
v_{n}\coloneq u_{n}+h+h_{n}
and observe that
v_{n}\to g_{1}
in
L^{0}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
\nabla v_{n}\rightharpoonup G_{1}
weakly in
{L}^{p}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
as
n\to+\infty
, so that
v_{n}\mathbin{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}(g_{1},G_{1})
in
O
. In particular,

(6.39)

I^{p}_{\star}(g_{1},G_{1},O)\leq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}(v_{n},O).

As
J_{v_{n}}\subset J_{u_{n}}\cup J_{h_{n}}\cup J_{h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we can choose an orientation for the normals
such that, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle[v_{n}]=[u_{n}]+[h_{n}]+[h]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\text{ $\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-a.e. on $(J_{u_{n}}\cup J_{h_{n}}\cup J_{h}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\cap O$}.

By the previous observations, using also
(

Ψ

2)
,
(

γ

2)
,
(

γ

3)
, the fact that
\nabla h=G_{1}-G_{2}

\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e. in
O
, and Hölder’s inequality we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(v_{n},O)

\displaystyle=\int_{O}\Psi(x,\nabla u_{n}+\nabla h)\,{\rm d}x+\int_{J_{v_{n}}\cap O}\gamma(x,[v_{n}],\nu_{v_{n}})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{O}\Psi(x,\nabla u_{n})\,{\rm d}x+C_{\Psi}\int_{O}|\nabla h|(1+|\nabla u_{n}+\nabla h|^{p-1}+|\nabla u_{n}|^{p-1})\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{J_{u_{n}}\cap O}\gamma(x,[u_{n}],\nu_{v_{n}})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}+\int_{J_{h_{n}}\cap O}\gamma(x,[h_{n}],\nu_{h_{n}})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}+\int_{J_{h}\cap O}\gamma(x,[h],\nu_{h})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{O}\Psi(x,\nabla u_{n})\,{\rm d}x+\int_{J_{u_{n}}\cap O}\gamma(x,[u_{n}],\nu_{u_{n}})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}+C_{\gamma}\mathcal{V}(h_{n},O)

(6.40)

\displaystyle\quad+C\big(\|G_{1}-G_{2}\|^{p}_{L^{p}(O)}+\|G_{1}-G_{2}\|_{L^{1}(O)}\big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+C\|\nabla u_{n}\|^{p-1}_{L^{p}(O)}\|G_{1}-G_{2}\|_{L^{p}(O)}

for
C>0
depending only on
c_{\Psi}
,
C_{\Psi}
,
C_{\gamma}
, and
p
. In light of
(

Ψ

4)
and (
6.37
), we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{n}\|^{p-1}_{L^{p}(O\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{1}{c_{\Psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\int_{O\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\Big(\Psi(x,\nabla u_{n})+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{1}{c_{\Psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Big)\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{(p-1)/p}

\displaystyle\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2\Big(\frac{1}{c_{\Psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\Big)^{(p-1)/p}I^{p}_{\star}(g_{2},G_{2},O)^{(p-1)/p}+\Big(\frac{1}{c_{\Psi}^{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\Big)^{(p-1)/p}(\mathcal{L}^{d}(O))^{(p-1)/p}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
large enough.
Hence, in view of (
6.37
), (
6.38
), (
6.40
), and Hölder’s inequality we deduce

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}(v_{n},O)

\displaystyle\leq I^{p}_{\star}(g_{2},G_{2},O)+C_{\gamma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}(g_{1}-g_{2},O)

\displaystyle\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+C\|G_{1}-G_{2}\|^{p}_{L^{p}(O)}+C\Big(I^{p}_{\star}(g_{2},G_{2},O)^{(p-1)/p}+(\mathcal{L}^{d}(O))^{(p-1)/p}\Big)\|G_{1}-G_{2}\|_{L^{p}(O)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

for a different constant
C>0
, depending only on
c_{\Psi}
,
{C}_{\Psi}
,
C_{\gamma}
,
p
, and
d
. By (
6.39
), this leads to (
6.3
), which concludes the proof.
∎

We now study truncation properties of
I^{p}_{\star}(\cdot,0,\cdot)
.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
p>1
and assume that
\Psi
and
\gamma
satisfy
(

Ψ

1)
–
(

Ψ

4)
and
(

γ

1)
,
(

γ

2)
, and
(

γ

5)
. Then for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
g\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
,
B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega)
, and
R>0
it holds

(6.41)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}I^{p}_{\star}\big(\Phi_{R_{i}}\circ g,0,B\big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)I^{p}_{\star}(g,0,B)+C\mathcal{L}^{d}\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\{x\in B\colon\,|{g}(x)|\geq R\}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big)+\frac{C}{N}\mathcal{L}^{d}(B)

for
C>0
depending only on
c_{\Psi},\overline{C}_{\Psi},c_{\gamma},C_{\gamma},
and
p
, where we have set
R_{i}\coloneq\tau^{i-1}R
.

Moreover, for every
g\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
,
B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega)
, and every sequence
\{R_{N}\}_{N}\subset(0,+\infty)
, with
R_{N}\to+\infty
as
N\to+\infty
, it holds

(6.42)

\lim_{N\to+\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(I^{p}_{\star})^{c}(\Phi_{\tau^{i-1}R_{N}}\circ g,0,B)=(I^{p}_{\star})^{c}(g,0,B).

Proof.

As
I^{p}_{\star}\in\mathfrak{F}^{p}_{\star}
, in view of (
3.12
), it is enough to prove (
6.41
) and (
6.42
) for all
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
.
Let us fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
g\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
,
R>0
, and
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
.
We let
\{u_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm SBV}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
be a sequence with
u_{n}\mathbin{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}(g,0)
in
O
such that

(6.43)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}(u_{n},O)=I^{p}_{\star}(g,0,O).

We set
v^{i}_{n}\coloneq\Phi_{R_{i}}\circ u_{n}
and
v^{i}\coloneq\Phi_{R_{i}}\circ g
. An application of Lemma
6.3
yields

(6.44)

v^{i}_{n}\mathbin{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}(v^{i},0)\quad\text{in $O$ as $n\to+\infty$}

for all
i\in\{1,\dots,N\}
. Thanks to Lemma
6.2
, there exists
C>0
, depending only on
c_{\Psi},\overline{C}_{\Psi},c_{\gamma},C_{\gamma},
such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{E}(v^{i}_{n},O)\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)\mathcal{E}(u_{n},O)+C\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{x\in O\colon\,|{u_{n}}(x)|\geq R\})+\frac{C}{N}\mathcal{L}^{d}(O)

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. In light of (
6.43
) and of the convergence
u_{n}
to
g
in
L^{0}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, this implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}(v^{i}_{n},O)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+\frac{C}{N}\Big)I^{p}_{\star}(g,0,O)+C\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{x\in O\colon\,|{g}(x)|\geq R\}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})+\frac{C}{N}\mathcal{L}^{d}(O).

Combining this inequality with (
6.44
), by definition (
3.11
) of
I^{p}_{\star}
we infer
(
6.41
). The proof of (
6.42
) can be obtained arguing exactly as in
[
30
, Lemma 5.2]
.
∎

We are finally ready to prove Theorem
3.16
.

Proof of Theorem
3.16
.
As we have already characterised the bulk and surface parts of
I^{p}_{\star}
in Theorem
3.14
, we are left with proving that (recall Definition
3.5
)

(6.45)

(I^{p}_{\star})^{c}(g,G,B)=\int_{B}H_{p}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{{\rm d}D^{c}g}{{\rm d}|D^{c}g|},0\Big)\,{\rm d}|D^{c}g|

for all
(g,G)\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega)
.

Step 1.
(Replacement of
G
by
0
)
We first show that

(6.46)

(I^{p}_{\star})^{c}(g,G,B)=(I^{p}_{\star})^{c}(g,0,B)

for all
(g,G)\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega)
. Equivalently, we prove that
for all
(g,G)\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
it holds

(6.47)

\frac{{\rm d}I^{p}_{\star}(g,G,\cdot)}{{\rm d}|D^{c}g|}(x)=\frac{{\rm d}I^{p}_{\star}(g,0,\cdot)}{{\rm d}|D^{c}g|}(x)\quad\text{for $|D^{c}g|$-a.e.\ $x\in\Omega$.}

To prove this, for every
x\in\Omega
and
\rho>0
we use Lemma
6.4
with
g_{1}=g_{2}=g
and
B=B(x,\rho)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{I^{p}_{\star}(g,0,B(x,\rho))}{|D^{c}g|(B(x,\rho))}\leq\frac{I^{p}_{\star}(g,G,B(x,\rho))}{|D^{c}g|(B(x,\rho))}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{C}{|D^{c}g|(B(x,\rho))}\Big(\|G\|^{p}_{L^{p}(B(x,\rho))}+\widehat{C}\|G\|_{L^{p}(B(x,\rho))}\Big),

where
\widehat{C}
is given by (
6.35
). By the Besicovitch Derivation Theorem this proves that

\frac{{\rm d}I^{p}_{\star}(g,0,\cdot)}{{\rm d}|D^{c}g|}(x)\leq\frac{{\rm d}I^{p}_{\star}(g,G,\cdot)}{{\rm d}|D^{c}g|}(x)\quad\text{for $|D^{c}g|$-a.e.\ $x\in\Omega$.}

The reverse inequality is obtained in the exact same way. This concludes the proof of (
6.47
) and thus of (
6.46
).

Step 2.
(Perturbative argument) For
\delta>0
, consider the functional
I^{p,\delta}_{\star}\colon{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})\times\mathcal{B}(\Omega)\to[0,+\infty)
defined by

I^{p,\delta}_{\star}(g,B)\coloneq I^{p}_{\star}(g,0,B)+\delta\int_{J_{g}\cap B}|[g]|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\quad\text{ for all }g\in{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})\text{ and }B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega).

Since
I^{p}_{\star}\in\mathfrak{F}^{p}_{\star}
and
I^{p}_{\star}
satisfies the invariance property (
3.24
), the functional
I^{p,\delta}_{\star}
satisfies all hypotheses of
[
18
, Theorem 3.12]
. Hence, there exist functions
f_{\rm bulk}^{\delta}\colon\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
and
f_{\rm surf}^{\delta}\colon\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to[0,+\infty)
such that

(6.48)

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I^{p,\delta}_{\star}(g,B)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\int_{B}f_{\rm bulk}^{\delta}(\nabla g)\,{\rm d}x+\int_{B}f^{\delta,\infty}_{\rm bulk}\Big(\frac{{\rm d}D^{c}g}{{\rm d}|D^{c}g|}\Big)\,{\rm d}|D^{c}g|+\int_{J_{g}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B}f_{\rm surf}^{\delta}([g],\nu_{g})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

for all
g\in{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega)
. It is elementary to see that
f^{\delta}_{\rm bulk}=H_{p}
by Theorem
3.14
, and thus, in view of Proposition
3.15
, we have
f^{\delta}_{\rm bulk}(A)\leq C_{H}(|A|+1)
for all
A\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
. Similarly, as
h_{p}
satisfies
(

γ

2)
, it holds that
f^{\delta}_{\rm surf}(\zeta,\nu)\leq(C_{\gamma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\delta)|\zeta|
for all
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. Moreover, one can check that

f^{\delta}_{\rm surf}
and
f^{\delta}_{\rm bulk}
are nondecreasing in
\delta
, and that the same property holds for
A\mapsto f^{\delta,\infty}_{\rm bulk}(A)
when
A
is a rank-one matrix with unit norm. Thus, we may define

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{\rm bulk}(A)=\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}f^{\delta}_{\rm bulk}(A)=\inf_{\delta>0}f^{\delta}_{\rm bulk}(A)\quad\text{ for all $A\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}$,}

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{\rm Cantor}(A)\coloneq\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}f^{\delta,\infty}_{\rm bulk}(A)=\inf_{\delta>0}f^{\delta,\infty}_{\rm bulk}(A)\quad\text{ for all $A\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}$ with rank $1$ and $|A|=1$,}

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{\rm surf}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\zeta,\nu)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\coloneq\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}f_{\rm surf}^{\delta}(\zeta,\nu)=\inf_{\delta>0}f_{\rm surf}^{\delta}(\zeta,\nu)\quad\text{ for all $\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{k}$ and $\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}$}.

The function
\widehat{f}_{\rm Cantor}
is extended by
1
-homogeneity to all rank-one matrices.
Together with (
6.48
), the Monotone Convergence Theorem then implies that

I^{p}_{\star}(g,0,B)=\int_{B}\widehat{f}_{\rm bulk}(\nabla g)\,{\rm d}x+\int_{B}\widehat{f}_{\rm Cantor}\Big(\frac{{\rm d}D^{c}g}{{\rm d}|D^{c}g|}\Big)\,{\rm d}|D^{c}g|+\int_{J_{g}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B}\widehat{f}_{\rm surf}([g],\nu_{g})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

for all
g\in{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega)
. Thus, by Theorem
3.14
, we obtain

\widehat{f}_{\rm bulk}(A)=H_{p}(A,0)\quad\text{and }\quad\widehat{f}_{\rm surf}(\zeta,\nu)=h_{p}(\zeta,\nu)

for all
A\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
,
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.

Then, we observe that the corresponding bounds for
f^{\delta}_{\rm bulk}
and
f^{\delta}_{\rm surf}
also give that
\widehat{f}_{\rm bulk}(A)\leq C_{H}(|A|+1)
for all
A\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
and that
\widehat{f}_{\rm surf}(\zeta,\nu)\leq C_{\gamma}|\zeta|
for all
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. Since the functional
g\mapsto I^{p}_{\star}(g,0,O)
is
L^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
-lower semicontinuous on
{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
for all
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
, by Lemma
E.1
we then deduce that

H_{p}^{\infty}(A,0)=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{f}_{\rm Cantor}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(A)

for all rank-one matrices
A\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
and

I^{p}_{\star}(g,0,B)=\int_{B}H_{p}(\nabla g,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\,{\rm d}x+\int_{B}H_{p}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{{\rm d}D^{c}g}{{\rm d}|D^{c}g|},0\Big)\,{\rm d}|D^{c}g|+\int_{J_{g}\cap\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B}h_{p}([g],\nu_{g})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

for all
g\in{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega)
. In light of (
6.46
), this shows that (
6.45
) holds whenever
(g,G)\in{\rm SD}^{p}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, with
g\in{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
.

Step 3.
(Conclusion) We now deal with the general case
g\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
. To this end, for every
R>0
we consider the set
\widetilde{\Omega}^{R}_{g}\coloneq\{x\in\Omega\colon\,\widetilde{g}(x)\text{ exists and }|\widetilde{g}(x)|\leq R\}
. We claim that it is enough to prove (
6.45
) for every
B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega)
for which there exists
R>0
such that
B\subset\widetilde{\Omega}^{R}_{g}
. Indeed, by Proposition
A.6
we have that
\widetilde{\Omega}^{R}_{g}\nearrow\Omega_{\rm reg}\coloneq\{x\in\Omega\colon\widetilde{g}(x)\,\,\text{exists}\}
and
|D^{c}g|(\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\rm reg})=0
. Hence, every
B\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega)
can be written, up to a
|D^{c}g|
-negligible set, as the increasing union of Borel sets each contained in
\widetilde{\Omega}^{R_{n}}_{g}
, for some sequence
R_{n}\to+\infty
. This proves the claim.

Let us fix
R>0
, a Borel set
B\subset\widetilde{\Omega}^{R}_{g}
, and a sequence
\{R_{N}\}_{N}
with
R_{N}\to+\infty
and
R_{N}>R
. Thanks to Lemma
6.5
, we have

(6.49)

\lim_{N\to+\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(I^{p}_{\star})^{c}(v^{i}_{N},0,B)=(I^{p}_{\star})^{c}(g,0,B),

where we have set
v^{i}_{N}\coloneq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Phi_{\tau^{i-1}R_{N}\circ g}
. Since
v^{i}_{N}\in{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,N\}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
the integral representation (
6.45
) in
{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
can be applied, and thus

(6.50)

(I^{p}_{\star})^{c}(v^{i}_{N},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B)=\int_{B}H_{p}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{{\rm d}D^{c}v^{i}_{N}}{{\rm d}|D^{c}v^{i}_{N}|},0\Big)\,d|D^{c}v^{i}_{N}|

for every
i\in\{1,\dots,N\}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
. As we assumed that
B\subset\widetilde{\Omega}^{R}_{g}
and
R_{N}>R
, we have
B\subset\widetilde{\Omega}^{R_{N}}_{g}
. Thus, from (ii) of Proposition
A.6
we get

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}D^{c}v^{i}_{N}}{{\rm d}|D^{c}v^{i}_{N}|}=\frac{{\rm d}D^{c}g}{{\rm d}|D^{c}g|}\quad|D^{c}g|\text{-a.e. in }B,

\displaystyle\displaystyle|D^{c}v^{i}_{N}|=|D^{c}g|\quad\text{as Borel measures on $B$},

for every
i\in\{1,\dots,N\}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
. Together with (
6.46
), (
6.49
), and (
6.50
), these equalities give (
6.45
). This concludes the proof.

4.1
and of Remark
4.2
in the context of a different research project.
