Appendix A
Generalised functions of bounded variation

In the sequel,
\Omega
stands for an open, bounded subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We do not assume any regularity on
\Omega
unless specifically stated.

In this section, we collect some further properties of generalised functions of bounded variation. Before doing so, we recall the definition of the approximate gradient and of the jump set of an
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-measurable function.

Approximate gradient.

Let
E
be an
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-measurable subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and let
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a point with positive
d
-dimensional density, i.e.,

(A.1)

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathcal{L}^{d}(E\cap B_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{d}}>0.

A function
u\in L^{0}(E;\mathbb{R}^{k})
admits an
approximate limit

\widetilde{u}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
at
x

if, for every
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{y\in E\colon\,|u(y)-\widetilde{u}(x)|>\varepsilon\}\cap B_{\rho}(x))}{\rho^{d}}=0;

in this case, we write

(A.2)

\text{ap}\!\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\to x\\
y\in E\end{subarray}}u(y)=\widetilde{u}(x).

Throughout the paper, the symbol
\widetilde{u}(x)
always denotes the approximate limit of
u
defined by (
A.2
), which, thanks to (
A.1
), is uniquely defined. We also introduce the set
S_{u}
of
singular points
of
u
as the complement in
E
of the set of points where the approximate limit exists. We recall that for every
u\in L^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{k})
it holds
\mathcal{L}^{d}(S_{u})=0
.

If
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
and
u\in L^{0}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, a function
\nabla u\in L^{0}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
is said to be the approximate gradient of
u
if, for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e.
x\in O\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, it holds

(A.3)

{\rm ap}\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\to x\\
y\in O\end{subarray}}\frac{u(y)-\widetilde{u}(x)-\nabla u(x)(y-x)}{|y-x|}=0.

Jump set.

We recall the definition of jump set of a measurable function. Let
O\in\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
u\in L^{0}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
. The
jump set

J_{u}
is the Borel set consisting of all points
x\in O
for which there exists a triple
(u^{+}(x),u^{-}(x),\nu_{u}(x))\in\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, with
u^{+}(x)\neq u^{-}(x)
, such that

(A.4)

\displaystyle{\rm ap}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\to x\\
y\in H^{\pm}(x)\cap O\end{subarray}}u(y)=u^{\pm}(x),

where we have set
H^{\pm}(x)\coloneq\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\pm(y-x)\cdot\nu_{u}(x)>0\}
. Note that the triple
(u^{+}(x),u^{-}(x),\nu_{u}(x))
is uniquely defined up to swapping the roles of
u^{+}(x)
and
u^{-}(x)
and changing the sign of
\nu_{u}(x)
. For brevity of notation, we set
[u](x)\coloneq u^{+}(x)-u^{-}(x)
for all
x\in J_{u}
.

We fix a smooth map
\Phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{k};\mathbb{R}^{k})
satisfying the following conditions (see, for an example,
[
19
, Section 4]
):

(A.5)

\begin{cases}\Phi(y)=y\quad\text{for }y\in\{|y|\leq 1\},\\
\Phi(y)=0\quad\text{for }y\in\{|y|\geq\tau\},\\
{\rm Lip}(\Phi)\leq 1,\end{cases}

for a fixed constant
\tau>2
. (In our work, we choose
\tau\geq\max\{3,\beta_{\gamma}+1\}
, see before Lemma
6.2
.) For every
R>0
we set

(A.6)

\Phi_{R}(y)\coloneq R\Phi\big(\frac{y}{R}\big)\quad\text{for all $y\in\mathbb{R}^{k}$},

that satisfies

(A.7)

\begin{cases}\Phi_{R}(y)=y\quad\text{for }y\in\{|y|\leq R\},\\
\Phi_{R}(y)=0\quad\text{for }y\in\{|y|\geq\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R\},\\
{\rm Lip}(\Phi_{R})\leq 1.\end{cases}

The following proposition collects the main properties of functions in
{\rm GBV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
. For this, observe that
\Phi_{R}\circ u
lies in
{\rm BV}_{\rm loc}
and thus admits the usual representation of the distributional derivative
D(\Phi_{R}\circ u)
.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
u\in{\rm GBV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
. The following hold:

(a)

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(S_{u}\setminus J_{u})=0
;

(b)

u
admits an approximate gradient
\nabla u\in L^{0}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
;
moreover,
\nabla u(x)=\nabla(\Phi_{R}\circ u)(x)
for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e.
x\in\{|u|\leq R\}
;

(c)

for every
0<R_{1}<R_{2}
we have
J_{\Phi_{{R_{1}}\circ u}}\subset J_{\Phi_{{R_{2}}\circ u}}
up to a set of
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-measure zero and

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(J_{u}\setminus\bigcup\nolimits_{R>0}J_{\Phi_{R}\circ u}\big)=0;

moreover, for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
x\in J_{u}
we have
(\Phi_{R}\circ u)^{+}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(x)=u^{+}(x)
and
(\Phi_{R}\circ u){}^{-}(x)=u^{-}(x)
, whenever
R>\max\{|u^{+}(x)|,|u^{-}(x)|\}
.

Moreover, if
u\in{\rm GBV}(\Omega)^{k}
, then

(d)

there exists a measure
|D^{c}u|\in\mathcal{M}^{+}(\Omega)
, possibly unbounded, such that
|D^{c}u|(B)=|D^{c}(\Phi_{R}\circ u)|(B)
for every Borel set
B\subset\{|\widetilde{u}|\leq R\}
and
R>0
, and
|D^{c}u|(B)=0
whenever
B\subset\Omega
is a Borel set
\sigma
-finite with respect to
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
; in addition, we have

|D^{c}u|(B)=\lim_{R\to+\infty}|D^{c}(\Phi_{R}\circ u)|(B)=\sup_{R>0}|D^{c}(\Phi_{R}\circ u)|(B)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in[0,\infty]

for all Borel sets
B\subset\Omega
.

Remark A.2
.

If
k=1
, the space
{\rm GBV}(\Omega)
may also be characterised by a different type of truncation. Indeed, it can be shown (see, for instance,
[
48
]
) that
{\rm GBV}(\Omega)
is the space of all

u\in L^{0}(\Omega)
such that
u^{(n)}\in{\rm BV}_{\rm loc}(\Omega)
for all
n>0
, where we have set
u^{(n)}\coloneq(u\lor(-n))\land n
. Moreover, properties (b) and (d) of Proposition
A.1
can be strengthened in this case. Indeed, given
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
with
n_{1}<n_{2}
, we also have

\displaystyle|\nabla u^{(n_{1})}|\leq|\nabla u^{(n_{2})}|\quad\text{ $\mathcal{L}^{d}$-a.e.\ in $\Omega$ }\quad\text{ and }\quad|D^{c}u^{(n_{1})}|(B)\leq|D^{c}u^{(n_{2})}|(B),

\displaystyle J_{u^{(n_{1})}}\subset J_{u^{(n_{2})}}\quad\text{ and }\quad J^{1}_{u^{(n_{1})}}\subset J^{1}_{u^{(n_{2})}}\quad\text{up to $\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-negligible sets},

\displaystyle\quad|[u^{(n_{1})}]|\leq|[u^{(n_{2})}]|\quad\text{$\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-a.e.\ on $J_{u^{(n_{1})}}$}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},

for all Borel sets
B\subset\Omega
.

The following proposition, see
[
39
, Proposition 3.9 and its proof]
, characterises
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
by means of the truncation functions
\Phi_{R}
.

Proposition A.3
.

Let
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
and let
u\in L^{0}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
. Then
u
belongs to
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
if and only if
\Phi_{R}\circ u\in{\rm BV}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
for every
R>0
and

S\coloneq\sup_{R>0}\mathcal{V}(\Phi_{R}\circ u,O)<+\infty.

In this case,
\mathcal{V}(u,O)=S
.

Remark A.4
.

The space
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
can also be characterised by truncations as those considered in Remark
A.2
. More precisely, a function
u\in L^{0}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
belongs to
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0};\mathbb{R}^{k})
if and only if
u^{(m)}\in{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
for all
m>0
and

\sup_{m>0}\mathcal{V}(u^{(m)},\Omega)<+\infty,

where we have set
u^{(m)}\coloneq\big((u_{1}\lor(-m))\land m,\dots,(u_{k}\lor(-m))\land m\big)
.

Moreover, given
a,b\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
with
a_{i}\leq b_{i}
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
and
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, we have
v\coloneq(u\lor a)\land b\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
,
\mathcal{V}(v,O)\leq\mathcal{V}(u,O)
for all
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
, as well as
|D^{c}v|(O)\leq|D^{c}u|(O)
,

\displaystyle|Dv|(O\setminus J^{r}_{u})\leq(r\vee 1)\mathcal{V}(u,O\setminus J^{r}_{u})\quad\text{ and }|Dv|(J^{r}_{u}\cap O)\leq k|b-a|\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u}\cap O),

and
J_{v}^{r}\subset J^{r}_{u}
, up to an
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-negligible set, for all
r\geq 0
.

It is possible to associate to every function
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
a
matrix-valued
measure
D^{c}u\in\mathcal{M}_{b}(O\color[rgb]{0,0,0};\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
that generalises the Cantor part of the distributional derivative for functions of bounded variation. Note that its total variation measure coincides with the measure introduced in (d) of Proposition
A.1
. For a proof of this result, we refer the reader to
[
2
, Lemma 2.10]
and to
[
32
, Theorem 2.7 and Proposition 2.9]
.

Proposition A.5
.

Let
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
. Then there exists a measure
D^{c}u\in\mathcal{M}_{b}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k\times d})
such that
D^{c}u(B)=D^{c}(\Phi_{R}\circ u)(B)
for every Borel set
B\subset\{|\widetilde{u}|\leq R\}
, and
D^{c}u(B)=0
whenever
B\subset O
is a Borel set
\sigma
-finite with respect to
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
; in addition, we have

\displaystyle D^{c}u(B)=\lim_{R\to+\infty}D^{c}(\Phi_{R}\circ u)(B)\quad\text{ for all Borel sets }B\subset O,

\displaystyle|D^{c}u|(B)=\lim_{R\to+\infty}|D^{c}(\Phi_{R}\circ u)|(B)=\sup_{R>0}|D^{c}(\Phi_{R}\circ u)|(B)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in[0,\infty)\quad\text{ for all Borel sets }B\subset O.

We also state a result concerning the Cantor part of the composition of
{\rm GBV}_{\star}
-functions with smooth functions, whose proof can be found in
[
39
, Proposition 3.13]
.

Proposition A.6
.

Let
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
R>0
. Then,

(i)

D^{c}(\Phi_{R}\circ u)=\nabla\Phi_{R}(\widetilde{u})D^{c}u
as Radon measures on
\Omega
;

(ii)

we have

\frac{{\rm d}D^{c}(\Phi_{R}\circ u)}{{\rm d}|D^{c}(\Phi_{R}\circ u)|}=\frac{{\rm d}D^{c}u}{{\rm d}|D^{c}u|}\quad\text{$|D^{c}u|$-a.e.\ in $\ \widetilde{\Omega}^{R}_{u}$},

where
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}^{R}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\coloneq\{x\in\Omega\colon\widetilde{u}(x)\text{ exists and }|\widetilde{u}(x)|\leq R\}
.
As a consequence, we have

\lim_{R\to+\infty}\frac{{\rm d}D^{c}(\Phi_{R}\circ u)}{{\rm d}|D^{c}(\Phi_{R}\circ u)|}=\frac{{\rm d}D^{c}u}{{\rm d}|D^{c}u|}\quad\text{ $|D^{c}u|$-a.e.\ in $\Omega$}.

If
\Omega
has Lipschitz boundary and
u\in{\rm GBV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, we let
u_{\partial\Omega}
denote the trace of
u
on
\partial\Omega
, which is defined for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
x\in\partial\Omega
in the sense of approximate limits. We conclude the section by proving that it is always possible to glue functions in
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
across a Lipschitz boundary.

Lemma A.7
.

Let
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
with
O\subset\subset\Omega
and with Lipschitz boundary. Let
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(O;\mathbb{R}^{k})
,
v\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega\setminus\overline{O};\mathbb{R}^{k})
, and consider the function defined for
x\in\Omega
by

w(x)\coloneq\begin{cases}u(x)&\text{if $x\in O$},\\
v(x)&\text{if $x\in\Omega\setminus\overline{O}$}.\end{cases}

Then
w\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
. Moreover,
|[w]|\coloneq|u_{\partial O}-v_{\partial O}|

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e. on
\partial O
.

Proof.

It is immediate to check that
w\in{\rm GBV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and that
\mathcal{V}(w,\Omega\setminus\overline{O})+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}(w,O)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}<+\infty
. Since

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{J_{w}\cap\partial O}|[w]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial O)<+\infty,

we get
\mathcal{V}(w,\Omega)<+\infty
, and hence
w\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
.
∎

Appendix B
Slicing properties of generalised functions of bounded variation

In this section, we present some new results concerning fine properties of
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
-functions and use
these properties to provide a crucial result that is needed in the proof of the Fundamental Estimate of Lemma
5.1
(see Lemma
B.5
). In particular, we show that the space
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
can be characterised by means of one-dimensional slicing. This characterisation shows that the space
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
is the analogue to Dal Maso’s
{\rm GBD}(\Omega)
(see
[
29
]
) in the context of functions of bounded variation.

In order to present the characterisation mentioned above,
we set some notation and recall some properties of one-dimensional slices of
{\rm GBV}
-functions. Given
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we denote the hyperplane orthogonal to
\xi
and passing through the origin by
\Pi^{\xi}\coloneq\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\colon\,y\cdot\xi=0\}
, and let
\pi^{\xi}\colon\Omega\to\Pi^{\xi}
be the orthogonal projection onto
\Pi^{\xi}
. Given a set
E\subset\Omega
, its one-dimensional slice in direction
\xi
with base point
y\in\Pi^{\xi}
is defined as
E^{\xi}_{y}\coloneq\{t\in\mathbb{R}\colon\,y+t\xi\in E\}.
For
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
u\in L^{0}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
,
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and
y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)
, we let
u^{\xi}_{y}\colon\Omega^{\xi}_{y}\to\mathbb{R}^{k}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
be the function defined by
u^{\xi}_{y}(t)\coloneq u(y+t\xi)
for
t\in\Omega^{\xi}_{y}
.
We recall that, if
u\in{\rm GBV}(\Omega)^{k}
, then for every
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)
we have
u^{\xi}_{y}\in{\rm GBV}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})^{k}
and (recall (
2.5
))

(B.1)

\displaystyle\nabla u^{\xi}_{y}=\nabla u(y+t\xi)\xi\quad\text{$\mathcal{L}^{1}$-a.e.\ in $\Omega^{\xi}_{y}$,}

(B.2)

\displaystyle J_{u^{\xi}_{y}}=(J_{u})^{\xi}_{y}\quad\text{ and }\quad J^{r}_{u^{\xi}_{y}}=(J^{r}_{u})^{\xi}_{y}\quad\text{ for all $r\geq 0$,}

(B.3)

\displaystyle(u^{\pm})^{\xi}_{y}(t)=(u^{\xi}_{y})^{\pm}(t)\quad\text{ for all $t\in(J_{u})^{\xi}_{y}=J_{u^{\xi}_{y}}$},

where the normals at
J_{u}
and at
J_{u^{\xi}_{y}}
are oriented in such a way that
\nu_{u}\cdot\xi\geq 0
and
\nu_{u^{\xi}_{y}}=1
. For a proof of these properties, we refer the reader to
[
5
, Proposition 4.35]
.

The following lemma shows that, for a function
u\in{\rm GBV}(\Omega)
, it is always possible to bound the integral of
\mathcal{V}(u^{\xi}_{y},\Omega^{\xi}_{y})
with respect to
y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)
from above by
\mathcal{V}(u,\Omega)
.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
u\in{\rm GBV}(\Omega)
and
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. Then

(B.4)

\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\big(|Du^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y}\setminus J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}})+\mathcal{H}^{0}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y}\cap J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}})\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\leq\mathcal{V}(u,\Omega).

Moreover, if
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega)
, we have

(B.5)

\displaystyle\int_{B}(\nabla u)\xi\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\pi^{\xi}(B)}\int_{B^{\xi}_{y}}\nabla u^{\xi}_{y}(t)\,{\rm d}t\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y),

(B.6)

\displaystyle D^{c}u(B)\xi

\displaystyle=\int_{\pi^{\xi}(B)}D^{c}u^{\xi}_{y}(B^{\xi}_{y})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y),

(B.7)

\displaystyle\int_{B\cap(J_{u}\setminus J^{1}_{u})}[u](\nu_{u}\cdot\xi)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\pi^{\xi}(B)}Du^{\xi}_{y}\big((B\cap(J_{u}\setminus J^{1}_{u}))^{\xi}_{y}\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)

for every Borel set
B\subset\Omega
and
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.

Proof.

If
u\notin{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega)
by the definition (
2.2
) of
\mathcal{V}
we have
\mathcal{V}(u,\Omega)=+\infty
, so that we may assume that
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega)
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and consider the function
u^{(m)}\in{\rm BV}(\Omega)
(see Remark
A.2
). By the slicing properties of functions of bounded variation (see
[
5
, Theorems 3.107 and 3.108]
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u^{(m)}|\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\Big(\int_{\Omega^{\xi}_{y}}|\nabla(u^{(m)})^{\xi}_{y}|\,{\rm d}t\Big){\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y),

\displaystyle|D^{c}u^{(m)}|(\Omega)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}|D^{c}(u^{(m)})^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y),

which, thanks to Remark
A.2
, yields

(B.8)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\Big(\int_{\Omega^{\xi}_{y}}|\nabla(u^{(m)})^{\xi}_{y}|\,{\rm d}t\Big){\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y),

(B.9)

\displaystyle|D^{c}u|(\Omega)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}|D^{c}(u^{(m)})^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y).

As
u\in{\rm GBV}(\Omega)
, for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)
we have
u^{\xi}_{y}\in{\rm GBV}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})
. Thus, applying Remark
A.2
to the slices
u^{\xi}_{y}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\xi}_{y}}|\nabla(u^{(m)})^{\xi}_{y}|\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\to\int_{\Omega^{\xi}_{y}}|\nabla u^{\xi}_{y}|\,{\rm d}t\quad\text{ for $\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-a.e.\ $y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)$ as $m\to+\infty$},

\displaystyle|D^{c}(u^{(m)})^{\xi}_{y}|(B^{\xi}_{y})

\displaystyle\to|D^{c}u^{\xi}_{y}|(B^{\xi}_{y})\quad\text{ for \color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-a.e.\ $y\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)$ as $m\to+\infty$},

with both convergence being monotone. Therefore, by the Monotone Convergence Theorem and (
B.8
)–(
B.9
) we infer

(B.10)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\Big(\int_{\Omega^{\xi}_{y}}|\nabla u^{\xi}_{y}|\,{\rm d}t\Big){\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y),

(B.11)

\displaystyle|D^{c}u|(\Omega)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}|D^{c}u^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y).

As for the jump part, we use the Area Formula (see
[
5
, (2.47)]
), (
B.3
), and (
B.2
) to get

\displaystyle\int_{J_{u^{(m)}}}|[u^{(m)}]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\Big(\int_{J_{(u^{(m)})^{\xi}_{y}}}|[(u^{(m)})^{\xi}_{y}](t)|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{0}(t)\Big){\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\Big(|D^{j}(u^{(m)})^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y}\setminus J^{1}_{(u^{(m)})^{\xi}_{y}})+\mathcal{H}^{0}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y}\cap J^{1}_{(u^{(m)})^{\xi}_{y}})\Big){\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y).

Using again Remark
A.2
and the Monotone Convergence Theorem, we obtain

(B.12)

\int_{J_{u}}|[u]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\geq\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\Big(|D^{j}u^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y}\setminus J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}})+\mathcal{H}^{0}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y}\cap J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}})\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y).

Putting together (
B.10
)–(
B.12
), we obtain (
B.4
).

To prove (
B.5
)–(
B.7
), we use
[
5
, Theorems 3.107 and 3.108]
to get

(B.13)

\displaystyle\int_{B}(\nabla u^{(m)})\xi\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\pi^{\xi}(B)}\Big(\int_{B^{\xi}_{y}}\nabla(u^{(m)})^{\xi}_{y}(t)\,{\rm d}t\Big){\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y),

(B.14)

\displaystyle D^{c}u^{(m)}(B)\xi

\displaystyle=\int_{\pi^{\xi}(B)}D^{c}(u^{(m)})^{\xi}_{y}(B^{\xi}_{y})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y).

Thanks to (
B.4
), for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)
the function
u^{\xi}_{y}
belongs to
{\rm BV}_{\rm loc}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})
, so that

\displaystyle\int_{B^{\xi}_{y}}\nabla(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi}_{y}(t)\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\to\int_{B^{\xi}_{y}}\nabla u^{\xi}_{y}(t)\,{\rm d}t\quad\text{ for $\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-a.e.\ $y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)$ as $m\to+\infty$},

\displaystyle D^{c}(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi}_{y}(B^{\xi}_{y})

\displaystyle\to D^{c}u^{\xi}_{y}(B^{\xi}_{y})\quad\text{for $\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-a.e.\ $y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)$ as $m\to+\infty$.}

Thus, we may let
m\to+\infty
in (
B.13
) and (
B.14
). Then, by Propositions
A.1
and
A.5
, and the Dominated Convergence Theorem we obtain (
B.5
) and (
B.6
).

As for (
B.7
), it is enough to apply the Area Formula and to use (
B.2
) and (
B.3
).
∎

The following proposition characterises
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
by means of one-dimensional slicing.

Proposition B.2
.

Let
u\in L^{0}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
. Then
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
if and only if for every
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
the following two conditions hold:

(B.15)

\displaystyle u^{\xi}_{y}\in{\rm BV}_{\rm loc}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y};\mathbb{R}^{k})\quad\text{ for $\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-a.e.\ $y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)$},

(B.16)

\displaystyle\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\big(|Du^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y}\setminus J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}})+\mathcal{H}^{0}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y}\cap J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}})\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)<+\infty.

Proof.

It is enough to prove the statement in the case
k=1
.

The fact that
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega)
implies (
B.15
) and (
B.16
) is a simple consequence of Lemma
B.1
and the fact that in dimension
1
the space
{\rm GBV}_{\star}
is contained in
{\rm BV}_{\rm loc}
(see
[
32
, Remark 3.2]
).

We now prove that (
B.15
) and (
B.16
) imply
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega)
.
To this end, let us fix
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. By (
B.15
), there exists a Borel set
N^{1}_{\xi}\subset\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)
, with
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(N^{1}_{\xi})=0
, such that
u^{\xi}_{y}\in{\rm BV}_{\rm loc}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})
for all
y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)\setminus N^{1}_{\xi}
. Let us fix
m>0
. For every
y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)\setminus N^{1}_{\xi}
, by the Chain Rule for functions of bounded variation (see
[
5
, Theorem 3.99]
) we have
(u^{(m)})^{\xi}_{y}\in{\rm BV}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})
and

(B.17)

D(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi}_{y}=\chi_{\{|\widetilde{u}^{\xi}_{y}|\leq m\}}(D^{a}u^{\xi}_{y}+D^{c}u^{\xi}_{y})+\big(((u^{\xi}_{y})^{+})^{(m)}-((u^{\xi}_{y})^{-})^{(m)}\big)\mathcal{H}^{0}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}J_{u^{\xi}_{y}}

as Borel measures on
\Omega^{\xi}_{y}
.
In particular,

|D(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})\leq|D^{a}u^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})+|D^{c}u^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})+|D^{j}u^{\xi}_{y}|(J_{u^{\xi}_{y}}\setminus J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+2m\mathcal{H}^{0}(J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

for every
y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)\setminus N^{1}_{\xi}
.
Combining the previous inequality with (
B.16
),
we obtain

\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}|D(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)<+\infty.

By the arbitrariness of
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
[
5
, Remark 3.104]
, this implies that
u^{(m)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in{\rm BV}(\Omega)
, whence
u\in{\rm GBV}(\Omega)
.

To conclude that
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega)
, it is then enough to show (see Remark
A.4
) that

(B.18)

\displaystyle\int_{J_{u^{(m)}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}|[u^{(m)}]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\leq C_{1},

(B.19)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|(\nabla u^{(m)})e_{i}|\,{\rm d}x+|(D^{c}(u^{(m)})e_{i}|(\Omega)\leq C_{2}\quad\text{ for all $i\in\{1,\dots,d\}$},

for constants
C_{1}
,
C_{2}>0
independent of
m
.

Let
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, let
N^{1}_{\xi}\subset\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)
be as above, and let
N^{2}_{\xi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)
be a Borel set, with
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(N_{\xi}^{2})=0
, such that (
B.2
) and (
B.3
) hold for every
y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)\setminus N^{2}_{\xi}
. Resorting to (
B.17
), we get

\displaystyle J_{(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi}_{y}}\subset J_{u^{\xi}_{y}}=(J_{u})^{\xi}_{y}\quad\text{ and }J^{1}_{(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi}_{y}}\subset J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}}=(J^{1}_{u})^{\xi}_{y},

and

\displaystyle|D(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi}_{y}|\big(B^{\xi}_{y}\setminus J^{1}_{(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi}_{y}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq|D(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi}_{y}|\big(B^{\xi}_{y}\setminus J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}}\big)+\mathcal{H}^{0}\big(B^{\xi}_{y}\cap(J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}}\setminus J^{1}_{(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi}_{y}})\big)

(B.20)

\displaystyle\leq|Du^{\xi}_{y}|\big(B^{\xi}_{y}\setminus J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}}\big)+\mathcal{H}^{0}\big(B^{\xi}_{y}\cap(J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}}\setminus J^{1}_{(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi}_{y}})\big)

for all Borel sets
B\subset\Omega
and for every
y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)\setminus(N^{1}_{\xi}\cup N^{2}_{\xi})
.

We now prove (
B.18
).
We consider a finite collection of vectors
\{\xi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}\subset\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with the property that the sets
S_{j}\coloneq\{\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\colon\,|\xi-\xi_{j}|\leq 1/2\}
cover
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. We set
B_{j}\coloneq\{x\in J_{u}\colon\,\nu_{u}(x)\in S_{j}\}
and note that
J_{u}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{N}B_{j}
. Note also that, if
\xi\in S_{j}
for some
j\in\{1,\dots,N\}
, then
\xi\cdot\xi_{j}=1-|\xi-\xi_{j}|^{2}/2\geq 7/8
, so that

(B.21)

\nu_{u}(x)\cdot\xi_{j}\geq 7/8\quad\text{ for all $x\in B_{j}$.}

By the Area Formula (see
[
5
, (2.47)]
), (
B.3
), (
B.20
), and (
B.21
), for all
j\in\{1,\dots,N\}
we have

\displaystyle\frac{7}{8}\int_{B_{j}}|[u^{(m)}]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{j}}(|[u^{(m)}]|\land 1)(\nu_{u}\cdot\xi_{j})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\pi^{\xi_{j}}(B_{j})}\Big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\int_{(B_{j})^{\xi_{j}}_{y}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|[(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi_{j}}_{y}](t)|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{0}(t)\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\pi^{\xi_{j}}(B_{j})}\Big(|D(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi_{j}}_{y}|\big((B_{j})^{\xi_{j}}_{y}\setminus J^{1}_{(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi_{j}}_{y}}\big)+\mathcal{H}^{0}\big((B_{j})^{\xi_{j}}_{y}\cap J^{1}_{(u^{(m)}){}^{\xi_{j}}_{y}}\big)\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\pi^{\xi_{j}}(B_{j})}\Big(|Du^{\xi_{j}}_{y}|\big((B_{j})^{\xi_{j}}_{y}\setminus J^{1}_{u^{\xi_{j}}_{y}}\big)+\mathcal{H}^{0}\big((B_{j})^{\xi_{j}}_{y}\cap J^{1}_{u^{\xi_{j}}_{y}}\big)\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y).

Summing over
j\in\{1,\dots,N\}
the previous inequality, for all
y\in\pi^{\xi_{j}}(\Omega)\setminus(N^{1}_{\xi_{j}}\cup N^{2}_{\xi_{j}})
we find by the Area Formula

\int_{J_{u}}|[u^{(m)}]|\land 1\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\leq\frac{8}{7}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\int_{\pi^{\xi_{j}}(B_{j})}\Big(|Du^{\xi_{j}}_{y}|\big((\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{j}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})^{\xi_{j}}_{y}\setminus J^{1}_{u^{\xi_{j}}_{y}}\big)+\mathcal{H}^{0}\big((\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B_{j}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})^{\xi_{j}}_{y}\cap J^{1}_{u^{\xi_{j}}_{y}}\big)\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)=:C_{1}.

From (
B.16
) it follows
C_{1}<+\infty
, which proves (
B.18
).

To prove (
B.19
), we use the slicing properties of functions of bounded variation (see
[
5
, Theorem 3.108]
) to get

\int_{\Omega}|(\nabla u^{(m)})e_{i}|\,{\rm d}x+|(D^{c}u^{(m)})e_{i}|(\Omega)=\int_{\pi^{e_{i}}(\Omega)}\Big(\int_{\Omega^{e_{i}}_{y}}|\nabla(u^{(m)})^{e_{i}}_{y}|\,{\rm d}t+|D^{c}(u^{(m)})^{e_{i}}_{y}|(\Omega^{e_{i}}_{y})\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y).

In view of (
B.16
), of (
B.20
) applied with
B=\Omega\setminus J_{u}
, and of (
B.2
), the previous equality proves (
B.19
) with

C_{2}\coloneq\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\pi^{e_{i}}(\Omega)}\Big(\int_{\Omega^{e_{i}}_{y}}|\nabla u^{e_{i}}_{y}|\,{\rm d}t+|D^{c}u^{e_{i}}_{y}|(\Omega^{e_{i}}_{y})\Big){\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y).

This proves that
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega)
and concludes the proof.
∎

Remark B.3
.

If
\Omega
has Lipschitz boundary and
u\in{\rm GBV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, then we may replace
{\rm BV}_{\rm loc}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y};\mathbb{R}^{k})
in (
B.15
) by
{\rm BV}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y};\mathbb{R}^{k})
. Indeed, let
y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)
be such that

(B.22)

\displaystyle u^{\xi}_{y}\in{\rm BV}_{\rm loc}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y};\mathbb{R}^{k}),\ \ \mathcal{H}^{0}(\partial\Omega^{\xi}_{y})<+\infty,\ \ \text{and}\ \ |Du^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y}\setminus J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}})+\mathcal{H}^{0}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y}\cap J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}})<+\infty.

Since
\mathcal{H}^{0}(J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}})<+\infty
, it follows immediately that
|Du^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})<+\infty
. Condition
\mathcal{H}^{0}(\partial\Omega^{\xi}_{y})<+\infty
implies that
\Omega^{\xi}_{y}
is a finite union of open intervals, so that by the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus for one-dimensional BV functions we also get
u^{\xi}_{y}\in L^{1}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y};\mathbb{R}^{k})
. As the conditions in (
B.22
) are satisfied by
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)
, the claim is proved.

We now show that the measure
D^{c}u
enjoys some useful disintegration properties.

Lemma B.4
.

Let
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, let
\varphi\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
be a bounded Borel function, and let
B\subset\Omega
be a Borel set. Then for every
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
we have

(B.23)

\Big(\int_{B}\varphi\,{\rm d}D^{c}u\Big)\xi=\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\Big(\int_{B^{\xi}_{y}}\varphi^{\xi}_{y}\,{\rm d}D^{c}u^{\xi}_{y}\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y).

Proof.

The case
\varphi=\chi_{E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
for some Borel set
E\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is exactly (
B.6
). This result is clearly extended to simple bounded Borel functions by additivity. The general case can be obtained by approximating each bounded Borel function by a sequence of uniformly bounded simple Borel functions, taking into account (
B.16
), in order to be able to use the Dominated Convergence Theorem on the right-hand side of (
B.23
).
∎

The following result, which will be used in Section
5
, shows that the product of a smooth function
\varphi
with a function
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
still belongs to
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, provided that
u
satisfies some additional integrability conditions.

Lemma B.5
.

Suppose that
\Omega
has Lipschitz boundary. Let
\varphi\in C^{1}_{c}(\overline{\Omega};[0,1])
and let
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
.
Assume that
u\in L^{1}(K\color[rgb]{0,0,0};\mathbb{R}^{k})
, where
K\coloneq\{y\in\Omega\colon\,0<\varphi<1\}
.
Then
v\coloneq\varphi u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})

and

(B.24)

\displaystyle\nabla v=u\otimes\nabla\varphi+\varphi\nabla u\quad\text{ $\mathcal{L}^{d}$-a.e.\ in $\Omega$},

(B.25)

\displaystyle D^{c}v=\varphi D^{c}u\quad\text{ as Borel measures on $\Omega$},

(B.26)

\displaystyle J_{v}\subset J_{u}\quad\text{up to \color[rgb]{0,0,0}an \color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-negligible set }\text{ and }[v](x)=\varphi(x)[u](x)\quad\text{ for $\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-a.e.\ $x\in J_{v}$.}

Proof.

Let us fix
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. In view of Remark
B.3
, we have
u^{\xi}_{y}\in{\rm BV}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})
for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
y\in\ \pi^{\xi}(\Omega)
. By the Leibniz rule for one-dimensional
{\rm BV}
-functions we then have that
v^{\xi}_{y}\in{\rm BV}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})
and

(B.27)

\displaystyle\nabla v^{\xi}_{y}=\varphi^{\xi}_{y}\nabla u^{\xi}_{y}+(\nabla\varphi^{\xi}_{y})u^{\xi}_{y}\quad\text{ $\mathcal{L}^{1}$-a.e.\ in $\Omega^{\xi}_{y}$},

(B.28)

\displaystyle D^{c}v^{\xi}_{y}=\varphi^{\xi}_{y}D^{c}u^{\xi}_{y}\quad\text{ as Borel measures on $\Omega^{\xi}_{y}$\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

(B.29)

\displaystyle J_{v^{\xi}_{y}}=J_{u^{\xi}_{y}}\cap\{t\in\Omega^{\xi}_{y}\colon\,\varphi^{\xi}_{y}(t)\neq 0\}\quad\text{ and }\quad(v^{\xi}_{y})^{\pm}=\varphi^{\xi}_{y}(u^{\xi}_{y})^{\pm}\quad\text{ on }J_{u^{\xi}_{y}}

for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)
.
In particular, from the previous equality and the fact that
0\leq\varphi\leq 1
, it follows that

|[v^{\xi}_{y}]|\leq|[u^{\xi}_{y}]|\quad\text{ on ${J}_{u^{\xi}_{y}}$}\quad\text{ and }J^{1}_{{v}^{\xi}_{y}}\subset J^{1}_{{u}^{\xi}_{y}}

for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
y\in\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)
. This inclusion then implies

(B.30)

\displaystyle\hskip-11.38092pt\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}|Dv^{\xi}_{y}|(J_{v^{\xi}_{y}}\setminus J^{1}_{v^{\xi}_{y}})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\leq\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\big(|Du^{\xi}_{y}|(J_{u^{\xi}_{y}}\setminus J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}})+\mathcal{H}^{0}(J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}}\setminus J^{1}_{v^{\xi}_{y}})\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)<+\infty,

(B.31)

\displaystyle\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\mathcal{H}^{0}(J^{1}_{v^{\xi}_{y}})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\leq\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\mathcal{H}^{0}(J^{1}_{u^{\xi}_{y}})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)<+\infty.

In view of Proposition
B.2
, we get that
u
satisfies (
B.16
). Then, by the fact that
u\in L^{1}(K;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and Fubini’s Theorem we find

(B.32)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\Big(\int_{\Omega^{\xi}_{y}}|\varphi^{\xi}_{y}\nabla u^{\xi}_{y}|\,{\rm d}t\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\leq\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\Big(\int_{\Omega^{\xi}_{y}}|\nabla u^{\xi}_{y}\,|{\rm d}t\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)<+\infty,

(B.33)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\!\!\!\Big(\int_{\Omega^{\xi}_{y}}|(\nabla\varphi^{\xi}_{y})u^{\xi}_{y}|\,{\rm d}t\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\leq\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\!\!\!\Big(\int_{K^{\xi}_{y}}|u^{\xi}_{y}|\,{\rm d}t\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)<+\infty,

(B.34)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\Big(\int_{\Omega^{\xi}_{y}}|\varphi^{\xi}_{y}|\,{\rm d}|D^{c}u^{\xi}_{y}|\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)\leq\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}|D^{c}u^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)<+\infty.

Recalling (
B.27
) and (
B.28
), combining (
B.30
)–(
B.34
), we conclude that

\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\big(|Dv^{\xi}_{y}|(\Omega^{\xi}_{y}\setminus J^{1}_{v^{\xi}_{y}})+\mathcal{H}^{0}(\Omega^{\xi}_{y}\cap J^{1}_{v^{\xi}_{y}})\big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)<+\infty.

As
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
was arbitrary, Proposition
B.2
ensures that
v\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})\cap L^{1}(K;\mathbb{R}^{k}).

Equation (
B.26
) follows immediately from (
B.3
) and (
B.29
).
To prove (
B.24
), it is enough to combine (
B.1
) and (
B.27
).
As for (
B.25
), we note that by (
B.6
) applied for
v
in place of
u
, (
B.28
), and Lemma
B.4
we have

D^{c}v(B)\xi=\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}D^{c}v^{\xi}_{y}(B^{\xi}_{y})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)=\int_{\pi^{\xi}(\Omega)}\Big(\int_{B^{\xi}_{y}}\varphi^{\xi}_{y}\,{\rm d}Du^{\xi}_{y}\Big)\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(y)=\Big(\int_{B}\varphi\,{\rm d}D^{c}u\Big)\xi

for every
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and every Borel set
B\subset\Omega
. Since the previous equality implies (
B.25
), the proof is complete.
∎

Appendix C
Poincaré inequality for functions with small jump set

This section is devoted to a Poincaré-type inequality for functions in
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
,
which will be a key tool for a series of results that we will present in Appendix
D
. It is also employed in the proof of Lemma
5.4
. The inequality is visibly
reminiscent of the one proved by De Giorgi, Carriero, & Leaci
[
36
, Theorem 3.1]
for functions in
{\rm SBV}(\Omega)
with small jump set. Indeed, our proof follows closely the argument they devised.

We begin by setting some notation. Throughout this section, we make the further assumption that
d\geq 2
and
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is connected with Lipschitz boundary.
Given
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega)
, we define

(C.1)

u_{\star}(s,\Omega)\coloneq\inf\big\{t\in[-\infty,+\infty]\colon\,\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{u<t\})\geq s\big\}

for all
s\in(0,\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega))
. We say that
m\in\mathbb{R}
is a
median
of
u
on
\Omega
if

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{u<t\})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega)/2\,\,\,\text{ for all $t<m$,}

(C.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{u>t\})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega)/2\,\,\,\text{for all $t>m$}.

It follows immediately from (
C.1
) that
u_{\star}(\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega)/2,\Omega)
is a median of
u
on
\Omega
.
In the vector-valued case
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
with
k>1
, we say that a vector
m\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
is a median of
u
on
\Omega
if
m_{i}
is a median of
u_{i}
on
\Omega
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
.
For future use, we report here the statement of the classical relative isoperimetric inequality (see, for instance,
[
5
, (3.43)]
). Recall (
2.1
).

Proposition C.1
.

There exists a constant
\gamma=\gamma(\Omega)>0
such that for every
\rho>0
, every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and every set
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
E_{\rho,x}\subset\Omega_{\rho,x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
having finite perimeter in
\Omega_{\rho,x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
it holds

\min\Big\{\mathcal{L}^{d}(E_{\rho,x}\cap\Omega_{\rho,x}),\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega_{\rho,x}\setminus E_{\rho,x})\Big\}\leq\big(\gamma\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(\partial^{*}E_{\rho,x}\cap\Omega_{\rho,x}\big)\big)^{d/(d-1)}.

Remark C.2
.

The isoperimetric constant
\gamma=\gamma(\Omega)
can be taken to be the same as the one in the Sobolev Embedding Theorem for functions of bounded variation. In particular, the following property holds: let
\mathcal{I}\subset\mathbb{R}
be a family of indices, and let
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\{\Omega_{i}\}{}_{i\in\mathcal{I}}
be a collection of domains with the property that for each
i_{1},i_{2}\in\mathcal{I}
there exists a bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism
\varphi_{i_{1},i_{2}}
from
\Omega_{i_{1}}
onto
\Omega_{i_{2}}
such that, denoting with
L_{i_{1},i_{2}}
the Lipschitz constant of
\varphi_{i_{1},i_{2}}
, it holds
\sup_{i_{1},i_{2}}L_{i_{1},i_{2}}<+\infty
. Then it is possible to choose a positive constant
\gamma=\gamma(\{\Omega_{i}\}_{i\in\mathcal{I}})
, independent of
i\in\mathcal{I}
, for which Proposition
C.1
holds true for every
i\in\mathcal{I}
.

In the following,
\gamma=\gamma(\Omega)>0
always denotes the constant of Proposition
C.1
. We will often assume that the function
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
under consideration satisfies

(C.3)

\big(2\gamma\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u}\cap\Omega)\big)^{d/(d-1)}\leq\frac{\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega)}{2}

for some
r\geq 0
. When (
C.3
) is satisfied, we define the vectors
\tau^{\prime}(u,\Omega,r),\tau^{\prime\prime}(u,\Omega,r)\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
by setting

(C.4)

\displaystyle\tau^{\prime}_{i}(u,\Omega,r)

\displaystyle\coloneq(u_{i})_{\star}\big((2\gamma\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u_{i}}\cap\Omega))^{d/(d-1)},\Omega\big),

\displaystyle\tau^{\prime\prime}_{i}(u,\Omega,r)

\displaystyle\coloneq(u_{i})_{\star}\big(\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega)-(2\gamma\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u_{i}}\cap\Omega))^{d/(d-1)},\Omega\big)

for all
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
. For brevity of notation, if
r=0
, we write
\tau^{\prime}(u,\Omega)
and
\tau^{\prime\prime}(u,\Omega)
in place of
\tau^{\prime}(u,\Omega,r)
and
\tau^{\prime\prime}(u,\Omega,r)
. Observe that, if
m_{i}
is a median of
u_{i}
, then it holds

(C.5)

\tau^{\prime}_{i}(u,\Omega,r)\leq m_{i}\leq\tau^{\prime\prime}_{i}(u,\Omega,r).

We now present a Poincaré inequality for functions in
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
with a small set of large jumps, whose lines of proof follow closely those of
[
36
, Theorem 3.1]
(see also
[
5
, Theorem 4.14]
).

Theorem C.3
.

There exists a constant
C_{\rm Poin}=C_{\rm Poin}(\Omega,k)>0
such that, for every
r\geq 0
and
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
satisfying (
C.3
), denoting by
\overline{u}_{r}\in{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
the function
\overline{u}_{r}\coloneq\big(u\lor\tau^{\prime}(u,\Omega,r)\big)\land\tau^{\prime\prime}(u,\Omega,r)
, it holds

(C.6)

\displaystyle|D\overline{u}_{r}|(\Omega)

\displaystyle\leq 2(r\vee 1)\mathcal{V}(u,\Omega\setminus J^{r}_{u}),

(C.7)

\displaystyle\|\overline{u}_{r}-m\|_{L^{d/(d-1)}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\rm Poin}(r\vee 1)\mathcal{V}(u,\Omega\setminus J^{r}_{u}),

for any median
m\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
of
u
on
\Omega
. Moreover, we have

(C.8)

\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{\overline{u}_{r}\neq u\})\leq C_{\rm Poin}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u}\cap\Omega)^{d/(d-1)}.

Proof.

Since the proof of the result in the general case
k>1
follows immediately from the case
k=1
, we may assume
u
to be scalar-valued. Let
r\geq 0
and
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega)
be as in the statement.
For simplicity of notation, throughout the proof we write
\tau^{\prime}
and
\tau^{\prime\prime}
in place of
\tau^{\prime}(u,\Omega,r)
and
\tau^{\prime\prime}(u,\Omega,r)
.
Without loss of generality, we also assume that
m=0
, which by (
C.5
) implies
\tau^{\prime}\leq 0
and
\tau^{\prime\prime}\geq 0
.

Inequality (
C.8
) is a simple consequence of the fact that

\{\overline{u}_{r}\neq u\}=\{u<\tau^{\prime}\}\cup\{u>\tau^{\prime\prime}\}

and the definition (
C.4
) of
\tau^{\prime}
and
\tau^{\prime\prime}
. We now come to the proof of (
C.6
) and (
C.7
).
We begin by observing that, thanks to Remark
A.4
,
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{u}_{r}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
belongs to
{\rm BV}(\Omega)
, and by the same remark we have

(C.9)

|D\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{u}_{r}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|(\Omega)\leq(r\vee 1)\mathcal{V}(u,\Omega\setminus J^{r}_{u})+(\tau^{\prime\prime}-\tau^{\prime})\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u}\cap\Omega).

As
\tau^{\prime}\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{u}_{r}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\tau^{\prime\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{u}_{r}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=u
on
\{\tau^{\prime}\leq u\leq\tau^{\prime\prime}\}
, by the Coarea Formula we get

(C.10)

|D\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{u}_{r}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|(\Omega)=\int_{\tau^{\prime}}^{\tau^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}\{u\geq t\}\cap\Omega)\,{\rm d}t.

By Proposition
C.1
applied with
\rho=1
and
x=0
, we can estimate from below the right-hand side of the previous equality by

(C.11)

\int_{\tau^{\prime}}^{\tau^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial^{*}\{u\geq t\}\cap\Omega)\,{\rm d}t\geq\frac{1}{\gamma}\Big(\int_{\tau^{\prime}}^{0}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}u<t\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\})^{(d-1)/d}\,{\rm d}t+\int^{\tau^{\prime\prime}}_{0}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}u\geq t\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\})^{(d-1)/d}\,{\rm d}t\Big).

From the definitions of
\tau^{\prime}
and
\tau^{\prime\prime}
in (
C.4
) it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{u\color[rgb]{0,0,0}<\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t\})^{(d-1)/d}\geq 2\gamma\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u}\cap\Omega)\quad\text{ for all }t\in(\tau^{\prime},0),

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{u\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\geq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t\})^{(d-1)/d}\geq 2\gamma\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u}\cap\Omega)\quad\text{ for all }t\in(0,\tau^{\prime\prime}),

which, combined with (
C.10
) and (
C.11
), yield

2(\tau^{\prime\prime}-\tau^{\prime})\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u}\cap\Omega)\leq|D\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{u}_{r}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|(\Omega).

In light of (
C.9
), this inequality also implies

\displaystyle(\tau^{\prime\prime}-\tau^{\prime})\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u}\cap\Omega)\leq(r\vee 1)\mathcal{V}(u,\Omega\setminus J^{r}_{u}),

which in turn gives (
C.6
). As we assumed
\Omega
to be connected with Lipschitz boundary, the Poincaré-type inequality (
C.7
) then follows from (
C.6
) by applying to
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{u}_{r}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
the classical Poincaré-Sobolev inequality for functions of bounded variation, see, for instance,
[
5
, Theorem 3.51]
.
∎

Remark C.4
.

When
r=0
and
u\in{\rm SBV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, the previous result reduces exactly to the Poincaré inequality of De Giorgi, Carriero, & Leaci
[
36
, Theorem 3.1]
. If
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u})=0
for some
r\geq 0
, then
u\in{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and (by convention)
\tau_{i}^{\prime}(u,\Omega,r)=-\infty
and
\tau_{i}^{\prime\prime}(u,\Omega,r)=+\infty
, so that (
C.7
) reduces to the usual Poincaré inequality for functions of bounded variation.

Remark C.5
.

Inspecting the proof of Theorem
C.3
, it is clear that the constant
C_{\rm Poin}
depends on
\Omega
only via the isoperimetric constant
\gamma=\gamma(\Omega)
. In particular, letting
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{I}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
be a family of indices and
\{\Omega_{i}\}_{i\in\mathcal{I}}
be a collection of domains as in Remark
C.2
, it is possible to choose a uniform constant
C_{\rm Poin}
for which the conclusions of Theorem
C.3
hold for every
i\in\mathcal{I}
.

Remark C.6
.

Let
\rho>0
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and consider the set
\Omega_{\rho,x}
given by (
2.1
). By Hölder’s inequality and a scaling argument it can be shown that for each
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega_{\rho,x};\mathbb{R}^{k})
satisfying the hypotheses of Theorem
C.3
we have

\|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{u}_{r}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-m\|_{L^{1}(\Omega_{\rho,x})}\leq\rho C_{\rm Poin}(r\vee 1)\mathcal{V}(u,\Omega_{\rho,x}\setminus J^{r}_{u}),

where
C_{\rm Poin}
is the constant corresponding to
\rho=1
.

As a simple consequence of the previous result, we show that, in the case that
u
satisfies zero boundary conditions, the Poincaré-type inequality (
C.7
) holds with
m=0
for the original function, at the expense of removing a set
\omega
of small Lebesgue measure.

Corollary C.7
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a bounded Lipschitz domain. Then there exists a constant
\bar{C}_{\rm Poin}=\bar{C}_{\rm Poin}(\Omega,k)>0
such that for all
r\geq 0
and
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, with
u=0
on
\partial\Omega
, there is a Borel set
\omega\subset\Omega
, with

\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega)\leq\bar{C}_{\rm Poin}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u})^{d/(d-1)},

such that

(C.12)

\|u\|_{L^{d/(d-1)}(\Omega\setminus\omega)}\leq\bar{C}_{\rm Poin}(r\vee 1)\mathcal{V}(u,\Omega\setminus J^{r}_{u}).

Proof.

Let
r\geq 0
, let
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, and let
\Omega^{\prime}
be a bounded Lipschitz domain with
\Omega\subset\subset\Omega^{\prime}
. Since
u=0
on
\partial\Omega
, we can extend (without relabelling)
u
to a function
{u}\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega^{\prime};\mathbb{R}^{k})
by setting
{u}=0
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega
. Moreover, it is not restrictive to assume that

(C.13)

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u})^{d/(d-1)}\leq\frac{\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega)}{2C_{\rm Poin}},

where
C_{\rm Poin}=C_{\rm Poin}(\Omega^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},k\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
is the positive constant of Theorem
C.3
. Indeed, if (
C.13
) is not satisfied, it is enough to set
\omega=\Omega
and choose
\bar{C}_{\rm Poin}
large enough. By possibly choosing a smaller
\Omega^{\prime}
, we may also assume that

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u})^{d/(d-1)}\leq\frac{\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega)}{2C_{\rm Poin}}\leq\frac{1}{(2\gamma)^{d/(d-1)}}\frac{\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega^{\prime})}{2}.

In view of this inequality,
{u}
satisfies (
C.3
), so that we can apply Theorem
C.3
to
{u}
on
\Omega^{\prime}
. We obtain a vector
m\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and a Borel set
\omega\coloneq\{{u}\neq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{{u}}_{r}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\}\subset\Omega^{\prime}
such that

(C.14)

\|{u}-m\|_{L^{d/(d-1)}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\omega)}\leq C_{\rm Poin}(r\vee 1)\mathcal{V}({u},\Omega\setminus J^{r}_{u})

and

(C.15)

\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega)\leq C_{\rm Poin}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{{u}})^{d/(d-1)}\leq\frac{\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega)}{2},

where in the second inequality we have used (
C.13
). Next, we note that, since
{u}=0
on
\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega
, from (
C.14
) it follows that

|m|\leq\frac{C_{\rm Poin}}{\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus(\Omega\cup\omega))}(r\vee 1)\mathcal{V}(u,\Omega\setminus J^{r}_{u}).

Clearly,
\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus(\Omega\cup\omega))\geq\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega)-\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega)
, so that by (
C.15
) this implies that
\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus(\Omega\cup\omega))\geq\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega)/2
. Hence,

|m|\leq\frac{2C_{\rm Poin}}{\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega^{\prime}\setminus\Omega)}(r\vee 1)\mathcal{V}(u,\Omega\setminus J^{r}_{u}).

This shows that (
C.12
) holds for a suitable choice of
\bar{C}_{\rm Poin}
.
∎

Remark C.8
.

Let
\rho>0
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and consider the set
\Omega_{\rho,x}
given by (
2.1
). By Hölder’s inequality and a scaling argument, it can be shown that for each
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega_{\rho,x};\mathbb{R}^{k})
, with
u=0
on
\partial\Omega_{\rho,x}
, we can find
\omega\subset\Omega_{\rho,x}
such that

\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega)\leq\bar{C}_{\rm Poin}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u}\cap\Omega_{\rho,x})^{d/(d-1)}\quad\text{ and }\quad\|u\|_{L^{1}(\Omega_{\rho,x}\setminus\omega)}\leq\rho\bar{C}_{\rm Poin}(r\vee 1)\mathcal{V}(u,\Omega_{\rho,x}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus J^{r}_{u}),

where the constant
\bar{C}_{\rm Poin}
is the one obtained for
\rho=1
.

We conclude the section by presenting a last consequence of the Poincaré-type inequality in Theorem
C.3
, namely a compactness result for sequences
\{u_{n}\}_{n}
with vanishing set of large jumps. Here and in the rest of the work, given a function
u\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
, we let
{\rm med}(u,\Omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
be the vector whose
i
-th component is given by
(u_{i})_{\star}(\mathcal{L}^{d}(\Omega)/2,\Omega)
, which we recall is a median of
u
on
\Omega
.

Corollary C.9
.

Let
r\geq 0
and let
\{u_{n}\}_{n}\subset{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
be a sequence such that

(C.16)

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{V}(u_{n},\Omega\setminus J^{r}_{u_{n}})<+\infty\quad\text{ and }\quad\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J^{r}_{u_{n}}\cap\Omega)=0.

Let
\overline{u}_{n}
be the function given by
\big((u_{n})\lor\tau^{\prime}(u_{n},\Omega,r)\big)\land\tau^{\prime\prime}(u_{n},\Omega,r)
. Then the sequence
\{\overline{u}_{n}-{\rm med}(u_{n},\Omega)\}_{n}
is uniformly bounded in
{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and there exist a subsequence, not relabelled, and a function
u\in{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
such that

(C.17)

\displaystyle\{\overline{u}_{n}-{\rm med}(u_{n},\Omega)\}_{n}\text{ converges to }u\text{ strongly in }L^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})\text{ as }n\to+\infty,

(C.18)

\displaystyle\{u_{n}-{\rm med}(u_{n},\Omega)\}_{n}\text{ converges to }u\text{ in }L^{0}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})\text{ as }n\to+\infty.

Proof.

Thanks to the equality in (
C.16
), we may invoke Theorem
C.3
to find that, for
n
large enough, the function
\overline{u}_{n}
belongs to
{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and satisfies

\displaystyle|D\overline{u}_{n}|(\Omega)\leq 2(r\vee 1)\mathcal{V}(u_{n},\Omega\setminus J^{r}_{u_{n}}),

\displaystyle\|\overline{u}_{n}-{\rm med}(u_{n},\Omega)\|_{L^{d/(d-1)}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\rm Poin}(r\vee 1)\mathcal{V}(u_{n},\Omega\setminus J^{r}_{u_{n}}).

In light of the inequality in (
C.16
), we deduce that
\{\overline{u}_{n}-{\rm med}(u_{n},\Omega)\}_{n}
is uniformly bounded in
{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
. Hence, there exist a subsequence, not relabelled, and a function
u\in{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
such that (
C.17
) holds. By (
C.8
) applied to
u_{n}
and (
C.16
)–(
C.17
) we finally get (
C.18
).
∎

Appendix D
Blow-ups of
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
-functions

In this section, we analyse properties of blow-ups of functions in
{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
around a point
x\in\Omega
. The key tool for this analysis will be the Poincaré-type inequality introduced in the previous section.
We carry out this study in two distinct cases: first when
x
is a point of approximate differentiability of
u
, and then when
x
is a jump point of
u
. In both cases, we will show that it is always possible to find modifications of the function
u
which are close in measure to
u
, but that behave better in the blow-up limit. The tools we develop here are crucial in the proof of Propositions
5.6
and
5.7
.

We begin by treating the case of points of approximate differentiability. The proof of the result borrows ideas from
[
17
, Lemma 2]
.

Lemma D.1

(Blow-up at points of approximate differentiability)
.

Let
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
, and for
x\in\Omega
let

u_{\rho}
and
\overline{u}_{\rho}
be the functions defined by
(
recall the notation in Theorem
C.3
for
r=0
)

u_{\rho}(y)\coloneq({u}(y)-\widetilde{u}(x))/\rho\quad\text{ and }\quad\overline{u}_{\rho}(y)\coloneq\big(u_{\rho}(y)\lor\tau^{\prime}(u_{\rho},Q(x,\rho)\big)\land\tau^{\prime\prime}(u_{\rho},Q(x,\rho))\ \ \ \text{for }y\in Q(x,\rho).

Then for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e.
x\in\Omega
we have

(D.1)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d+1}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{\overline{u}_{\rho}\neq u_{\rho}\})=0,

(D.2)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q(x,\rho)}\Big|\overline{u}_{\rho}(y)-\nabla u(x)\frac{(y-x)}{\rho}\Big|\,{\rm d}y=0,

(D.3)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q(x,\rho)}|\rho\nabla\overline{u}_{\rho}(y)-\nabla u(x)|\,{\rm d}y=0,

(D.4)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{\overline{u}_{\rho}}\cap Q(x,\rho))=0\quad\text{ and }\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}|D^{c}\overline{u}_{\rho}|(Q(x,\rho))=0.

Proof.

We give the proof only in the scalar valued case
k=1
, as the general case
k>1
is an immediate consequence of the former. We note that for
\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e.
x\in\Omega
we have

(D.5)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q(x,\rho)}|\nabla u(y)-\nabla u(x)|\,{\rm d}y=0,

(D.6)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u}\cap Q(x,\rho))=0\quad\text{ and }\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}|D^{c}u|(Q(x,\rho))=0,

(D.7)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}\Big(\Big\{y\in Q(x,\rho)\colon\,\frac{|u(y)-\widetilde{u}(x)-\nabla u(x)(y-x)|}{|y-x|}>\lambda\Big\}\Big)=0\text{ for all }\lambda>0.

It suffices to prove the statement for such
x\in\Omega
satisfying (
D.5
)–(
D.7
). We begin by observing that (
D.7
) implies

(D.8)

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}\Big(\Big\{y\in Q(x,\rho)\colon\,\frac{|u(y)-\widetilde{u}(x)-\nabla u(x)\cdot(y-x)|}{\rho}>\lambda\Big\}\Big)=0\text{ for all }\lambda>0,

that
u_{\rho}\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(Q(x,\rho))
and, by Remark
A.4
, that
\overline{u}_{\rho}
belongs to
{\rm BV}(Q(x,\rho))\cap L^{\infty}(Q(x,\rho))
. By the same remark it also follows that
J_{\overline{u}_{\rho}}\subset J_{u}
up to an
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-negligible set and that
|D^{c}\overline{u}_{\rho}|(Q(x,\rho))\leq\rho^{-1}|D^{c}u|(Q(x,\rho))
. Thus, by (
D.6
) we get

\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{\overline{u}_{\rho}}\cap Q(x,\rho))

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{{u}}\cap Q(x,\rho))=0,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}|D^{c}\overline{u}_{\rho}|(Q(x,\rho))

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}|D^{c}{u}|(Q(x,\rho))=0.

This proves (
D.4
),
so that we are left with proving (
D.1
)–(
D.3
).

To this end, we
consider suitable rescalings of
u_{\rho}
. More precisely, for
z\in Q
we define
v_{\rho}(z)\coloneq u_{\rho}(x+\rho z)
. We have
\{v_{\rho}\}_{\rho}\subset{\rm GBV}_{\star}(Q)
and by a change of variables it holds

\mathcal{V}(v_{\rho},Q\setminus J_{v_{\rho}})\leq\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\Big(\int_{Q(x,\rho)}|\nabla{u}|\,{\rm d}x+|D^{c}{u}|\big(Q(x,\rho)\big)\Big).

Since
x+\rho J_{v_{\rho}}=J_{u_{\rho}}=J_{u}\cap Q(x,\rho)
, up to
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-negligible sets, by a change of variables we get

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(Q\cap J_{v_{\rho}})=\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u}\cap Q(x,\rho)).

Combining the two previous displayed formulas with (
D.5
) and (
D.6
), we get

(D.9)

\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\mathcal{V}(v_{\rho},Q\setminus J_{v_{\rho}})<+\infty\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{v_{\rho}}\cap Q)=0.

Therefore, for
\rho>0
small enough we may apply Theorem
C.3
with
r=0
, and by (
C.8
) we get that the function defined by
\overline{v}_{\rho}(z)\coloneq(v_{\rho}(z)\lor\tau^{\prime}(v_{\rho},Q))\land\tau^{\prime\prime}(v_{\rho},Q)
for
z\in Q
satisfies

(D.10)

\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{\overline{v}_{\rho}\neq v_{\rho}\})\leq C_{\rm Poin}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{v_{\rho}}\cap Q)^{d/(d-1)}.

For every
0<s\leq\rho^{d}
we have the equality
(u_{\rho})_{\star}(s,Q(x,\rho))=(v_{\rho})_{\star}(s/\rho^{d},Q)
, so that (recall (
C
))

\displaystyle\tau^{\prime}(v_{\rho},Q)=\tau^{\prime}(u_{\rho},Q(x,\rho))\quad\text{ and }\quad\tau^{\prime\prime}(v_{\rho},Q)=\tau^{\prime\prime}(u_{\rho},Q(x,\rho)),

\displaystyle\text{med}(v_{\rho},Q)=\text{med}(u_{\rho},Q(x,\rho)),

and, as consequence,
\overline{v}_{\rho}(z)=\overline{u}_{\rho}(x+\rho z)
. A change of variables and (
D.10
) then show that

\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{\overline{u}_{\rho}\neq u_{\rho}\})\leq C_{\rm Poin}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Big(\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u_{\rho}}\cap Q(x,\rho))\Big)^{d/(d-1)}.

Therefore,

\frac{1}{\rho^{d^{2}/(d-1)}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{\overline{u}_{\rho}\neq u_{\rho}\})\leq C_{\rm Poin}\Big(\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(J_{u_{\rho}}\cap Q(x,\rho)\big)\Big)^{\frac{d}{d-1}}=C_{\rm Poin}\Big(\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(J_{u}\cap Q(x,\rho)\big)\Big)^{\frac{d}{d-1}}.

Recalling the first equality in (
D.6
), and observing that
d^{2}/(d-1)\geq(d+1)
, we have just proved

(D.11)

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d+1}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{\overline{u}_{\rho}\neq u_{\rho}\})=0,

which is exactly (
D.1
). To get (
D.3
), we simply observe that

\nabla\overline{u}_{\rho}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(y)=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\rho^{-1}\nabla u(y)&\text{ if $y\in\{\overline{u}_{\rho}=u_{\rho}\}$},\\
0&\text{ if $y\in Q(x,\rho)\cap\{\overline{u}_{\rho}\neq u_{\rho}\}$},\end{cases}

so that, in view of (
D.5
) and (
D.11
), we infer

\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}

\displaystyle\int_{Q(x,\rho)}|\rho\nabla\overline{u}_{\rho}(y)-\nabla u(x)|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\Big(\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q(x,\rho)}|\nabla u(y)-\nabla u(x)|\,{\rm d}y+\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{\overline{u}_{\rho}\neq u_{\rho}\})|\nabla u(x)|\Big)=0.

We conclude by proving (
D.2
).
To this end, we observe that, thanks to (
D.9
), we may apply Corollary
C.9
with
r=0
to obtain a sequence
\{\rho_{n}\}_{n}
, with
\rho_{n}\to 0
as
n\to+\infty
, and a function
v_{0}\in{\rm BV}(Q)
such that

(D.12)

\displaystyle v_{\rho_{n}}-{\rm med}(v_{\rho_{n}},Q)\ \text{ converges to }v_{0}\text{ in }L^{0}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{k})\text{ as }n\to+\infty,

(D.13)

\displaystyle\overline{v}_{\rho_{n}}-{\rm med}(v_{\rho_{n}},Q)\ \text{ converges to }v_{0}\text{ strongly in }L^{1}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{k})\text{ as }n\to+\infty.

The same arguments portrayed in
[
17
, Lemma 2, Page 196]
show that, by using (
D.8
),

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\text{med}(v_{\rho_{n}},Q)=0.

In view of (
D.8
), (
D.12
), and (
D.13
), this implies that
v_{0}(z)=\nabla u(x)z
for all
z\in Q
, so that
v_{0}
does not depend on the chosen subsequence
\{\rho_{n}\}_{n}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\{\overline{v}_{\rho}\}_{\rho}\text{ converges to }\nabla u(x)z\text{ strongly in }L^{1}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{k})\text{ as }\rho\to 0^{+}.

It is now enough to observe that, by another change of variables, this can be rewritten as

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q(x,\rho)}\Big|\overline{u}_{\rho}(y)-\nabla u(x)\frac{(y-x)}{\rho}\Big|\,{\rm d}y=0,

which is exactly (
D.2
). This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

In the following, we analyse the behaviour of blow-ups of functions in
{\rm GBV_{\star}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
around jump points, drawing ideas from
[
28
, Lemma 6.1]
and
[
26
, Lemma 3.4]
. The strategy is similar to the one employed in Lemma
D.1
, based on the following observation: since the jump set
J_{u}
is
(d-1)
-rectifiable, around a jump point
x
and for
\rho>0
small enough,
J_{u}\cap Q(x,\rho)
is mostly contained in a smooth hypersurface which subdivides the cube into two connected halves. As the restrictions of the function
u
to each of the two halves have small jump sets, it is then possible to perform the modifications proposed in Lemma
D.1
in each half. Recall (
2.1
) and (
3.4
).

Lemma D.2

(Blow-up at jump points)
.

Let
u\in{\rm GBV}_{\star}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
. Then, for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
x\in J_{u}
, letting
\nu\coloneq\nu_{u}(x)
and
v_{x}\coloneq v_{x,u^{+},u^{-},\nu}
, there exists
u_{\rho}\in{\rm BV}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho);\mathbb{R}^{k})
such that

(D.14)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{u_{\rho}\neq u\}\cap Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))=0,

(D.15)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}|u_{\rho}-{v}_{x}|\,{\rm d}y=0,

(D.16)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\,\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u_{\rho}}\cap E_{\rho,x})\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\Pi_{x}^{\nu}\cap E)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\text{for all Borel sets }E\subset\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{\nu}(x,1),\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(D.17)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\ \frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\int_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}|\nabla u_{\rho}|\,\mathrm{d}y=0\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\ \frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}|D^{c}u_{\rho}|(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))=0.

Note that the function
u_{\rho}
is different from the one constructed in Lemma
D.1
although we use the same notation.

Proof.

Clearly, it is enough to prove the result in the scalar case
k=1
.

We observe that for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e.
x\in J_{u}
the following holds: first, we have

(D.18)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(J_{u}\cap Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))=1,

(D.19)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\int_{Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)}|\nabla u|\,{\rm d}y=0\quad\text{ and }\quad\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}|D^{c}u|(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))=0,

(D.20)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{y\in Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\,|u(y)-v_{x}(y)|>\lambda\})=0\quad\text{for all $\lambda>0$}.

Indeed, almost every jump point is a point of density
1
for
J_{u}
, i.e., (
D.18
) holds. Condition (
D.19
) is satisfied for
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-a.e. point of
\Omega
since the measures
\nabla u\mathcal{L}^{d}
and
D^{c}u
are diffuse (see, for instance,
[
5
, Theorem 2.56]
). Condition (
D.20
) is a rephrasing of the definition of jump points given in (
A.4
).

Moreover, there exist
\overline{\rho}>0
and a
(d-1)
-dimensional surface
\Gamma\subset Q_{\nu}(x,\bar{\rho})
of class
C^{1}
with
x\in\Gamma
such that
\Gamma
is the graph of a function
h
defined on the hyperplane
\Pi^{\nu}_{x}=\{(y-x)\cdot\nu=0\}
, the hyperplane
\Pi^{\nu}_{x}
is tangent to
\Gamma
in
x
, for all
0<\rho<\overline{\rho}
the surface
\Gamma\cap Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)
separates
Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)
into two open connected components
Q^{\Gamma,\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)
, and

(D.21)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big((J_{u}\triangle\Gamma)\cap Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\big)=0,

(D.22)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d-1}}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\Gamma\cap E_{\rho,x})=\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\Pi^{\nu}_{x}\cap E)\quad\text{for all Borel sets }E\subset Q_{\nu}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0},1).

This property can be justified by arguing exactly as in the beginning of the proof of
[
23
, Theorem 2]
(see also the comments in
[
24
, Section 3]
), noting that the arguments rely only on the rectifiability of the set
J_{u}
.

We start with a first observation: the Lipschitz constant of
h\,\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}_{\Pi^{\nu}_{x}\cap Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
converges to zero as
\rho\to 0^{+}
. This implies that

(D.23)

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}\big(Q_{\nu}^{\Gamma,\pm}(x,\rho)\triangle Q_{\nu}^{\pm}(x,\rho)\big)=0,

where we have set
Q_{\nu}^{\pm}(x,\rho)\coloneq\{y\in Q_{\nu}(x,\rho)\colon\pm(y-x)\cdot\nu>0\}
.

In view of (
D.21
), the restriction of
u
to
Q^{\Gamma,\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)
satisfies the hypothesis of Theorem
C.3
with
\Omega
replaced by
Q^{\Gamma,\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)
. As
\{\frac{1}{\rho}(\Gamma\cap Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))\}_{\rho}
are graphs of functions whose Lipschitz constant converges to zero as
\rho\to 0^{+}
, the constant in the Poincaré-type inequality can be chosen independently of
\rho
, see Remarks
C.5
–
C.6
. Hence, letting
\overline{u}^{\pm}_{\rho}\in{\rm BV}(Q^{\Gamma,\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho))
be the functions defined by

\overline{u}_{\rho}^{\pm}\coloneq\big(u\lor\tau^{\prime}(u,Q_{\nu}^{\Gamma,\pm}(x,\rho)\big)\land\tau^{\prime\prime}(u,Q_{\nu}^{\Gamma,\pm}(x,\rho))

and letting
m^{\pm}_{\rho}\coloneq{\rm med}(u,Q_{\nu}^{\Gamma,\pm}(x,\rho))
, recalling also Remark
C.6
,
we have that

(D.24)

\displaystyle|D\overline{u}^{\pm}_{\rho}|(Q_{\nu}^{\Gamma,\pm})\leq 2\mathcal{V}\big(u,Q^{\Gamma,\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus J_{u}\big),

(D.25)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\nu}^{\Gamma,\pm}(x,\rho)}|\overline{u}^{\pm}_{\rho}-m^{\pm}_{\rho}|\,{\rm d}y\leq\rho C_{\rm Poin}\mathcal{V}\big(u,Q^{\Gamma,\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus J_{u}\big),

(D.26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{d}(\{\overline{u}^{\pm}_{\rho}\neq u\})\leq C_{\rm Poin}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\big(J_{u}\cap Q^{\Gamma,\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\big)^{d/(d-1)},

(D.27)

\displaystyle J_{\overline{u}^{\pm}_{\rho}}\subset J_{u}\cap Q^{\Gamma,\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\quad\text{up to an $\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-negligible set}

for a constant
C_{\rm Poin}>0
independent of
\rho
. Note that from (
D.21
) and (
D.26
) it follows that

(D.28)

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}\big(\{\overline{u}^{\pm}_{\rho}\neq u\}\cap Q^{\Gamma,\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\big)=0.

We now introduce the modifications
u_{\rho}\in{\rm BV}(Q_{\nu}(x,\rho))
by setting

u_{\rho}(y)\coloneq\begin{cases}\overline{u}^{+}_{\rho}&\text{ if $y\in Q^{\Gamma,+}_{\nu}(x,\rho)$},\\
\overline{u}^{-}_{\rho}&\text{ if $y\in Q^{\Gamma,-}_{\nu}(x,\rho)$,}\end{cases}

and show that
\{u_{\rho}\}_{\rho}
satisfy properties (
D.14
)–(
D.17
). Property (
D.14
) has already been addressed in (
D.28
). To prove (
D.16
), we first observe that by (
D.27
) we have

J_{u_{\rho}}\subset\Gamma\cup J_{u}\quad\text{ up to an $\mathcal{H}^{d-1}$-negligible set},

which, together with (
D.21
) and (
D.22
), shows (
D.16
).
The equalities in (
D.17
) are a consequence of (
D.19
) and of (
D.24
).

It remains to prove (
D.15
). We note that this would follow from

(D.29)

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}{m^{\pm}_{\rho}}=u^{\pm}(x).

Indeed, (
D.19
) and (
D.25
) give that

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q^{\Gamma,\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}|u_{\rho}-m^{\pm}_{\rho}|\,{\rm d}y=0.

Recalling (
D.23
), by the triangle inequality this implies

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int_{Q^{\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)}|{u}_{\rho}-m^{\pm}_{\rho}|\,{\rm d}y=0.

Then, (
D.29
) and the triangle inequality yield, in turn, (
D.15
).

We now show (
D.29
). In view of (
D.20
), by a diagonal argument we can find a sequence
\{\lambda_{\rho}\}_{\rho}\subset(0,+\infty)
with
\lambda_{\rho}\to 0
as
\rho\to 0^{+}
such that the sets

{\omega}^{\pm}_{\rho}\coloneq\{y\in Q^{\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\colon\,|u(y)-u^{\pm}(x)|>\lambda_{\rho}\}

satisfy

(D.30)

\lim_{\rho\to 0^{+}}\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}(\omega^{\pm}_{\rho})=0.

Recalling (
D.25
), we have that

(D.31)

\int_{Q^{\Gamma,\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus\{\overline{u}^{\pm}_{\rho}\neq u\}}|u(y)-m^{\pm}_{\rho}|\,{\rm d}y\leq\rho C_{\rm Poin}\mathcal{V}\big(u,Q^{\Gamma,\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus J_{u}\big).

By the definition of
{\omega}^{\pm}_{\rho}
, using (
D.31
) and the triangle inequality, we then get

(D.32)

|u^{\pm}(x)-m^{\pm}_{\rho}|\mathcal{L}^{d}\big((Q^{\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\cap Q_{\nu}^{\Gamma,\pm}(x,\rho))\setminus(\{\overline{u}^{\pm}_{\rho}\neq u\}\cup\omega^{\pm}_{\rho})\big)\leq\rho C_{\rm Poin}\mathcal{V}\big(u,Q^{\Gamma,\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus J_{u}\big)+\rho^{d}\lambda_{\rho}.

From (
D.23
), (
D.28
), and (
D.30
) we deduce the existence of
{\rho}_{0}>0
such that

\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\mathcal{L}^{d}\big((Q^{\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\cap Q_{\nu}^{\Gamma,\pm}(x,\rho))\setminus(\{\overline{u}^{\pm}_{\rho}\neq u\}\cup\omega^{\pm}_{\rho})\big)\geq\frac{1}{4}

for every
\rho\in(0,{\rho}_{0})
. Then from inequality (
D.32
) we infer

|u^{\pm}(x)-m^{\pm}_{\rho}|\leq\frac{4C_{\rm Poin}}{\rho^{d-1}}\mathcal{V}\big(u,Q^{\Gamma,\pm}_{\nu}(x,\rho)\setminus J_{u}\big)+4\lambda_{\rho}

for all
\rho\in(0,{\rho}_{0})
. Letting
\rho\to 0^{+}
and resorting to (
D.19
) and to the fact that
\lambda_{\rho}\to 0
as
\rho\to 0^{+}
, we obtain (
D.29
). This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Appendix E
A lower semicontinuity lemma

In this section, we present a well-known property for lower semicontinuous integral functionals on
{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
with linear growth, employed in the proof of Theorem
3.16
. In the following, we denote by
\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}_{1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}
the subspace of rank-one matrices.

Lemma E.1
.

Let
f\colon\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}\to[0,+\infty)
,
h\colon\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}_{1}\to[0,+\infty)
, and
g\colon\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to[0,+\infty)
be Borel integrands such that

\displaystyle 0\leq f(A)\leq C(|A|+1)\quad\text{ for all $A\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}$,}

\displaystyle h\text{ is positively \color[rgb]{0,0,0}$1$-homogeneous},

\displaystyle g(\zeta,\nu)=g(-\zeta,-\nu)\quad\text{ and }\quad 0\leq g(\zeta,\nu)\leq C|\zeta|\quad\text{for all $\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{k}$ and $\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}$}

for some
C>0
. Consider the functional
F\colon{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})\times\mathcal{O}(\Omega)\to[0,+\infty)
given by

F(u,O)\coloneq\int_{O}f(\nabla u)\,{\rm d}x+\int_{O}h\Big(\frac{{\rm d}D^{c}u}{{\rm d}|D^{c}u|}\Big)\,{\rm d}|D^{c}u|+\int_{J_{u}\cap O}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[u],\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nu_{u})\,{\rm d}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}

for all
u\in{\rm BV}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
and all
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
, and assume that
F(\cdot,O)
is
L^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{k})
-lower semicontinuous for all
O\in\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
. Then

h(A)=f^{\infty}(A)\quad\text{for all $\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}^{k\times d}_{1}$,}

where
f^{\infty}(A)\coloneq\limsup_{t\to+\infty}\frac{f(tA)}{t}
.

Proof.

Let us fix
a\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and set
A\coloneq a\otimes\nu
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
Q_{\nu}\subset\Omega
, where we recall that
Q_{\nu}
is a cube centred at 0 with two faces orthogonal to
\nu
and sides of unit length.

We first prove that
h(A)\leq f^{\infty}(A).
To this end, we let
\psi\colon[-1/2,1/2]\to[-1/2,1/2]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(be a translation of) Cantor’s staircase function and we consider the function defined for
x\in Q_{\nu}
by
u(x)\coloneq a\psi(x\cdot\nu)
. Clearly,
u\in{\rm BV}(Q_{\nu};\mathbb{R}^{k})
,
|Du|(Q_{\nu})=|D^{c}u|(Q_{\nu})=|A|
, and

\frac{{\rm d}D^{c}u}{{\rm d}|D^{c}u|}=\frac{A}{|A|}\quad\text{$|D^{c}u|$-a.e.\ on $Q_{\nu}$}.

In particular,
F(u,Q_{\nu})=f(0)+h(A)
. Then, we let
\{\psi_{n}\}_{n}
be the sequence of piecewise affine functions considered in the construction of
\psi
, which converges to
\psi
uniformly and satisfies

\psi^{\prime}_{n}=t_{n}\chi_{E_{n}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{L}^{1}(E_{n})=t_{n}^{-1}

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, where
t_{n}\coloneq(3/2)^{n}
and
E_{n}
is the union of the
2^{n}
intervals remaining in the
n
-th step of the construction of the Cantor set. We set
u_{n}(x)\coloneq a\psi_{n}(x\cdot\nu)
. We note that
\{u_{n}\}_{n}
converges to
u
strongly in
L^{1}(Q_{\nu};\mathbb{R}^{k})
and that
\nabla u_{n}(x)=t_{n}\chi_{E_{n}}(x\cdot\nu)A.
Thus,

F(u_{n},Q_{\nu})=\int_{Q_{\nu}}f(\nabla u_{n})\,{\rm d}x=(1-t_{n}^{-1})f(0)+\frac{f(t_{n}A)}{t_{n}}.

By the
L^{1}
-lower semicontinuity of
F
, this implies

f(0)+h(A)=F(u,Q_{\nu})\leq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}F(u_{n},Q_{\nu})\leq\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f(0)+f^{\infty}(A),

which proves
h(A)\leq f^{\infty}(A)
.

To prove the converse inequality, we let
t>0
and consider the affine map defined by
v(x)=ta(x\cdot\nu)
for
x\in Q_{\nu}
. Then, we set

w_{n}(s)\coloneq\frac{1}{n}\big(\lfloor ns\rfloor+\psi(ns-\lfloor ns\rfloor)\big)\quad\text{ for $s\in[-1/2,1/2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]$},

where
\lfloor\cdot\rfloor
denotes the floor function.
Clearly,
\{w{}_{n}\}_{n}
converges uniformly to the identity on
[-1/2,1/2\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]
, and
|Dw_{n}|(-1/2,1/2\color[rgb]{0,0,0})=|D^{c}w_{n}|(-1/2,1/2\color[rgb]{0,0,0})=1
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, so that the functions
v_{n}(x)\coloneq taw_{n}(x\cdot\nu)
converge to
v
strongly in
L^{1}(Q_{\nu};\mathbb{R}^{k})
,
|Dv_{n}|(Q_{\nu})=|D^{c}v_{n}|(Q_{\nu})=t|a|\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=t|A|
, and

\frac{{\rm d}D^{c}v_{n}}{{\rm d}|D^{c}v_{n}|}=\frac{A}{|A|}\quad\text{$|D^{c}v_{n}|$-a.e.\ in $Q_{\nu}$}.

Thus,
F(v_{n},Q_{\nu})=f(0)+th(A)
, which by lower semicontinuity gives

f(tA)=F(v,Q_{\nu})\leq\liminf_{n\to+\infty}F(v_{n},Q_{\nu})=f(0)+th(A).

Dividing by
t
and letting
t\to+\infty
we obtain
f^{\infty}(A)\leq h(A)
, concluding the proof.
∎
